=== GroupTrack_BL02_channel_num_bug (1).docx ===
GroupTrack.org
BL-02: channel_num = 0 in Master Config Capture
March 24, 2026  |  Priority: HIGH — affects all radio config deploys

Symptom
After Capture Master Config, the saved master_config.json shows:
"loraChannelNum": 0
The radio is physically set to 903 MHz which corresponds to channel_num = 7 in the Meshtastic US915 LoRa band plan. The capture is not reading this value correctly.

Root Cause Analysis
Why channel_num reads as 0
Meshtastic uses Protocol Buffers (proto3) for all config serialization. Proto3 does not serialize fields that contain their default value. The default value for an integer field in proto3 is 0. When the Meshtastic app reads localConfig from localConfigFlow (the datastore), channel_num is present in the radio but proto3 strips it from the serialized form if its encoded value happens to equal 0 — OR the datastore simply never captures it from the radio response.
The capture code path

What Must Be Investigated

Files to Investigate

Proposed Fix Options

Related Context

Test to Confirm Fix
After implementing fix:

=== GroupTrack_Complete_Roadmap.docx ===
GroupTrack
Complete Product Roadmap
March 30, 2026  |  Off-Grid Awareness Platform  |  Confidential
1. Product Vision
GroupTrack is an off-grid awareness platform for any group activity in areas without cell service — off-road vehicles, hikers, mountain bikers, overlanders, search and rescue, hunting parties. The platform provides real-time group position awareness over LoRa mesh radio, organized ride management, community track sharing, and radio configuration — all without requiring cell or internet connectivity during the activity itself.
The platform is built on three principles: radio operations never require internet, platform features are web-enabled and repeatable across Android and web, and every release builds on a proven stable foundation before the next begins.

2. Navigation Architecture — Final Model
GroupTrack functions are accessed via two dedicated buttons attached to the GroupTrack entry point in the Meshtastic navigation bar. The convoy map runs permanently as a background process — it is never a navigation destination.

=== GroupTrack_ConvoyTrackPickerFixPlan_v1.docx ===
GroupTrack V2.4
Convoy Track Picker Fix Plan
April 23, 2026 | Branch: feature/convoy-event-ride
1. Problem Summary
The convoy map track picker panel was added to ConvoyScreen.kt on April 22. It introduced two issues:
Issue A — ANR (Application Not Responding): The track picker calls convoyListTracks() which runs File.listFiles() on the main thread. On Android 14, this syscall blocks waiting for the storage daemon, especially during simultaneous tile downloads. This freezes the UI and triggers Android's ANR dialog. Fix applied: wrapped scan in async coroutine. Status: FIXED in stashed code.
Issue B — Layout Conflict: The track picker panel is inserted into the convoy map's floating settings bar between the map source buttons and the expanded settings section. When expanded, it pushes or overlaps the download controls (SHOW DOWNLOADED, area draw, zoom slider). The SHOW DOWNLOADED button becomes unresponsive — either obscured by the track panel or its touch target is intercepted. Status: NOT FIXED.
2. Root Cause Analysis
2.1 Convoy Map Floating Bar Structure
The convoy map floating bar is a single scrollable Column containing multiple sections that expand/collapse:
The track picker panel (NEW) was inserted between rows 2 and 3. When both the track panel AND expanded settings are open simultaneously, the Column becomes taller than the screen. The verticalScroll modifier allows scrolling, but touch events on the SHOW DOWNLOADED button are intercepted by the track panel's LazyColumn scroll gesture.
2.2 Map Viewer vs Convoy Map — Why One Works
Map Viewer's track panel works because it has a simpler layout — no download controls, no area draw, no zoom slider. The track panel is the ONLY expandable section, so there is no conflict. The convoy map has multiple expandable sections competing for the same vertical space.
3. Fix Approach
3.1 Option A — Mutual Exclusion (Recommended)
Only one panel can be open at a time. Tapping TRACKS closes expanded settings. Tapping the chevron closes the track panel. This prevents any layout overlap.
Implementation:
1. In TRACKS button clickable: add showMapSettings = false before showConvoyTrackPicker = !showConvoyTrackPicker
2. In chevron button clickable: add showConvoyTrackPicker = false before showMapSettings = !showMapSettings
3. No layout changes needed — panels never coexist.
Effort: 2 lines of code. Zero risk.
3.2 Option B — Separate Track Panel Location
Move the track panel outside the floating settings bar entirely. Place it as a bottom sheet or a separate overlay that slides up from the bottom. This keeps the settings bar unchanged and eliminates any interaction.
Effort: More complex. Requires new composable layout, animation, and state management. Not recommended for today.
3.3 Option C — Replace Column Scroll with Fixed Heights
Set fixed maximum heights on each section so they never exceed screen bounds. Track panel maxHeight = 120dp, expanded settings maxHeight = 200dp. Both can be open but neither overflows.
Risk: Small screens may still have issues. Content gets cut off. Not clean.
4. Recommended Fix Sequence
5. Stash Resolution Status
6. Code Changes — Option A Detail
6.1 TRACKS Button — Close Settings
File: ConvoyScreen.kt
Location: TRACKS button clickable handler (around line 860)
Current code: showConvoyTrackPicker = !showConvoyTrackPicker
New code: if (!showConvoyTrackPicker) showMapSettings = false; showConvoyTrackPicker = !showConvoyTrackPicker
6.2 Chevron Button — Close Track Picker
File: ConvoyScreen.kt
Location: Chevron clickable handler (around line 815)
Current code: showMapSettings = !showMapSettings
New code: if (!showMapSettings) showConvoyTrackPicker = false; showMapSettings = !showMapSettings
6.3 Remove ALL Button
File: ConvoyScreen.kt
Location: Track picker action bar (around line 985)
Remove the entire ALL Text/clickable block. Keep FIT and CLEAR.
Reason: ALL loads every track file synchronously — reading XML, parsing coordinates, injecting into WebView — all on the main thread. Even with async scan, the ALL loader runs synchronously. On a device with 87 tracks, this is a guaranteed ANR. Users on convoy map should pick individual reference tracks, not load everything.
7. Test Plan After Fix
8. What Is Working (Last Clean Commit 9d66c9781)
9. Post-Fix Remaining Tasks

=== GroupTrack_Day25_WorkPlan.docx ===
GroupTrack
Day 25 — V2.4 Offline Maps Completion Plan
March 26, 2026  |  Branch: feature/convoy-event-ride  |  Goal: Seal V2.4


CURRENT STATE — START OF DAY 25

ALREADY COMPLETE — DO NOT REBUILD


STEP-BY-STEP WORK PLAN

STEP 1 — F3: Android Bridge + Size Dialog
Wire the onAreaSelected() JavaScript callback to Android. Calculate tile count. Show size dialog before download starts. Block if over 500MB ceiling.

Files to modify:
app/src/main/java/com/geeksville/mesh/convoy/ConvoyScreen.kt — add @JavascriptInterface, dialog UI
app/src/main/java/com/geeksville/mesh/convoy/ConvoyViewModel.kt — add DownloadState StateFlow

What to build:
Add @JavascriptInterface fun onAreaSelected(n,s,e,w: Double) to WebAppInterface class in ConvoyScreen.kt
On callback: call ConvoyTileCalculator.quickEstimate(n,s,e,w) to get DownloadEstimate
Post estimate to ViewModel via StateFlow
Show AlertDialog: 'Download Map Area? X tiles, Y MB. This may take several minutes.' DOWNLOAD / CANCEL
If estimate.withinCeiling == false: show 'Area too large — Y MB exceeds 500MB. Reduce area.' OK only

Commit message:
feat(V2.4): F3 — onAreaSelected bridge + size estimation dialog with 500MB ceiling

STEP 2 — F4: ConvoyTileDownloader
New Kotlin file. Downloads tiles from active tile source URL to local device storage. Coroutine-based, cancellable, progress callback.

New file:
app/src/main/java/com/geeksville/mesh/convoy/ConvoyTileDownloader.kt

What to build:
object ConvoyTileDownloader
fun buildTileUrl(tile: TileKey, sourceUrl: String): String — replace {z}/{y}/{x} AND {z}/{x}/{y} template vars
fun tilePath(context, sourceName, tile): File — returns context.filesDir/tiles/{sourceName}/{z}/{x}/{y}.png, creates dirs
suspend fun downloadTile(url, dest): Boolean — OkHttp GET, write bytes, return success. Retry once on fail.
suspend fun downloadTiles(context, tiles, sourceUrl, sourceName, onProgress): Result<DownloadSummary> — iterate tiles, call downloadTile, call onProgress(downloaded, total, failCount)
data class DownloadSummary(downloaded: Int, failed: Int, totalMB: Float)

Tile URL formats — both must work:
Esri SAT/TOPO: {z}/{y}/{x} — note y before x
Carto RD: {z}/{x}/{y}.png — note x before y
Google HYB: x={x}&y={y}&z={z} — query params

OkHttp dependency — already in Meshtastic. No new dependency needed.

Commit message:
feat(V2.4): F4 — ConvoyTileDownloader — coroutine tile download with retry and progress

STEP 3 — F6: Download Progress UI
Show progress during download in the gear settings panel. LinearProgressIndicator, tile count, MB remaining, CANCEL button.

Files to modify:
app/src/main/java/com/geeksville/mesh/convoy/ConvoyViewModel.kt — DownloadState StateFlow
app/src/main/java/com/geeksville/mesh/convoy/ConvoyScreen.kt — gear panel progress UI

DownloadState sealed class:
Idle — show DRAW MAP AREA button
Downloading(downloaded: Int, total: Int, failCount: Int) — show progress bar
Complete(summary: DownloadSummary) — show completion message 3 seconds
Cancelled — show cancelled message
Error(message: String) — show error with retry

Progress UI in gear panel:
Observe downloadState in ConvoyScreen.kt gear panel
When Downloading: LinearProgressIndicator(progress = downloaded/total), Text('X of Y tiles — Z MB remaining'), Button('CANCEL')
CANCEL taps cancel the coroutine job stored in ViewModel
When Complete: Text('Downloaded X tiles (Y MB). Z failed.') for 3 seconds then Idle

Commit message:
feat(V2.4): F6 — download progress UI with cancel in gear panel

STEP 4 — F5: Leaflet Local Tile Serving
Configure WebView to serve tiles from local device storage. Switch to local URL after download completes. Leaflet auto-falls back to online if local tile missing.

Files to modify:
app/src/main/assets/convoy_map.html — setTileUrl() already handles file:// — verify
app/src/main/java/com/geeksville/mesh/convoy/ConvoyScreen.kt — WebView settings, switch to local URL
app/src/main/java/com/geeksville/mesh/convoy/ConvoyConfig.kt — LOCAL_TILE_BASE constant

What to build:
WebView settings: allowFileAccessFromFileURLs = true, allowUniversalAccessFromFileURLs = true
LOCAL_TILE_BASE in ConvoyConfig.kt = 'file:///data/user/0/com.geeksville.mesh.google.debug/files/tiles/'
After download completes: build local URL = LOCAL_TILE_BASE + sourceName + '/{z}/{x}/{y}.png'
Call evaluateJavascript('setTileUrl("' + localUrl + '")', null)
Verify convoy_map.html setTileUrl() handles file:// prefix correctly

Test: turn WiFi off. Open app. Tap downloaded tile type. Map displays from cache.

feat(V2.4): F5 — Leaflet local tile serving from device filesDir

Track downloaded regions. Show list in gear panel. Allow delete. Persist via SharedPreferences.

New file:
app/src/main/java/com/geeksville/mesh/convoy/ConvoyMapRegion.kt

What to build:
data class MapRegion(sourceName, tileCount, sizeMB, downloadDate, boundsDescription)
object ConvoyRegionRegistry: save(context, region), load(context): List<MapRegion>, delete(context, sourceName) — deletes files from filesDir/tiles/{sourceName}/ and removes from SharedPreferences JSON
In gear panel: LazyColumn of regions. Each row: source name, tile count, size, date, DELETE button
Total storage used shown at bottom of list
Empty state: 'No regions downloaded — draw an area to start'

feat(V2.4): F7 — ConvoyMapRegion registry and gear panel region list

=== GroupTrack_Day26_Summary (1).docx ===
GroupTrack
Day 26 Summary + Day 27 Work Plan
March 28, 2026  |  Branch: feature/convoy-event-ride  |  Next Test: Wednesday
1. Completed Today — Day 26

2. Current Build State


3. Open Show Stopper — Offline Maps Bug
Offline map tiles do not render when OFFLINE switch is toggled. Map goes grey. Online maps work perfectly. Tiles confirmed present on device at correct path.

3.1 What We Know

3.2 Most Likely Cause
The persistent WebView (viewModel.persistentWebView) was created BEFORE allowFileAccessFromFileURLs was added. WebView settings only apply before loadUrl() is called. The persistent WebView loaded convoy_map.html once and cached it. Even after reinstall, the ViewModel retains the old WebView instance without the file access setting.

3.3 Recommended Fix — First Thing Day 27
Step 1 — Verify WebChromeClient is still missing (JS console logging):
grep -n "WebChromeClient" app/src/main/java/com/geeksville/mesh/convoy/ConvoyScreen.kt

Step 2 — Null the persistent WebView to force recreation with correct settings:
grep -n "persistentWebView" app/src/main/java/com/geeksville/mesh/convoy/ConvoyViewModel.kt
Add to ConvoyViewModel init or onCleared: persistentWebView = null on every app start so the else branch always runs fresh with allowFileAccessFromFileURLs = true.

Step 3 — Add WebChromeClient to capture JS console in logcat:
cp ~/Downloads/patch_chrome_client.py . && python3 patch_chrome_client.py

Step 4 — Build, install, toggle OFFLINE, watch logcat:
adb -s 8624SBCEDF00001789 logcat | grep -E "ConvoyJS|TILE|ConvoyOffline"

Step 5 — If still failing: NanoHTTPD local tile server on localhost:8888. Bypasses all file:// restrictions. Single Java file already in Meshtastic dependencies.

DO NOT clear app storage — deletes master.cfg, downloaded tiles, stored ride configs. Tiles are confirmed present at files/tiles/SAT/ — do not re-download until offline rendering is confirmed working.


4. Lead Cart Lock — In Progress (Not Yet Built)
4.1 Problem Statement
The current ConvoyEngine derives the lead cart dynamically every tick using minByOrNull { it.convoyPosition }. On switchbacks and tight turns at slow speed, heading and position change rapidly, causing the algorithm to oscillate between multiple carts as lead. Every lead change draws a new track line from the new lead position — resulting in spaghetti lines on tight trail sections.

This is NOT a GPS sampling or packet delay problem. It is the algorithm re-deriving lead mid-ride when position relationships temporarily change on tight corners.

4.2 Solution Design — Agreed

KEY INSIGHT: Each device independently derives and locks lead. No mesh packet needed. All devices see same mesh data — after 60 seconds of movement they converge on same lead independently.

4.3 Files to Modify

4.4 ConvoyEngine.kt — Current Lead Derivation (line 92)
val leadNode = active.minByOrNull { it.convoyPosition }
Change to:
val leadNode = if (explicitLeadId != null) active.firstOrNull { it.nodeId == explicitLeadId } ?: active.minByOrNull { it.convoyPosition }
                else active.minByOrNull { it.convoyPosition }

4.5 Where to Resume in New Conversation
1. Read this document for context
2. Fix offline maps first (Section 3) — show stopper

val leadNode = if (explicitLeadId != null) active.firstOrNull { it.nodeId == explicitLeadId } ?: active.minByOrNull { it.convoyPosition }
                else active.minByOrNull { it.convoyPosition }

4.5 Where to Resume in New Conversation
1. Read this document for context
2. Fix offline maps first (Section 3) — show stopper

=== GroupTrack_EnrollmentAPI_v1.docx ===
GroupTrack — Enrollment & Follow API Specification
Document: GroupTrack_EnrollmentAPI_v1.docx
Created: April 12, 2026  |  Branch: feature/convoy-event-ride
Status: DEFINED — NOT YET IMPLEMENTED

1. Enrollment Endpoints
POST /enrollments — Create New Enrollment
Used when a rider responds to a public ride (Section 4) with no prior invite record. invite_date is NULL on this path.


Response: { enrollment_id, ride_id, user_id, status, invite_date, enrolled_at }

PUT /enrollments/{id} — Update Enrollment Status
Used when a rider changes their response in Section 2 or Section 3. Enrollment record already exists.


Response: { enrollment_id, ride_id, user_id, status, updated_at }

GET /enrollments — Fetch Rider Enrollments for Dashboard
Called on dashboard load to populate sections 2 and 3. Returns all enrollment records for the current user with full ride detail joined.


Response: array of enrollment objects, each including ride_id, ride_name, ride_date, start_time, arrive_time, visibility, ride_status, trailhead, trailhead_lat, trailhead_lng, location, organizer_id, organizer_name, organizer_email, enrollment_id, status, invite_date, enrolled_at

2. Follow / Unfollow Endpoints
POST /follows — Follow Organizer
Synchronous. Writes follow record, finds all organizer open public rides, creates invited enrollment for each (skips if already enrolled), queues invite emails. App refreshes all four dashboard sections on response.


Response: { message, enrollments_created, rides_invited[] }

Side effects:
Writes follows record with status=active
Queries rides WHERE organizer_id=? AND ride_status='open' AND is_public=1
Creates enrollment with status='invited' for each — skips if record exists
Sends invite email per ride (Phase C — stub in place)
App triggers full dashboard refresh on success response

DELETE /follows — Unfollow Organizer
Synchronous. Sets follow record inactive, removes only invited enrollments on open public rides. Never touches accepted/maybe/declined or private ride enrollments. App refreshes all four dashboard sections on response.


Response: { message, enrollments_removed, rides_checked }

Side effects:
Sets follows.status = inactive — record preserved for history
Deletes enrollments WHERE status='invited' AND ride is open AND public — only
Never touches accepted/maybe/declined enrollments
Never touches private ride enrollments regardless of status
App triggers full dashboard refresh on success response

3. Enrollment State Machine
Complete rules for every status transition across all three dashboard sections.


4. Calendar Entry Specification


Rule: always UPDATE the existing entry in place — never delete and recreate on title changes. Only DELETE when status transitions to declined.

5. Dashboard Refresh Rules


6. Section 4 Button Rules
Section 4 (Public Rides Near Me) only ever shows riders who have no existing enrollment record for that ride.


Once any enrollment record exists for a ride, it never appears in Section 4 again. The existing filter criteria handles this automatically.

7. Server Write Rules — CRITICAL
These rules are enforced on every API change to prevent file corruption.

Field | Type | Detail
ride_id | string (UUID) | Required — ride being enrolled in
user_id | string (UUID) | Required — rider's user_id
status | enum | accepted | maybe | declined
invite_date | date | NULL | NULL — no invite was sent on public path
Field | Type | Detail
status | enum | accepted | maybe | declined | cancelled
Parameter | Type | Detail
user_id | string (UUID) | Required — current rider's user_id
Field | Type | Detail
follower_id | string (UUID) | Required — rider doing the following
following_id | string (UUID) | Required — organizer being followed
Field | Type | Detail
follower_id | string (UUID) | Required
following_id | string (UUID) | Required
From | To | Section | Enrollment Action | Calendar Action
none | accepted | 4 only | POST /enrollments invite_date=NULL | CREATE — title=ride name
none | maybe | 4 only | POST /enrollments invite_date=NULL | CREATE — title=MAYBE — [Ride Name]
none | declined | 4 only | NOT VALID — button hidden | None
invited | accepted | 2 | PUT /enrollments/{id} | CREATE — title=ride name
invited | maybe | 2 | PUT /enrollments/{id} | CREATE — title=MAYBE — [Ride Name]
invited | declined | 2 | PUT /enrollments/{id} | None — no entry exists
accepted | maybe | 3 | PUT /enrollments/{id} | UPDATE — prepend MAYBE —
accepted | declined | 3 | PUT /enrollments/{id} | DELETE entry
maybe | accepted | 3 | PUT /enrollments/{id} | UPDATE — restore ride name
maybe | declined | 3 | PUT /enrollments/{id} | DELETE entry
declined | accepted | 3 | PUT /enrollments/{id} | CREATE — title=ride name
declined | maybe | 3 | PUT /enrollments/{id} | CREATE — title=MAYBE — [Ride Name]
Field | Value
Title — accepted | [Ride Name]
Title — maybe | MAYBE — [Ride Name]
Title — declined (if reverting to this) | DECLINED — [Ride Name]  (entry deleted per state machine)
Date | ride_date
Time | arrive_time — rider must arrive before rollout
Location | Trailhead GPS coordinates if available; trailhead name as fallback
Description | GroupTrack ride · Organizer: [name] · [email]
Trigger | Refresh Scope
Any enrollment button tap (Accept / Maybe / Decline) | All four dashboard sections
Follow checkbox tapped ON — POST /follows | All four dashboard sections
Follow checkbox tapped OFF — DELETE /follows | All four dashboard sections
Button | State | Rule
ACCEPT | Visible, enabled | Creates enrollment — card moves to Section 3
MAYBE | Visible, enabled | Creates enrollment — card moves to Section 3
DECLINE | Hidden | No enrollment exists — decline is not valid
SHOW RIDE DETAILS | Always visible | Navigates to ride detail screen
Rule | Detail
NEVER use heredoc from Windows Git Bash | Writes CRLF line endings — corrupts PHP file
NEVER use Python tempfile from Windows | Writes CRLF line endings — corrupts PHP file
CORRECT pattern — write on server side | SSH to server, use python3 -c on the server to write new code
Snapshot before every change | cp convoy_api.php ~/convoy_api_snapshot_$(date +%Y%m%d_%H%M%S).php
Git checkpoint before API/DB changes | git add -A && git commit -m 'checkpoint: pre-[change]'
Verify after every change | grep for new function names to confirm write succeeded

=== GroupTrack_Firebase_SHA256_Fix.docx ===
GroupTrack
Firebase SHA-256 Fix
Resolves 7–10 Second Startup Delay
May 2, 2026  |  Continuation Task
Problem
GroupTrack experiences a 7–10 second delay on app startup, showing the Meshtastic logo screen and occasionally a "Getting Started" onboarding screen. The app is unresponsive during this delay. This affects both release and debug builds on all devices.
Root Cause
Diagnosed via adb logcat. Google Play Services is rejecting the app’s signing certificate:
SecurityException: GoogleCertificatesRslt: not allowed: pkg=com.grouptrack.android,
sha256=35b05fe0c752f0a1de8ffbf793e31ad9ec703d621280b6067918c2f5b7873dc5
The google-services.json was updated for com.grouptrack.android in commit 744c1588c (Play Store prep), but the SHA-256 fingerprints for both the debug and release signing keys were never registered in Firebase. Google Play Services tries to authenticate on every app launch, gets rejected, and waits for timeout — that’s the 7–10 second delay.
SHA-256 Fingerprints to Register
Release fingerprint obtained from: keytool -list -v -keystore C:/Users/kixaz/grouptrack-release-key.jks -alias grouptrack
Debug fingerprint obtained from: logcat SecurityException error message
Solution — Step by Step
Part A: Add Fingerprints in Firebase Console
Open: https://console.firebase.google.com
Sign in with your Google account
Select the GroupTrack project (or the Meshtastic project that contains com.grouptrack.android)

=== GroupTrack_Handoff_May2_EOD.docx ===
GroupTrack
End of Day Handoff
May 1–2, 2026 Session
V2.4 Release Complete  |  V2.5 Planning  |  Confidential
PART 1: Completed This Session
1.1 Code Fixes Shipped
1.2 Code Fix Attempted and Reverted
1.3 Play Store & Infrastructure
1.4 Documents Created

PART 2: V2.4 Open Items — Moved to V2.5
These items were identified during V2.4 development and field testing. They are deferred to V2.5 to avoid delaying the Play Store release.
2.1 Firebase SHA-256 Startup Delay
Problem: 7–10 second delay on every app startup. Meshtastic logo screen hangs, occasionally shows Getting Started onboarding screen. Google Play Services throws SecurityException because the signing key SHA-256 fingerprints are not registered in Firebase for the com.grouptrack.android package.
Root cause: Commit 744c1588c updated google-services.json for the new package name but never registered the signing key fingerprints in Firebase console.
Fix: Add both release and debug SHA-256 fingerprints in Firebase Project Settings, download updated google-services.json, rebuild. Detailed instructions in GroupTrack_Firebase_SHA256_Fix.pdf.
Priority: CRITICAL — this affects every app launch for every user.
2.2 Tick Rationalization (Autopause + BLE + Android-Only)
Combined fix consolidating ANR-01, BLE-01, BLE-02, GPS-01, GPS-02 from earlier planning docs.
Problem: Autopause code is written and tested but was pulled from V2.4 because radio functions in the tick loop cause ANR when running Android-only after radio disconnect. The lead is locked to a radio node name (!phone is a different identity). When autopause disconnects the radio, lead goes null, trail accumulation skips, track freezes.
The single-node guard (if nodes.size <= 1) was attempted May 1 and reverted — caused Map Viewer ANR through unknown mechanism.
Approach: Full tick cycle audit using GroupTrack_TickEngine_Reference.md. Proper profiling with Android Studio Profiler before and after each change. No quick patches to tick.
2.3 Performance Regression
Observed: Android 2 (4/30 build) loads Map Viewer in ~6 seconds. Android 1 (current build) takes ~20 seconds. Same code. Needs proper profiling to identify cause — may be resolved by the Firebase fix (7–10 seconds of the delay is the Google Play Services timeout).
2.4 Track Management
Import code completed in V2.4 but testing was limited by storage permission issues (now fixed). Export and email share not yet implemented. Track picker layout conflict stashed.
Track picker layout fix (mutual exclusion per fix plan)
GPX import from Downloads — retest with storage permissions now fixed
Export recorded track to Downloads
Share track via email (share intent)
Export trail from trail info popup — tap trail, see info, export/share
Move GPX import parsing to background thread (71-track ANR fix)
2.5 Mileage Calculation — Enhanced
Basic mileage display is shipped and working in V2.4. V2.5 enhancement: tie mileage calculation to the GPX track writing process for accuracy. Currently accumulates in onGpsUpdate() which fires regardless of recording state.

PART 3: V2.5 New Features — Completing the Free Product
3.1 Vision
V2.4 ships as a working standalone Android trail app — GPS track recording, offline maps, trail overlays, KML/GPX import, and convoy tracking with mesh radios all work today. Radios are optional. The app is in the Play Store internal test pipeline.
V2.5 completes the free product feature set: trail discovery, route planning, waypoint management, track export/sharing, and auto-provisioning of map data. A rider can find trails for their area, plan a route, record a ride, and share the results without a subscription and without a radio.
The radio becomes an upgrade, not a prerequisite. Users discover the value of multi-rider convoy tracking through group rides with friends who have radios. V3.0 adds the premium layer — AWS backend, cloud sync, bounding box trail queries, org features — that drives revenue.
3.2 Trail Source Catalog (MAP-05/10) — Largest Task
The accuracy and completeness of trail data is the core differentiator. Our user experience is dictated by the accuracy of our trail component.
V2.5 builds a curated catalog of public trail data sources — BLM, USFS, state trail databases, county OHV systems — organized by state and region. The task is discovering those sources and creating the strategy to combine them. We do not want to import the whole country. We want a reference file that points to sources that our users can import.
Two-part work:
Part 1 — Source Discovery and Cataloging: Research every available public trail data source. Track API endpoint or download URL, format, coverage area, data quality, update frequency. Organize into a reference file the app can read.
Part 2 — User-Facing Catalog Browser: User selects state/region from catalog, sees available trail sources, picks what they want, data downloads and imports as local overlays available in both Map Viewer and Convoy Map.
V2.5 access method: Browse by state/region, select sources, import locally.
V3.0 enhancement: Bounding box query against AWS-hosted trail data as a premium feature. User draws a rectangle on the map, app queries for all trail sources in that area.
3.3 Snap-to-Trail Engine (MAP-06)
One engine, two applications:
Route planning: Draw a route on the map and it snaps to known trail geometry from loaded overlays. Plan rides that follow actual trails.
Convoy map lead track: The 5-second GPS broadcast creates angular straight lines between points. Snap-to adjusts those segments to conform to trail geometry underneath. Track follows the trail, not the GPS interpolation.
Core function: Nearest-neighbor projection onto trail segments from loaded GeoJSON. Build once, serve both maps.
3.4 Waypoint Management (MAP-07)
Drop pins on the map, name them, persist across sessions, display on both Map Viewer and Convoy Map. Waypoint categories: trailhead, staging, hazard, POI.
3.5 Tile Download Manager + Auto-Download (MAP-09)
Enhance tile download with auto-provisioning: when importing a track or saving a route, automatically download the map tiles required to support that data. Map data follows the data — no manual tile management needed.
3.6 GPS Recording 1-Second Interval (MAP-01)
ConvoyGpsService.kt line 249: change 3000L to 1000L. Track points every 1 second instead of 3. Higher resolution tracks for snap-to-trail accuracy and post-ride analysis.
3.7 GeoJSON Overlay Management
The feature set will expand past current GeoJSON to allow as many trail sources as possible. How we combine and present that data (multiple overlays, one overlay with multiple sources) will be part of the development process. Must support both Map Viewer and Convoy Map.

PART 4: V2.5 Complete Scope Summary
4.1 V2.4 Items Moved to V2.5
4.2 V2.5 New Features
4.3 Delivered in V2.4 (No Longer in Scope)
4.4 Moved to V3.0

PART 5: Play Store & Website Status

5.2 Website — grouptrack.org

GroupTrack
Installation Guide — Version 2.3
March 2026  |  Android  |  For Authorized Testers


PART 1 — WHAT YOU NEED

PART 2 — ENABLE INSTALLATION FROM UNKNOWN SOURCES
Android blocks apps from outside the Google Play Store by default. You need to enable this once before installing GroupTrack.

Android 8.0 and Later (Most Devices)
Open Settings on your Android device
Go to Apps (or Apps & Notifications)
Tap Special app access
Tap Install unknown apps
Find your email app or Files app in the list
Toggle Allow from this source to ON


PART 3 — INSTALL THE APK
Step 1 — Get the APK File
The file GroupTrack-V2.3-debug.apk will be provided by the organizer. Transfer it to your Android device using any of these methods:


Step 2 — Install the APK
Open the Files app or Downloads app on your Android device
Navigate to where you saved GroupTrack-V2.3-debug.apk
Tap the file
Android will show an Install screen — tap Install
If Play Protect shows a warning, tap Install Anyway — this is expected for apps outside the Play Store
Wait for installation to complete (10–30 seconds)
Tap Done or Open when installation finishes


PART 4 — FIRST LAUNCH AND RADIO PAIRING
Open GroupTrack from your app drawer
The GroupTrack splash screen appears briefly, then the map loads as the home screen
Turn on your Meshtastic radio
Tap the connections icon at the top of the screen (person/antenna icon)
Tap Scan for devices
Select your radio from the list
Wait for CONNECTED status — the status indicator turns green


PART 5 — VERSION 2.3 WHAT'S NEW

TROUBLESHOOTING

GroupTrack  |  Installation Guide V2.3  |  March 2026  |  For Authorized Testers  |  visit www.grouptrack.org
GroupTrack is a Meshtastic-based off-grid convoy coordination app. No cellular required. No server. Everything works via low-power LoRa radio mesh. This guide covers APK installation for authorized testers on Android devices.
REQUIREMENT | DETAIL
Android Device | Android 8.0 or later. Tablet recommended for best map visibility while riding.
Meshtastic Radio | SenseCAP T1000-E (recommended), T-Beam v2, or any Meshtastic LoRa device running firmware 2.6+.
Bluetooth | Required to pair Android device with the radio.
Storage | Approximately 100 MB free storage.
APK File | GroupTrack-V2.3-debug.apk — provided by the organizer via email or direct transfer.
TIP: You only need to do this once. You can turn it back off after installation if preferred.
METHOD | INSTRUCTIONS
Email (Easiest) | Open the email on your Android device. Tap the GroupTrack-V2.3-debug.apk attachment to download it directly.
Google Drive | Upload the APK to Google Drive from your PC. Open the Drive app on your Android device and download it.
USB Cable | Connect your Android device to your PC. Copy the APK to the Downloads folder on the device.
OneDrive / Dropbox | Upload from PC, download via the app on your Android device.
Installation Complete: GroupTrack will appear in your app list. Look for the GroupTrack icon. Proceed to Part 4 to pair your radio.
INFO: If your radio is brand new or factory reset, the organizer must configure it using Apply Ride Info to Radio before the radio will communicate with other GroupTrack members. See the User Guide for full instructions.
FEATURE | DESCRIPTION
Single-boot radio config | Radio configuration now applies in one reboot. No second stage required. Channel and PSK travel in the binary.
Auto-reconnect | Automatic Bluetooth disconnect/reconnect during radio reboot. No manual toggle required on most devices.
GroupTrack branding | App rebranded from Convoy Tracker to GroupTrack. Launcher, splash screen, and all UI labels updated.
UTV splash screen | New branded splash screen with GroupTrack logo and desert UTV photo.
Map tile fixes | SAT, HYB, TOPO, and RD map layers all working. Esri satellite, Esri topo, Google hybrid, Carto road.
Channel frequency fix | LoRa channel number (903 MHz = channel 5) now correctly captured and applied during master config.
GroupTrack nav label | Bottom navigation bar and submenu now show GroupTrack branding.
PROBLEM | SOLUTION
No vehicles on map | Check radio is connected (green status). Verify all radios use same channel name and PSK.
Yellow lock icon on radio | Encryption not active. Run Apply Radio again — complete the process fully.
Radio does not reconnect | Toggle Bluetooth OFF then ON manually in Android notification shade. Wait 10 seconds.
Verify screen shows failures | Channel write incomplete. Run Apply Radio again.
Imported ride not in RIDE tab | Open GroupTrack menu — splash screen confirms import. If no splash, resend the ride.
Play Protect warning on install | Tap Install Anyway. Expected for APKs outside the Play Store.
App closes when tapping BT settings | Known persistence issue — restart Apply Radio from the menu.
Map tiles not loading | Check internet connection. Online connection required for map tile display.

GroupTrack
Installation Guide — Version 2.4
March 2026  |  Android  |  For Authorized Testers
GroupTrack is a Meshtastic-based off-grid convoy coordination app. No cellular required. No server. Everything works via low-power LoRa radio mesh. This guide covers APK installation for authorized testers on Android devices.

PART 1 — WHAT YOU NEED

PART 2 — ENABLE INSTALLATION FROM UNKNOWN SOURCES
Android blocks apps from outside the Google Play Store by default. You need to enable this once before installing GroupTrack.

Android 8.0 and Later (Most Devices)
1. Open Settings on your Android device
2. Go to Apps (or Apps & Notifications)
3. Tap Special app access
4. Tap Install unknown apps
5. Find your email app or Files app in the list
6. Toggle Allow from this source to ON

TIP: You only need to do this once. You can turn it back off after installation if preferred.

PART 3 — INSTALL THE APK
Step 1 — Get the APK File
The file GroupTrack-V2.4-debug.apk will be provided by the organizer. Transfer it to your Android device using any of these methods:


Step 2 — Install the APK
1. Open the Files app or Downloads app on your Android device
2. Navigate to where you saved GroupTrack-V2.4-debug.apk
3. Tap the file
4. Android will show an Install screen — tap Install
5. If Play Protect shows a warning, tap Install Anyway — this is expected for apps outside the Play Store
6. Wait for installation to complete (10-30 seconds)
7. Tap Done or Open when installation finishes

Installation Complete: GroupTrack will appear in your app list. Look for the GroupTrack icon. Proceed to Part 4 to pair your radio.

PART 4 — FIRST LAUNCH AND RADIO PAIRING
1. Open GroupTrack from your app drawer
2. The GroupTrack splash screen appears briefly, then the map loads as the home screen
3. Turn on your Meshtastic radio
4. Tap the connections icon at the top of the screen (person/antenna icon)
5. Tap Scan for devices
6. Select your radio from the list
7. Wait for CONNECTED status — the status indicator turns green

INFO: If your radio is brand new or factory reset, the organizer must configure it using Apply Ride Info to Radio before the radio will communicate with other GroupTrack members. See the User Guide for full instructions.

PART 5 — RECOMMENDED DISPLAY SETTINGS
GroupTrack is designed for outdoor use in direct sunlight on a moving vehicle. These Android settings significantly improve map and HUD readability in the field.

High Contrast Text (Recommended)
Makes all text easier to read by adding a thin outline. Dramatically improves HUD readability at a glance while riding.


TIP: High Contrast Text is the single most impactful display setting for GroupTrack. Enable it before your first ride.

Screen Brightness
Set screen brightness to maximum before heading into the field. Auto-brightness may dim the screen in sunlight.
Settings > Display > Brightness > drag to maximum. Disable Adaptive Brightness if present.

Screen Timeout — Keep Awake
Prevent the screen from sleeping during a ride. A sleeping screen interrupts GPS tracking and Bluetooth connection.
Settings > Display > Screen Timeout > set to 30 minutes or Never.

IMPORTANT: Keep app open during offline map download. Background download is not yet supported. If the screen times out mid-download, the download stops. Tiles already downloaded are retained — restart the download and it will resume from where it stopped.

Battery Optimization — Unrestricted
Prevent Android from killing GroupTrack in the background during a ride.
Settings > Apps > GroupTrack > Battery > select Unrestricted (or No restrictions).

Bluetooth Timeout
If Bluetooth disconnects when the app is paused or the screen sleeps, enable these settings:
Settings > Apps > GroupTrack > Battery > Unrestricted
Settings > Developer Options > Stay Awake (while charging at staging area)
If radio disconnects after screen sleep: toggle Bluetooth OFF then ON in the notification shade and reconnect GroupTrack to the radio.

PART 6 — VERSION 2.4 WHAT'S NEW

TROUBLESHOOTING

Map blank offline | No tiles downloaded for this area. Download tiles while online before going to field.
Download blocked — too large | Reduce the selected area. 500MB ceiling enforced per download.
Location search no results | Add state (e.g. Zion UT). Use coordinates for precise locations: 37.44, -113.25.

=== GroupTrack_Installation_Guide_V2.4.docx ===
GroupTrack
Installation Guide — Version 2.4
March 2026  |  Android  |  For Authorized Testers
GroupTrack is a Meshtastic-based off-grid convoy coordination app. No cellular required. No server. Everything works via low-power LoRa radio mesh. This guide covers APK installation for authorized testers on Android devices.

PART 1 — WHAT YOU NEED

PART 2 — ENABLE INSTALLATION FROM UNKNOWN SOURCES
Android blocks apps from outside the Google Play Store by default. You need to enable this once before installing GroupTrack.

Android 8.0 and Later (Most Devices)
Open Settings on your Android device
Go to Apps (or Apps & Notifications)
Tap Special app access
Tap Install unknown apps
Find your email app or Files app in the list
Toggle Allow from this source to ON

TIP: You only need to do this once. You can turn it back off after installation if preferred.

PART 3 — INSTALL THE APK
Step 1 — Get the APK File
The file GroupTrack-V2.4-debug.apk will be provided by the organizer. Transfer it to your Android device using any of these methods:


Step 2 — Install the APK
Open the Files app or Downloads app on your Android device
Navigate to where you saved GroupTrack-V2.4-debug.apk
Tap the file
Android will show an Install screen — tap Install
If Play Protect shows a warning, tap Install Anyway — this is expected for apps outside the Play Store
Wait for installation to complete (10–30 seconds)
Tap Done or Open when installation finishes

Installation Complete: GroupTrack will appear in your app list. Look for the GroupTrack icon. Proceed to Part 4 to pair your radio.

PART 4 — FIRST LAUNCH AND RADIO PAIRING
Open GroupTrack from your app drawer
The GroupTrack splash screen appears briefly, then the map loads as the home screen
Turn on your Meshtastic radio
Tap the connections icon at the top of the screen (person/antenna icon)
Tap Scan for devices
Select your radio from the list
Wait for CONNECTED status — the status indicator turns green

INFO: If your radio is brand new or factory reset, the organizer must configure it using Apply Ride Info to Radio before the radio will communicate with other GroupTrack members. See the User Guide for full instructions.

PART 5 — VERSION 2.4 WHAT'S NEW

TROUBLESHOOTING
REQUIREMENT | DETAIL
Android Device | Android 8.0 or later. Tablet recommended for best map visibility while riding.
Meshtastic Radio | SenseCAP T1000-E (recommended), T-Beam v2, or any Meshtastic LoRa device running firmware 2.6+.
Bluetooth | Required to pair Android device with the radio.
Storage | Minimum 500 MB free storage recommended for offline map tiles. 100 MB minimum for app only.
APK File | GroupTrack-V2.4-debug.apk — provided by the organizer via email or direct transfer.
METHOD | INSTRUCTIONS
Email (Easiest) | Open the email on your Android device. Tap the GroupTrack-V2.4-debug.apk attachment to download it directly.
Google Drive | Upload the APK to Google Drive from your PC. Open the Drive app on your Android device and download it.
USB Cable | Connect your Android device to your PC. Copy the APK to the Downloads folder on the device.
OneDrive / Dropbox | Upload from PC, download via the app on your Android device.
FEATURE | DESCRIPTION
Offline map tile download | Draw a rectangle on the map to download tiles for offline use. All 4 tile types (SAT, HYB, TOPO, RD) can be cached for field use without internet.
Location search | Search for a named area (city, park, trail system) before downloading. Map flies to center with a reference pin and crosshair lines. Up to 5 results shown.
Download progress chip | Live progress overlay on map during download: tile count, time remaining. Toast notifications on start and completion. Keep app open during download.
Online / Offline switch | Manual toggle in map settings to switch between streaming tiles (ONLINE) and local cache (LOCAL). Status shown in map header.
Fly radius control | Slider to control how close the map zooms when flying to a search result (1-10 miles).
Download size estimation | Before downloading, dialog shows area dimensions in miles, tile count, estimated MB, and tile source. 500MB ceiling enforced.
Single-boot radio config | Radio configuration applies in one reboot. Channel and PSK travel in the binary. No second stage required.
Auto-reconnect | Automatic Bluetooth disconnect/reconnect during radio reboot. No manual toggle required on most devices.
GroupTrack branding | App rebranded with GroupTrack launcher, splash screen, nav labels, and website reference.
PROBLEM | SOLUTION
No vehicles on map | Check radio is connected (green status). Verify all radios use same channel name and PSK.
Yellow lock icon on radio | Encryption not active. Run Apply Radio again — complete the process fully.
Radio does not reconnect | Toggle Bluetooth OFF then ON manually in Android notification shade. Wait 10 seconds.
Verify screen shows failures | Channel write incomplete. Run Apply Radio again.
Imported ride not in RIDE tab | Open GroupTrack menu — splash screen confirms import. If no splash, resend the ride.
Play Protect warning on install | Tap Install Anyway. Expected for APKs outside the Play Store.
Map tiles not loading online | Check internet connection. Online connection required for map tile display.
Map blank offline | No tiles downloaded for this area. Download tiles while online before going to field.
Download blocked — too large | Reduce the selected area. 500MB ceiling enforced per download.
Location search no results | Add state (e.g. 'Zion UT'). Use coordinates for precise locations: 37.44, -113.25.


=== GroupTrack_LeadLock_Problem_Resolution (1).docx ===
GroupTrack
Lead Cart Assignment — Problem Analysis & Resolution Plan
V2.4 Known Issue | Phase C Resolution | April 1, 2026

1. Current Implementation — Lead by Callsign
The current V2.4 lead cart assignment rule is based on the radio long name (callsign). The lead cart is the node whose callsign exactly matches "Lead" (case-insensitive).

Rule:
nodes.firstOrNull { it.callsign.equals("Lead", ignoreCase = true) }

This replaced the prior auto-election system which caused lead identity drift. The callsign rule is deterministic — one radio is programmed with long name "Lead" before the ride.


2. The Problem — Meshtastic Node Name Caching
Lead cart assignment fails when the Android device has a stale cached node name for the lead radio. This is a Meshtastic firmware behavior, not a GroupTrack code bug.

2.1 Root Cause
Meshtastic stores node info (including long name) in a local database on each Android device.
Node info is broadcast by the radio periodically — not continuously. Broadcast interval is firmware-controlled and can be hours apart.
When a radio is renamed to "Lead", the Android device does not immediately receive the updated name.
GroupTrack lead lock checks the callsign field which reads from this cached data — if the cache has the old name, no lead is found.
Clearing the Android node DB does not fix it — the stale data is served from the radio firmware cache, not the Android DB.

2.2 Observed Behavior
Radio programmed with long name "Lead".
Android device attached to Lead radio shows "Lead" correctly — local device always has current name.
Other Android devices show old cached name — do not recognize Lead radio.
GroupTrack finds no node with callsign "Lead" on those devices — no lead assigned, no track recorded.


3. Removed Code — Auto-Assign Lead Lock
The original auto-election system was developed and tested but removed from production before the V2.4 field test. It is preserved in git and documented for Phase C resurrection.

3.1 What Was Removed
The auto-assign lead lock used a quarter-mile accumulator to elect and lock a lead cart based on position and movement, without requiring a specific radio name.


3.2 Removed Fields and State (ConvoyViewModel.kt)
_leadLockedFlag — Boolean StateFlow, lead lock state
leadLockDistanceAccum — Float, GPS distance accumulator toward 0.25 mile lock
lastLeadLockLat / lastLeadLockLon — Double, last GPS position for accumulator calculation
_leadLocked — StateFlow<Boolean> exposed to UI
explicitLeadId — String?, duplicate lead representation that caused drift (removed separately)
Accumulator tick block — coroutine block in ConvoyViewModel that incremented distance and fired lock

3.3 Removed Functions (ConvoyEngine.kt)
assignLeadTail(nodes, lockedLeadId) — election and lock logic accepting optional locked ID
Lead election by speed gate — nodes.filter { speed_mph > 0.5f } before election
Lock fire condition — if (distanceTraveled >= LEAD_LOCK_DISTANCE_MILES && !leadLocked)

3.4 Why It Was Removed
Gradle cache corruption throughout the day (space in GRADLE_USER_HOME) meant no code changes were compiling — all test rides ran stale APKs.
Without reliable debug output, impossible to confirm lock was firing correctly.
LongName approach is deterministic and testable indoors — chosen for reliability ahead of Wednesday field test.

3.5 Resurrection Commands
To restore the auto-assign code from git:
git checkout 96a4dad19 -- app/src/main/java/com/geeksville/mesh/convoy/ConvoyEngine.kt
git checkout 96a4dad19 -- app/src/main/java/com/geeksville/mesh/convoy/ConvoyViewModel.kt

Note: The resurrected code needs the _lockedLeadId persistent storage fix applied before testing — this was never in a clean committed state.


4. Resolution Plan — Phase C Two-Tier Lead Assignment
The resolution combines the current callsign rule (Tier 1) with a resurrected accumulator fallback (Tier 2) that activates when no named lead is found within the first quarter mile.

4.1 Two-Tier Logic
Tier 1 — Callsign Match (current, unchanged):
Check for node with callsign == "Lead" on every tick.
If found, assign as lead. Lock after 0.25 miles. Normal operating mode.

Tier 2 — Accumulator Fallback (resurrect from 96a4dad19):
If no "Lead" callsign found after 0.25 miles of movement, activate fallback.
Fallback selects lead by position — frontmost node by distance traveled from start.
Fallback lead locked and flagged as FALLBACK in HUD (amber indicator).
If "Lead" callsign subsequently appears, switch to Tier 1 on next RECALC LEAD.

4.2 Implementation Scope
Phase C task — not Phase B.
Target files: ConvoyEngine.kt, ConvoyViewModel.kt, ConvoyScreen.kt (HUD indicator).
Target screens: s17 Map-CONVOY, s18 Map-ORGANIZED.
Reference: GroupTrack_LeadLock_AutoAssign_Design.md for original accumulator rules.


5. Interim Workaround — V2.4 Field Use
Until Phase C is implemented:
Program lead radio with long name "Lead" at least 24 hours before the ride.
At staging, verify all devices show "Lead" in stock Meshtastic node list before launching GroupTrack.

=== GroupTrack_LeadLock_Problem_Resolution.docx ===
GroupTrack
Lead Cart Assignment — Problem Analysis & Resolution Plan
V2.4 Known Issue | Phase C Resolution | April 1, 2026

1. Current Implementation — Lead by Callsign
The current V2.4 lead cart assignment rule is based on the radio long name (callsign). The lead cart is defined as the node whose callsign exactly matches "Lead" (case-insensitive).

Rule:
nodes.firstOrNull { it.callsign.equals("Lead", ignoreCase = true) }

This replaced the prior auto-election system (explicitLeadId) which caused lead identity drift and wrong-node track recording. The callsign rule is deterministic — one radio is programmed with long name "Lead" before the ride.


2. The Problem — Meshtastic Node Name Caching
Lead cart assignment fails when the Android device running GroupTrack has a stale cached node name for the lead radio. This is a Meshtastic firmware behavior, not a GroupTrack code bug.

2.1 Root Cause
Meshtastic stores node info (including long name) in a local database on each Android device.
Node info is broadcast by the radio periodically — not continuously. Broadcast interval is firmware-controlled and can be hours apart.
When a radio is renamed (long name changed to "Lead"), the Android device does not immediately receive the updated name.
The Android device continues to display and use the old cached name until the radio broadcasts a node info packet.
GroupTrack lead lock checks the callsign field which reads from this cached data — if the cache has the old name, no lead is found.

2.2 Observed Behavior
Radio is programmed with long name "Lead".
Android device A (attached to Lead radio) shows "Lead" correctly — local device always has current name.
Android device B (other cart) shows old cached name — does not recognize Lead radio.
GroupTrack on device B finds no node with callsign "Lead" — no lead assigned, no track recorded.
Clearing Android node DB does not fix it — the stale data is served from the radio firmware cache, not the Android DB.
Resetting radio node DB via web client does not fix it — the radio re-broadcasts its own node info but the timing is unpredictable.

2.3 Why the 15-Minute Pre-Ride Window Does Not Reliably Solve It
Node info broadcast interval in Meshtastic firmware is not guaranteed within 15 minutes.
The radio will eventually broadcast updated node info, but "eventually" can be hours.
There is no reliable way to force an immediate node info broadcast from the Android app without touching Meshtastic internals.


3. Approaches Attempted — April 1, 2026


4. Resolution Plan — Phase C Implementation
The lead cart assignment must be decoupled from the Meshtastic node name cache. The resolution requires a fallback mechanism that activates when no "Lead" callsign is found within the first quarter mile of a ride.

4.1 Two-Tier Lead Assignment (Proposed)
Tier 1 — Callsign Match (current, unchanged):
Check for node with callsign == "Lead" on every tick.
If found, assign as lead. Lock after 0.25 miles. This is the normal operating mode.

Tier 2 — Accumulator Fallback (new):
If no "Lead" callsign found after 0.25 miles of movement, activate fallback.
Fallback selects lead by position — the frontmost node (highest distance traveled from start point).
Fallback lead is locked and flagged as FALLBACK in HUD.
If "Lead" callsign subsequently appears (node info finally broadcasts), switch to Tier 1 and re-lock on named lead.

4.2 Quarter Mile Accumulator Logic

4.3 Pre-Ride Node Info Force Broadcast (Research Item)
A more complete fix requires forcing the radio to immediately broadcast its node info packet when GroupTrack launches. This requires one of:
Sending an admin packet to the local radio requesting immediate node info broadcast — requires RadioController access from GroupTrack.
Modifying the Meshtastic position broadcast interval to 0 seconds temporarily on launch — forces immediate position + node info packet.
Adding a "FORCE NODE ANNOUNCE" button to Field Radio screen (s31) — organizer taps before ride, radio broadcasts node info immediately.
Research required: which Meshtastic API call forces immediate node info rebroadcast without factory reset.

4.4 Implementation Scope
Phase C task — not Phase B. Phase B is platform wiring only.
Target screen: s17 Map-CONVOY, s18 Map-ORGANIZED (HUD fallback indicator).
Target files: ConvoyEngine.kt (two-tier logic), ConvoyViewModel.kt (accumulator state), ConvoyScreen.kt (HUD indicator).
The existing explicitLeadId design doc (GroupTrack_LeadLock_AutoAssign_Design.md) contains the original accumulator logic — reference for Phase C rebuild.


5. Interim Workaround — V2.4 Field Use
Until Phase C is implemented, the following pre-ride procedure ensures lead cart is correctly identified:

Program lead radio with long name "Lead" at least 24 hours before the ride. This gives time for the name to propagate to all devices that have previously seen that radio.
At ride staging, open Meshtastic (not GroupTrack) on all devices and confirm the Lead radio shows as "Lead" in the node list on every device.


=== GroupTrack_MapManager_Spec (1).docx ===
GroupTrack
Map Manager — Complete Specification
April 1, 2026  |  Phase C  |  Branch: feature/convoy-event-ride

1. Overview and Design Intent
Map Manager is a dedicated screen for all pre-ride planning activities involving maps, routes, tracks, and waypoints. It is completely isolated from the active ride map. No convoy nodes, no HUD, no ride buttons appear in Map Manager.

The core principle: planning activities belong in Map Manager, execution activities belong on the Ride Map. The two maps share an overlay selector component but serve distinct purposes.

2. Two Map Architecture
2.1 Ride Map — Persistent, Execution

2.2 Map Manager — On Demand, Planning

3. Four Search Scope Methods
All four methods resolve to the same result: a map viewport. Once the viewport is set, the overlay selector filters to that viewport. One consistent downstream behavior regardless of how the user arrived at the view.


4. Overlay Selector Component
Shared component used in both Ride Map and Map Manager. Filters dynamically to current map bounds. Used in Ride Map for active ride assets. Used in Map Manager for full library.

4.1 Content Types and Visual Language

4.2 Filtering Behavior
Bounds filter — default, auto-updates on pan/zoom. Shows only items whose geometry intersects current viewport.
Name search — global library search, returns matches regardless of location.
SELECT ALL — applies to filtered list only, not entire library.
Count indicator — X routes, Y waypoints, Z rides in this area.
Selected-outside-view — items selected but not in current view collapsed into a separate section.

5. Waypoint Creation
5.1 Ride Map — Field Waypoints
Long press on map canvas drops a waypoint at that location. Careful not to conflict with existing long press behavior used for node selection — waypoint drop only fires on empty map area, not on node markers.


5.2 Map Manager — Library Waypoints

6. Ride Creation Integration
Map Manager assets feed directly into ride creation. Three paths to defining the ride geographic scope:


Trailhead waypoint assigned in Map Manager auto-populates ride creation form fields: trailhead_lat, trailhead_lon, trailhead_name. No manual coordinate entry required.

7. Data Model — New DB Tables and JSON
7.1 New RDS Tables

7.2 waypoints Table Schema

7.3 Local JSON Storage
Waypoints and overlay selections stored locally in filesDir for offline access:


8. Screen Components
8.1 ConvoyMapManagerScreen.kt — New File

8.2 V2.4 Ride Map Changes

8.3 Navigation Entry Points

9. Phase C Build Sequence
Map Manager builds after Phase B is complete and field tested. All items below are Phase C tasks.


10. V2.4 Map Tasks — Current Sprint
Two changes only. Minimal touch to existing code. Execute before Map Manager build begins.


GroupTrack  |  Map Manager Specification  |  April 1, 2026  |  Phase C
Feature | Description
OFFLINE/ONLINE toggle | Always visible on floating bar — one tap, no menu
Waypoint drop | Long press on map — name dialog — saved locally and displayed immediately
Route overlay | Displays route assigned to active ride. Solid line, orange. Read-only during ride.
Waypoint display | Shows waypoints assigned to active ride. Icon by type.
Ride history overlay | Optional — toggle completed rides in same area as colored track lines
Track recording | REC / PAUSE / STOP — unchanged from V2.4
FIND AREA | REMOVED from Ride Map — planning function belongs in Map Manager only
Feature | Description
Tile download | Draw region, select zoom levels, execute download, track progress
FIND AREA | Geocoder search — flies map to named location, viewport becomes search scope
Route library | Import GPX/KML, view, name, assign to ride
Waypoint library | Create, name, type (Trailhead/Staging/Hazard/POI), manage, assign to ride
Route Planner | PLACEHOLDER V2.4 — grayed out, labeled Coming Soon. Active in Phase D.
Overlay selector | Filter and toggle routes, waypoints, ride history in current view
Ride assignment | Assign route + trailhead waypoint to a ride event — feeds ride creation form
# | Method | How It Works | Viewport Result
1 | Area Search | Default mode. Current map viewport. Auto-updates on every pan and zoom. | Current map view
2 | FIND AREA | Geocoder search — user types location name. Map flies to result. | Bounding box of named location
3 | Name Search | User types partial route or track name. Tap result — map centers on item bounding box. | Item bounding box
4 | Waypoint Radius | Radius slider draws circle from map center or GPS position. Adjustable distance control. | Circular radius boundary
Type | Visual Style | Color | Notes
Routes | Solid line, 3px weight | Orange #FF8C00 | Planned paths — pre-ride, assigned to ride events
Tracks | Dashed line, 2px weight | Blue/Purple #6B4EFF | Recorded paths — actual GPS history from rides
Waypoints | Icon by type | Icon color by type | Trailhead, Staging, Hazard, POI — distinct icons
Ride History | Solid line, 2px weight | Palette — unique per ride | Completed organized rides — color assigned from fixed palette
Step | Behavior
1. Long press | Detects long press on empty map area. Node marker long press unchanged.
2. Name dialog | Simple name entry dialog appears. SAVE / CANCEL.
3. Save | Waypoint stored to filesDir/waypoints/. Displayed on map immediately as POI icon.
4. Export | Field waypoints export with GPX track post-ride.
Field | Description
Name | Required. Free text.
Type | Trailhead / Staging Area / Hazard / Point of Interest
Description | Optional notes.
GPS | Auto-populated from drop location. Editable.
Assign to ride | Optional. Trailhead type auto-populates ride creation trailhead fields.
Priority | Method | Behavior
1 | Assign existing route | Bounding box auto-calculated from route geometry. Tile download suggestion auto-generated.
2 | Assign existing track | Bounding box auto-calculated from track geometry.
3 | Import GPX/KML | Import during ride creation. MD5 computed on device. Stored to kml_routes table. Bounding box auto-calculated.
4 | Draw manually | Map Manager area selection. Fallback only when no route or track exists.
Table | Purpose
waypoints | User waypoint library. Stores name, type, lat, lon, description, ride_id (nullable), user_id.
route_overlays | Junction table — which routes and waypoints are assigned to which rides for overlay display.
map_areas | Named map areas with bounding boxes. Created from tile download regions. Referenced by rides.
Column | Type | Notes
waypoint_id | INT PK AUTO | Primary key
user_id | INT FK | Foreign key to users table
ride_id | INT FK NULL | Optional — assigned to a specific ride
name | VARCHAR(100) | Required. Display name.
waypoint_type | ENUM | TRAILHEAD, STAGING, HAZARD, POI
latitude | DECIMAL(9,6) | GPS latitude
longitude | DECIMAL(9,6) | GPS longitude
description | TEXT NULL | Optional notes
created_at | TIMESTAMP | Auto-set on insert
File | Contents
filesDir/waypoints/waypoints.json | Full waypoint library. Synced to RDS on connectivity.
filesDir/overlays/ride_{id}_overlays.json | Per-ride overlay selections. Which routes and waypoints are toggled on.
filesDir/my_tracks/ | Recorded GPX tracks. Existing V2.4 location unchanged.
filesDir/routes/ | New. Imported GPX/KML route files.
Component | Description
MapManagerWebView | Full-screen Leaflet map. Same convoy:// tile intercept. No convoy data, no nodes.
MapManagerTopBar | Screen title, back button, FIND AREA search field.
MapManagerFloatingBar | Draggable floating panel. Two accordion sections: MAP CONTROLS and MAP DOWNLOAD.
OverlaySelectorPanel | Shared component. Filter tabs: Area / Name / Radius. SELECT ALL. Content list.
WaypointCreateDialog | Long press dialog. Name, type, description. SAVE / CANCEL.
RouteImportDialog | File picker for GPX/KML. Name field. IMPORT / CANCEL.
RoutePlannerPlaceholder | Grayed button. ROUTE PLANNER — Coming Soon label.
TileDownloadPanel | Moved from V2.4 ride map. Draw region, zoom level, download button, progress.
Change | Detail
Separate settings menu | Two accordion sections: Map Controls and Map Download. Replaces flat list.
OFFLINE toggle promoted | Visible on floating bar at all times. Not hidden in menu.
Waypoint drop | Long press on empty map area. Does not fire on node markers.
FIND AREA removed | Geocoder moved to Map Manager only.
Overlay selector added | Shows routes and waypoints assigned to active ride.
Entry Point | Detail
Dashboard — MAP MANAGER button | Standalone access. Internet gate not required — offline map functions work without connectivity.
Create Ride — SELECT ROUTE button | Launches Map Manager in route selection mode. Returns selected route to ride creation form.
Create Ride — IMPORT KML/GPX button | Opens file picker. Imports route into Map Manager library and assigns to ride.

=== GroupTrack_MapManager_Spec.docx ===
GroupTrack
Map Manager — Complete Specification
April 1, 2026  |  Phase C  |  Branch: feature/convoy-event-ride

1. Overview and Design Intent
Map Manager is a dedicated screen for all pre-ride planning activities involving maps, routes, tracks, and waypoints. It is completely isolated from the active ride map. No convoy nodes, no HUD, no ride buttons appear in Map Manager.

The core principle: planning activities belong in Map Manager, execution activities belong on the Ride Map. The two maps share an overlay selector component but serve distinct purposes.

2. Two Map Architecture
2.1 Ride Map — Persistent, Execution

2.2 Map Manager — On Demand, Planning

3. Four Search Scope Methods
All four methods resolve to the same result: a map viewport. Once the viewport is set, the overlay selector filters to that viewport. One consistent downstream behavior regardless of how the user arrived at the view.


4. Overlay Selector Component
Shared component used in both Ride Map and Map Manager. Filters dynamically to current map bounds. Used in Ride Map for active ride assets. Used in Map Manager for full library.

4.1 Content Types and Visual Language

4.2 Filtering Behavior
Bounds filter — default, auto-updates on pan/zoom. Shows only items whose geometry intersects current viewport.
Name search — global library search, returns matches regardless of location.
SELECT ALL — applies to filtered list only, not entire library.
Count indicator — X routes, Y waypoints, Z rides in this area.
Selected-outside-view — items selected but not in current view collapsed into a separate section.

5. Waypoint Creation
5.1 Ride Map — Field Waypoints
Long press on map canvas drops a waypoint at that location. Careful not to conflict with existing long press behavior used for node selection — waypoint drop only fires on empty map area, not on node markers.


5.2 Map Manager — Library Waypoints

6. Ride Creation Integration
Map Manager assets feed directly into ride creation. Three paths to defining the ride geographic scope:


Trailhead waypoint assigned in Map Manager auto-populates ride creation form fields: trailhead_lat, trailhead_lon, trailhead_name. No manual coordinate entry required.

7. Data Model — New DB Tables and JSON
7.1 New RDS Tables

7.2 waypoints Table Schema

7.3 Local JSON Storage
Waypoints and overlay selections stored locally in filesDir for offline access:


8. Screen Components
8.1 ConvoyMapManagerScreen.kt — New File

8.2 V2.4 Ride Map Changes

8.3 Navigation Entry Points

9. Phase C Build Sequence
Map Manager builds after Phase B is complete and field tested. All items below are Phase C tasks.


10. V2.4 Map Tasks — Current Sprint
Two changes only. Minimal touch to existing code. Execute before Map Manager build begins.


GroupTrack  |  Map Manager Specification  |  April 1, 2026  |  Phase C
Feature | Description
OFFLINE/ONLINE toggle | Always visible on floating bar — one tap, no menu
Waypoint drop | Long press on map — name dialog — saved locally and displayed immediately
Route overlay | Displays route assigned to active ride. Solid line, orange. Read-only during ride.
Waypoint display | Shows waypoints assigned to active ride. Icon by type.
Ride history overlay | Optional — toggle completed rides in same area as colored track lines
Track recording | REC / PAUSE / STOP — unchanged from V2.4
FIND AREA | REMOVED from Ride Map — planning function belongs in Map Manager only
Feature | Description
Tile download | Draw region, select zoom levels, execute download, track progress
FIND AREA | Geocoder search — flies map to named location, viewport becomes search scope
Route library | Import GPX/KML, view, name, assign to ride
Waypoint library | Create, name, type (Trailhead/Staging/Hazard/POI), manage, assign to ride
Route Planner | PLACEHOLDER V2.4 — grayed out, labeled Coming Soon. Active in Phase D.
Overlay selector | Filter and toggle routes, waypoints, ride history in current view
Ride assignment | Assign route + trailhead waypoint to a ride event — feeds ride creation form
# | Method | How It Works | Viewport Result
1 | Area Search | Default mode. Current map viewport. Auto-updates on every pan and zoom. | Current map view
2 | FIND AREA | Geocoder search — user types location name. Map flies to result. | Bounding box of named location
3 | Name Search | User types partial route or track name. Tap result — map centers on item bounding box. | Item bounding box
4 | Waypoint Radius | Radius slider draws circle from map center or GPS position. Adjustable distance control. | Circular radius boundary
Type | Visual Style | Color | Notes
Routes | Solid line, 3px weight | Orange #FF8C00 | Planned paths — pre-ride, assigned to ride events
Tracks | Dashed line, 2px weight | Blue/Purple #6B4EFF | Recorded paths — actual GPS history from rides
Waypoints | Icon by type | Icon color by type | Trailhead, Staging, Hazard, POI — distinct icons
Ride History | Solid line, 2px weight | Palette — unique per ride | Completed organized rides — color assigned from fixed palette
Step | Behavior
1. Long press | Detects long press on empty map area. Node marker long press unchanged.
2. Name dialog | Simple name entry dialog appears. SAVE / CANCEL.
3. Save | Waypoint stored to filesDir/waypoints/. Displayed on map immediately as POI icon.
4. Export | Field waypoints export with GPX track post-ride.
Field | Description
Name | Required. Free text.
Type | Trailhead / Staging Area / Hazard / Point of Interest
Description | Optional notes.
GPS | Auto-populated from drop location. Editable.
Assign to ride | Optional. Trailhead type auto-populates ride creation trailhead fields.
Priority | Method | Behavior
1 | Assign existing route | Bounding box auto-calculated from route geometry. Tile download suggestion auto-generated.
2 | Assign existing track | Bounding box auto-calculated from track geometry.
3 | Import GPX/KML | Import during ride creation. MD5 computed on device. Stored to kml_routes table. Bounding box auto-calculated.
4 | Draw manually | Map Manager area selection. Fallback only when no route or track exists.
Table | Purpose
waypoints | User waypoint library. Stores name, type, lat, lon, description, ride_id (nullable), user_id.
route_overlays | Junction table — which routes and waypoints are assigned to which rides for overlay display.
map_areas | Named map areas with bounding boxes. Created from tile download regions. Referenced by rides.
Column | Type | Notes
waypoint_id | INT PK AUTO | Primary key
user_id | INT FK | Foreign key to users table
ride_id | INT FK NULL | Optional — assigned to a specific ride
name | VARCHAR(100) | Required. Display name.
waypoint_type | ENUM | TRAILHEAD, STAGING, HAZARD, POI
latitude | DECIMAL(9,6) | GPS latitude
longitude | DECIMAL(9,6) | GPS longitude
description | TEXT NULL | Optional notes
created_at | TIMESTAMP | Auto-set on insert
File | Contents
filesDir/waypoints/waypoints.json | Full waypoint library. Synced to RDS on connectivity.
filesDir/overlays/ride_{id}_overlays.json | Per-ride overlay selections. Which routes and waypoints are toggled on.
filesDir/my_tracks/ | Recorded GPX tracks. Existing V2.4 location unchanged.
filesDir/routes/ | New. Imported GPX/KML route files.
Component | Description
MapManagerWebView | Full-screen Leaflet map. Same convoy:// tile intercept. No convoy data, no nodes.
MapManagerTopBar | Screen title, back button, FIND AREA search field.
MapManagerFloatingBar | Draggable floating panel. Two accordion sections: MAP CONTROLS and MAP DOWNLOAD.
OverlaySelectorPanel | Shared component. Filter tabs: Area / Name / Radius. SELECT ALL. Content list.
WaypointCreateDialog | Long press dialog. Name, type, description. SAVE / CANCEL.
RouteImportDialog | File picker for GPX/KML. Name field. IMPORT / CANCEL.
RoutePlannerPlaceholder | Grayed button. ROUTE PLANNER — Coming Soon label.
TileDownloadPanel | Moved from V2.4 ride map. Draw region, zoom level, download button, progress.
Change | Detail
Separate settings menu | Two accordion sections: Map Controls and Map Download. Replaces flat list.
OFFLINE toggle promoted | Visible on floating bar at all times. Not hidden in menu.
Waypoint drop | Long press on empty map area. Does not fire on node markers.
FIND AREA removed | Geocoder moved to Map Manager only.
Overlay selector added | Shows routes and waypoints assigned to active ride.
Entry Point | Detail
Dashboard — MAP MANAGER button | Standalone access. Internet gate not required — offline map functions work without connectivity.
Create Ride — SELECT ROUTE button | Launches Map Manager in route selection mode. Returns selected route to ride creation form.
Create Ride — IMPORT KML/GPX button | Opens file picker. Imports route into Map Manager library and assigns to ride.

=== GroupTrack_NightlyProcesses_v1.docx ===
GroupTrack — Nightly Process Specification
Document: GroupTrack_NightlyProcesses_v1.docx
Created: April 12, 2026  |  Branch: feature/convoy-event-ride
Status: DEFINED — NOT YET IMPLEMENTED
Purpose
This document is the single authoritative reference for all nightly batch processes in the GroupTrack system. All automated background jobs that run on a schedule must be defined here before implementation. No process runs in production without an entry in this document.
Design principle: one consolidated nightly runner, not scattered individual scripts. All jobs execute from a single entry point so the full scope of scheduled activity is always visible in one place.

Process Registry
All defined nightly processes. Status: DEFINED = specified not yet built. IMPLEMENTED = running in production.


Process Detail
NP-001 — Ride Auto-Complete
Purpose: Automatically transition rides to completed status when the ride date has passed. Prevents stale open/closed rides from appearing in rider dashboards indefinitely.


Scope: only open and closed rides transition to completed. Pending and cancelled rides are not affected.
Timing: 1 day past ride_date — rides remain visible on their ride date and transition the following morning.
Dashboard impact: completed rides drop from Section 2 (Invites Pending Replies) and Section 4 (Public Rides Near Me). They remain visible in Section 3 (My Invite Responses) as historical record.

Implementation Notes — When Built
Single entry point: rides_nightly.php on EC2. All NP-xxx processes execute sequentially from this one file.
Cron schedule: 0 1 * * * php /var/www/html/rides_nightly.php >> /var/log/grouptrack_nightly.log 2>&1
Logging: each process logs start time, rows affected, and completion time. Errors logged and do not halt subsequent processes.
Server: EC2 34.224.89.217 | Database: convoy-tracker-db.cudtjxrtdbql.us-east-1.rds.amazonaws.com | DB: convoy_tracker

Future Processes — To Be Defined
Add new process definitions here before implementation. Each entry requires: Process ID, Name, Target table, Condition, Action, Dashboard impact.
Candidate processes identified for future definition: invite expiry cleanup, orphaned enrollment cleanup, follower notification digest.
Process ID | Name | Description | Schedule | Status
NP-001 | Ride Auto-Complete | Set ride_status = completed for rides 1+ day past ride_date | 00:01 daily | DEFINED
Process | Target Table | Condition | Action
NP-001 | rides | ride_date < CURDATE() - INTERVAL 1 DAY AND ride_status IN ('open','closed') | SET ride_status = 'completed'


=== GroupTrack_OfflineMaps_Debug (1).docx ===
GroupTrack
Offline Maps Debug Summary
Day 25 — March 26, 2026  |  SHOW STOPPER — RESOLVE FIRST
SHOW STOPPER: Offline map tiles do not render when OFFLINE switch is toggled. Map goes grey. Online maps work perfectly. Tiles exist on device at correct path.

1. Current State

2. What We Know Works

3. What We Tried

4. Most Likely Cause
The persistent WebView (viewModel.persistentWebView) was created BEFORE allowFileAccessFromFileURLs was added. WebView settings can only be applied before loadUrl() is called. The persistent WebView loaded convoy_map.html once and cached it. Even after reinstall, the ViewModel retains the old WebView instance without the file access settings.

Secondary possibility: Android 10+ blocks file:// URL access in WebView regardless of allowFileAccessFromFileURLs setting. Requires testing with a fresh WebView instance to confirm.

5. Recommended Fix — Day 26 Start
Step 1 — Verify WebChromeClient is missing
grep -n "WebChromeClient|onConsoleMessage" app/src/main/java/com/geeksville/mesh/convoy/ConvoyScreen.kt
Expected: no output. Confirms patch failed.

Step 2 — Force WebView to recreate
In ConvoyViewModel.kt, find persistentWebView and check how it persists. The fix is to null it out temporarily so the else branch (new WebView creation) runs — this creates a fresh WebView WITH the allowFileAccessFromFileURLs setting.
grep -n "persistentWebView" app/src/main/java/com/geeksville/mesh/convoy/ConvoyViewModel.kt | head -10

Step 3 — Apply WebChromeClient patch correctly
The patch script patch_chrome_client.py is in ~/Downloads. Run it and verify:
cp ~/Downloads/patch_chrome_client.py . && python3 patch_chrome_client.py
grep -n "WebChromeClient" app/src/main/java/com/geeksville/mesh/convoy/ConvoyScreen.kt

Step 4 — Build, install, check JS console
./gradlew assembleGoogleDebug 2>&1 | grep -E "^e:|BUILD"
adb -s 8624SBCEDF00001789 install -r -d app/build/outputs/apk/google/debug/app-google-debug.apk
adb -s 8624SBCEDF00001789 logcat | grep -E "ConvoyJS|TILE"
Toggle OFFLINE switch. Look for TILE REQUEST and TILE ERROR lines. This tells us if Leaflet is attempting the file:// URL and whether it succeeds or fails.

Step 5 — If still failing: local HTTP tile server
If Android 10+ blocks file:// access regardless of settings, the fallback is serving tiles via a local HTTP server (NanoHTTPD) on localhost:8888. This bypasses all file:// restrictions. NanoHTTPD is a single Java file — already used in some Meshtastic components.

6. Files Modified — Current State

7. Do NOT Touch
DO NOT clear app storage — this will delete master.cfg, downloaded tiles, stored ride configs, and all JSON data.
DO NOT change tile storage path again — filesDir is correct and required for file:// WebView access.
Tiles confirmed present: files/tiles/SAT/ on device 8624SBCEDF00001789. Do not re-download until offline rendering is confirmed working.

8. Quick Reference
# Check tiles exist
adb -s 8624SBCEDF00001789 shell run-as com.geeksville.mesh.google.debug ls files/tiles/SAT/10/189/

# Check offline switch URL log
adb -s 8624SBCEDF00001789 logcat | grep ConvoyOffline

# Check JS console (after WebChromeClient fix)
adb -s 8624SBCEDF00001789 logcat | grep -E "ConvoyJS|TILE"
Item | Value
Branch | feature/convoy-event-ride
Last known working | ~2:30 PM March 26 — before external storage patch

Install command | adb -s 8624SBCEDF00001789 install -r -d app/build/outputs/apk/google/debug/app-google-debug.apk
