=== GroupTrack_PlayStore_Checklist_v1.docx ===
GroupTrack V2.4
Google Play Store Implementation Checklist
April 23, 2026 | Branch: feature/convoy-event-ride
1. Package Rename
Change application identity from Meshtastic fork to independent GroupTrack app. One-time breaking change for testers (fresh install required). Tile and track storage in /sdcard/Documents/ is package-independent and survives the rename.



2. Google HYB Tile Source — ToS Violation
CRITICAL: Current HYB source uses Google Maps tile URLs directly without API key. Violates Google ToS. Must replace before Play Store submission.



3. Intent Filter Cleanup
Narrow intent filters before submission. Overly broad filters cause GroupTrack to appear as handler for files it cannot process.


4. MANAGE_EXTERNAL_STORAGE Permission
The scoped storage issue blocking track import on sideloaded builds is resolved by Play Store deployment. Android 14 enables the permission toggle for Play Store apps but greys it out for sideloaded apps.



5. Open Source Attribution
Legal requirement before public distribution. All open source components and tile providers must be attributed.


6. Play Console Setup
Steps to create the Play Store listing and submit the app for review.



7. Build Commands
Release build sequence for Play Store submission.


8. Risk Summary
Items that could delay or block Play Store approval.


9. Recommended Implementation Sequence
Order of operations to minimize risk and rework.

=== GroupTrack_PlayStore_DeploymentNotes_v1.docx ===
GroupTrack Play Store Deployment
Manifest & File Handling — Required Changes
Reference Document | April 22, 2026
1. MANAGE_EXTERNAL_STORAGE Permission
1.1 Current State
The permission android.permission.MANAGE_EXTERNAL_STORAGE is declared in AndroidManifest.xml. On sideloaded (non-Play Store) installs running Android 14, the permission toggle in Settings is greyed out and cannot be enabled by the user. This blocks File.listFiles() from seeing files created by other apps in shared directories like /sdcard/Documents/my_tracks/.
1.2 Play Store Behavior
When installed from Google Play Store, the MANAGE_EXTERNAL_STORAGE toggle becomes active in Settings. The user can grant the permission. Once granted, File.listFiles() returns ALL files in any directory regardless of which app created them. ContentResolver can read any file URI without per-file permission grants. This is the definitive fix for all track file visibility issues.
1.3 Google Play Review Requirements
Google reviews all apps requesting MANAGE_EXTERNAL_STORAGE. A declaration form must be submitted in Play Console explaining the use case. GroupTrack's justification:
Category: File manager / backup utility for outdoor navigation
Justification: GroupTrack manages offline map tiles stored in shared device storage for use during off-grid outdoor activities. The app also imports and exports GPS track files (GPX/KML) that may originate from other navigation apps (Gaia GPS, onX Offroad, Google Earth). Full file access is required to: (a) manage offline map tile storage across app updates and reinstalls, (b) import GPS track files created by other apps for route planning and reference, (c) export recorded tracks for sharing with other navigation platforms.
Comparable apps with this permission: Gaia GPS, onX Offroad, Avenza Maps, Locus Map, OsmAnd.
1.4 Implementation Steps
1.5 Code to Restore
The following check was removed because it was non-functional for sideloaded apps. Restore it for Play Store build:
Location: ConvoyMapViewerScreen.kt, inside TRACKS button clickable handler
Code: if (!android.os.Environment.isExternalStorageManager()) { val intent = android.content.Intent(android.provider.Settings.ACTION_MANAGE_APP_ALL_FILES_ACCESS_PERMISSION, android.net.Uri.parse("package:" + context.packageName)); context.startActivity(intent); return@clickable }
This check runs before showing the track panel. If permission is not granted, it opens the GroupTrack permission page in Settings. User toggles the switch once, returns to app, TRACKS panel opens normally from then on.
2. Intent Filter Changes for Play Store
2.1 Current Intent Filters (Too Broad)
The current AndroidManifest.xml includes intent filters for these MIME types:
2.2 Recommended Play Store Filters
Keep only the two specific MIME types. Remove the broad types that would make GroupTrack appear as a handler for unrelated files:
KEEP: application/vnd.google-earth.kml+xml
KEEP: application/gpx+xml
REMOVE: application/octet-stream
REMOVE: text/xml
REMOVE: application/xml
NOTE: Many file managers assign application/octet-stream to .gpx and .kml files because they don't recognize the specific MIME types. Removing octet-stream means those file managers won't offer GroupTrack as a handler. However, with MANAGE_EXTERNAL_STORAGE granted, the in-app track picker sees all files directly — the intent filter becomes a convenience, not the primary import path.
2.3 Manifest Changes Required
Replace the current 5 intent-filter blocks with 2 specific ones:
Intent filter 1: action=VIEW, category=DEFAULT, scheme=content, mimeType=application/vnd.google-earth.kml+xml
Intent filter 2: action=VIEW, category=DEFAULT, scheme=content, mimeType=application/gpx+xml
3. First-Launch Permission Flow
3.1 User Experience
Step 1: User installs GroupTrack from Play Store and launches the app.
Step 2: On first launch (or first tap of TRACKS button), a dialog appears:
Title: File Access Required
Message: GroupTrack needs access to your files to manage offline maps and import GPS tracks from other apps like Gaia and onX. This is a one-time setup.
Buttons: [CONTINUE] [NOT NOW]
Step 3: CONTINUE opens Settings directly on GroupTrack's 'All files access' page.
Step 4: User toggles the switch ON.
Step 5: User presses Back to return to GroupTrack.
Step 6: SharedPreferences flag set — dialog never shows again.
3.2 Implementation
4. Package Name Migration
4.1 Current vs Future
Current package: com.geeksville.mesh (inherited from Meshtastic base)
Future package: com.grouptrack.android (GroupTrack branding)
4.2 Impact on Intent Filters
Intent filters are declared in AndroidManifest.xml and are tied to the package name automatically. When the package changes to com.grouptrack.android, the filters carry over — no code changes needed. However:
1. Users who set GroupTrack as the default handler for KML/GPX files will need to re-select it because Android associates default handlers with package names.
2. The MANAGE_EXTERNAL_STORAGE permission must be re-granted because it is also tied to the package name.
3. The SharedPreferences flag for the permission dialog will reset (new package = new app data directory), so the first-launch dialog will appear again. This is correct behavior.
4.3 Impact on File Storage
Tile storage: /sdcard/Documents/GroupTrack/maps/tiles/ — NO IMPACT. This is a public directory, not tied to any package name. All tiles survive the package rename.
Track storage: /sdcard/Documents/my_tracks/ — NO IMPACT. Same reason. All tracks survive.
App preferences: SharedPreferences are in the package-specific app data directory — WILL RESET. User will need to re-grant file access permission. Session/login data will clear.
Tile ownership: With MANAGE_EXTERNAL_STORAGE granted on the new package, File.listFiles() sees all tiles and tracks immediately. No migration needed.
5. Play Console Submission Checklist
6. Summary
The scoped storage / file access issue that has blocked reliable track import during V2.4 development is a direct consequence of sideloading. Play Store deployment with MANAGE_EXTERNAL_STORAGE is the designed solution. Google accepts this permission for navigation and mapping apps. The implementation is straightforward: restore the permission check, add a first-launch dialog, narrow the intent filters, and submit the declaration form. No architectural changes needed — the code is already written and tested, just awaiting the permission grant that Play Store enables.
GroupTrack | Play Store Deployment Notes | April 22, 2026
Step | Action | File
1 | Permission already declared — no change needed | AndroidManifest.xml
2 | Restore isExternalStorageManager() check on TRACKS button tap | ConvoyMapViewerScreen.kt
3 | Add first-launch permission dialog explaining why file access is needed | MainActivity.kt or Main.kt
4 | Use ACTION_MANAGE_APP_ALL_FILES_ACCESS_PERMISSION intent to go directly to GroupTrack's permission page | ConvoyMapViewerScreen.kt
5 | Set SharedPreferences flag after permission granted — never prompt again | ConvoyMapViewerScreen.kt
6 | Submit declaration form in Play Console under App Content > Sensitive Permissions | Play Console
MIME Type | Purpose | Play Store Risk
application/vnd.google-earth.kml+xml | KML files (Google Earth format) | SAFE — specific to KML
application/gpx+xml | GPX files (GPS exchange format) | SAFE — specific to GPX
application/octet-stream | Generic binary — matches ANY unknown file type | REMOVE — too broad, may trigger Play Store rejection
text/xml | Generic XML — matches any XML file | REMOVE — too broad
application/xml | Generic XML — matches any XML file | REMOVE — too broad
Component | Change | Details
SharedPreferences | Add 'files_permission_prompted' boolean flag | Checked before showing dialog
Dialog | AlertDialog in MainActivity.kt onCreate or Main.kt composable | Shows only when flag is false AND isExternalStorageManager() returns false
Intent | ACTION_MANAGE_APP_ALL_FILES_ACCESS_PERMISSION with package URI | Opens directly to GroupTrack's toggle, not the generic list
Resume check | onResume or LaunchedEffect checks if permission was granted | Proceeds to normal app flow if granted
Step | Action | Notes
1 | Create Play Console developer account | $25 one-time fee, Google account required
2 | Create app listing for GroupTrack | Title, description, screenshots, feature graphic
3 | Set content rating | Complete IARC questionnaire
4 | Set target audience and content | Outdoor navigation, all ages
5 | Declare MANAGE_EXTERNAL_STORAGE usage | App Content > Sensitive Permissions > All files access declaration form
6 | Narrow intent filters | Remove octet-stream, text/xml, application/xml from manifest before building release
7 | Restore permission check code | Add back isExternalStorageManager() check and first-launch dialog
8 | Build signed release APK with grouptrack-release-key.jks | Keystore stored in 4 locations — see session handoff docs
9 | Upload AAB (Android App Bundle) to Play Console | Play Console prefers AAB over APK for distribution
10 | Create closed beta track | Internal testing first, then closed beta with tester emails
11 | Submit for review | Review takes 1-7 days for new apps, especially with sensitive permissions
12 | After approval: promote to open beta or production | Tester feedback period before public launch

=== GroupTrack_Product_Roadmap_V9.docx ===
GroupTrack
Product Roadmap V9
Last Updated: March 25, 2026  |  Author: Fred Kix  |  Repo: kixazz25/Meshtastic-Android


MISSION AND GO-TO-MARKET
Value Proposition
GroupTrack provides safe ride awareness and ride awareness notifications to off-road vehicle riders. Powered by Meshtastic mesh radio — no cellular required during the ride. Coordination, route publishing, and rider networking managed through the GroupTrack platform.

Go-To-Market Strategy
Phase 1 — Android Google Play: Release to maximum riders. Build the community base. Early adopters create noise.
Phase 2 — iPad App Store: Port V2.4 sealed build to iOS via external developer. Parallel workstream. Standalone personal edition — no backend dependency.
Phase 3 — Organizations: Rider base creates organizational demand. Organizations pay to reach that audience.
Phase 4 — Architecture: Evaluate React Native cross-platform port at V4.0 before committing to full iOS rewrite.


REVENUE MODEL
Individual Subscription — One Tier, All Features


Organization Services

Advertising Revenue
Third revenue stream activated when rider base reaches meaningful scale. Non-intrusive placement only — never on safety-critical screens (convoy HUD, radio config, GPS recording). Placement on browse, enrollment, route library, and post-ride summary screens.


PLATFORM ARCHITECTURE
Technology Layers

iPad Strategy
V2.4 sealed build is the iOS port source — minimum viable field product with offline maps.
External iOS developer engaged after V2.4 seal. Works from GitHub V2.4 branch. Fixed scope — no moving target.
iOS personal edition = standalone app, no backend, one-time purchase or $3.00/month subscription.
When V3.0 backend launches, iOS app connects to same AWS API as Android.
Apple Developer Program enrollment required — $99/year — begin enrollment during V2.4 development.
React Native evaluation at V4.0 determines whether V5.0 is a native Swift rewrite or cross-platform port.


RELEASE SEQUENCE

V2.3 — SEALED  |  March 25, 2026



V2.4 — SIMPLE OFFLINE MAPS  |  IN PROGRESS

Seal Criteria
Draw bounding box on live map via Leaflet rectangle tool
Tile count and MB estimated — blocked if over 500MB ceiling
Tiles downloaded to device local storage from tile providers
Leaflet serves from local storage with WiFi off
All 4 tile types (SAT/HYB/TOPO/RD) verified offline at z10-z18
Zero open items — clean enough to hand to external iOS developer

Required Items

Backlog from V2.3

V2.4-iOS — iPAD PERSONAL EDITION  |  PARALLEL

Scope — What Is Included
Full GroupTrack convoy HUD — GROUP and MY CART modes
Offline map download — draw area, download tiles, serve offline
All 4 map tile types — SAT, HYB, TOPO, RD
GPS track recording
Single-boot radio config via Meshtastic-iOS BLE
GroupTrack branding — splash, icons, labels
Manual radio pairing — same as Android V2.4

Scope — What Is NOT Included
No backend — no accounts, no AWS, no subscriptions in personal edition
No ride creation or invite distribution
No follow relationships
No organizational features
No map region management beyond local device

Technical Requirements

V3.0 — BACKEND + BILLING + RIDE DISTRIBUTION  |  PLANNED

Seal Criteria
AWS EC2 live, PHP API serving, RDS connected
Google Sign-In creates user in RDS
Google Play $3.00/month subscription validates access
Apple IAP $3.00/month subscription validates access
Ride creation writes to RDS
Invite deep link working — rider taps link, ride downloaded
Public ride browse within 50-mile radius working
Private rides visible only to invited riders
Follow organizer and organization working
AWS SES automated ride publication email working
Online/offline gate enforced
Zero open items

Data Model — Key Design Decisions
One subscription tier — $3.00/month. is_active + expires_at only access control.
User has no fixed role — same person can be organizer on ride A, rider on ride B.
Role lives in enrollments.role ENUM(ORGANIZER, RIDER) per ride per user.
Public rides — visible to all active subscribers within 50-mile radius.
Private rides — invisible in all browse/follow feeds. Invite token required.
Follow organizer — see that organizer's PUBLIC rides. No access to private rides.
Follow organization — see org's PUBLIC rides within radius.
Organizer rider history — derived from enrollments table. No separate table.

Prerequisites

RDS Schema

Subscription Billing

PHP REST API

AWS SES Email Distribution

V4.0 — FULL MAP DISTRIBUTION + ARCHITECTURE REVIEW  |  PLANNED

Seal Criteria
Organizer draws map area, bounds saved to RDS rides table
AWS Level 1 dedup — only net new tiles downloaded from providers to AWS
Rider Level 2 dedup — only tiles not on device downloaded from AWS
Visual dedup overlay — green=AWS cached, blue=locally cached
Offline map works during ride — no internet
React Native architecture decision documented and approved
Zero open items

RDS Schema Additions

Level 1 Dedup — AWS Universal Registry
Every tile downloaded from any external provider lives on AWS exactly once. On new ride area submission:
calculateTiles(bounds, z10-z18) — full tile key list
SELECT tile_key FROM tile_registry WHERE tile_key IN (list) — find cached
net_new = requested - cached. Show organizer savings estimate.
Block if net_new estimated size > 500MB ceiling.
Download only net_new tiles from external providers to S3/EC2.
INSERT new tiles into tile_registry.
Build tile_manifest — all tile_keys for this ride.
Save manifest_id to rides table. Set map_status = READY.

Level 2 Dedup — Per-User Per-Ride
On ride enrollment map download:
Fetch tile_manifest from API.
Query local_tile_cache — tiles already on device.
delta = manifest - local_cached.
Show rider: X MB to download, Y MB already on device.
Rider confirms. TileSyncWorker starts.
Download delta from AWS in parallel — 4 threads.
Write to local storage. INSERT into local_tile_cache.
Update user_ride_sync = COMPLETE. Release screen.

TileSyncWorker

Visual Dedup Overlay

React Native Architecture Evaluation

GroupTrack  |  Product Roadmap V9  |  March 25, 2026  |  Fred Kix  |  kixazz25/Meshtastic-Android  |  visit www.grouptrack.org
GroupTrack sells safe ride awareness and ride awareness notifications. Revenue from individual subscriptions, organizational services, and advertising. Goal: maximize rider base on Android to create organizational demand, then expand to iPad via iOS port of V2.4.

=== GroupTrack_RideState_PhaseB_Plan (1).docx ===
GroupTrack
RideState — Rules, Touchpoints, and Phase B Implementation Plan
March 30, 2026  |  Branch: feature/convoy-event-ride  |  Phase B Thursday
1. Overview
RideState is a computed enum in ConvoyViewModel that drives three aspects of app behavior: map display, survey trigger, and public track repository eligibility. It is computed every tick from two inputs: the active node count on the mesh, and whether a formal ride record exists that matches the current radio channel name and today's date.
RideState controls behavior through conditional checks at specific touchpoints. It does not replace any existing V2.4 logic — it gates new V3.0 behavior alongside the existing code. V2.4 behavior is the default in all states.

GroupTrack
Session Closeout — April 10, 2026  |  v2

1. Build Completed Today — April 10

3. V3.0 Map Functions — Complete Definition
Full cradle-to-grave definition of GroupTrack V3.0 map architecture, asset types, display rules, trail smoothing, and data model. Captured April 10, 2026.

3.1 Map Architecture — Two Separate Maps

3.2 Asset Types

3.3 Track Storage Rules

RULE: All tracks stored in mytracks/ or subfolders — no exceptions. File picker scans Downloads only, GPX and KML files only.

3.4 Bounding Box Auto-Calculation

3.5 Ride Creation — Hard Gates
RULE: A ride CANNOT be saved without BOTH: (1) map boundary AND (2) trailhead pin.


NOTE: SAVE button shows MAP AREA + TRAILHEAD REQUIRED until both gates satisfied.

3.6 Pending Ride

3.7 Tile + Trail Geometry Download Pipeline
Every region download pulls six asset types:


NOTE: Trail geometry cached on EC2 by bounding box — 30 day TTL. Delivered to device at download time. User layer preference controls which tile layers download. Trail geometry always downloaded when tile download runs.

3.8 Trail Geometry Sources

NOTE: EC2 queries both ArcGIS USA Trails and USFS MVUM for ride bounding box. Merges into single GeoJSON. Delivered to device as trails/{bound_hash}.geojson.

3.9 Real-Time Trail Smoothing — Lead Cart Track
Real-time algorithm applied during the ride that snaps the lead cart's incoming GPS points to pre-downloaded trail geometry. Produces smooth trail-following tracks on all carts' maps even at 4-5 second GPS intervals.


Code Touchpoints — Real-Time Smoothing
New file: ConvoyTrailSnapper.kt
object ConvoyTrailSnapper {
    fun snapPoint(rawPoint: LatLon, trailGeometry: GeoJSON, toleranceFeet: Float): LatLon
    fun interpolateSegment(from: LatLon, to: LatLon, trail: GeoJSON): List<LatLon>
    fun loadTrailGeometry(context: Context, boundHash: String): GeoJSON?
}

Modified: ConvoyViewModel.kt — between GPS point receipt and track rendering:
// NEW — snap incoming lead cart point to trail geometry
val snappedPoint = ConvoyTrailSnapper.snapPoint(newLeadPosition, trailGeometry, 50f)
val segment = ConvoyTrailSnapper.interpolateSegment(lastSnapped, snappedPoint, trail)
trackPoints.addAll(segment)

Modified: ConvoyViewModel.startDownload() — add trail geometry download step:
// After tile downloads complete:
ConvoyTrailGeometry.downloadForBounds(context, north, south, east, west)

Modified: convoy_map.html — trail geometry rendered as Leaflet GeoJSON layer:
var trailLayer = L.geoJSON(trailGeojson, {
    style: { color: '#6B4EFF', weight: 1.5, opacity: 0.4, dashArray: '4,4' }
}).addTo(map);

3.10 Post-Ride Track Smoothing (Planning Map)
Separate from real-time smoothing — user-initiated from Track Detail screen or offered at save time. Applies same snap-to-trail algorithm to a completed recorded track.


3.11 Layer Panel — Accordion Display

Accordion — one section open at a time
No preferences stored — always opens with defaults, all sections collapsed
Active ride route always visible — cannot be toggled off
TRAIL NETWORK toggle OFF by default — user enables to see trail geometry overlay

3.12 Viewport-Driven Display — Hard Rule
RULE: Only assets whose geometry intersects the current map viewport are shown. List and map always in sync.


3.13 Layer Z-Order (Bottom to Top)

3.14 RDS Schema — Map Assets

3.15 EC2 API Endpoints — Map Functions

Rule | Detail
Paywall philosophy | Activates when channel saturation warrants it — not at launch. All signed-in users get full access at V3 launch.
Free ride channel | Free users get default convoy channel + PSK. No custom channel assignment.
Premium ride channel | Premium users can assign custom channel name and encryption key.
Offline maps are premium | Auto-download on ride accept and track import is a premium feature. Free users get NET-only maps.
Email links global rule | Every email address displayed anywhere in the app is a tappable mailto link launching device email app.
Follow state global rule | Follow state is single source of truth — toggling on any screen updates ALL screens immediately.
Unfollow removes notifications | Unfollow removes unread notifications and pending invite notifications from that organizer.
Self-follow protection | Follow checkbox hidden if organizer_id == current user_id — everywhere, no exceptions.
Section | Rule
Notifications | Organizer only. One card per organized ride. Future dates only. Collapsible accordion.
Notifications content | PUBLIC/PRIVATE badge, ride status badge, location, arrive/depart time, description, REPLIES nested accordion.
REPLIES accordion | One twistie collapses all 4 status types. Individual twisties for ACCEPTED/MAYBE/DECLINED/INVITED. Rider name/callsign/vehicle in each.
Pending Invites | Collapsible accordion. PUBLIC/PRIVATE badge, ride status, location, arrive/depart, description, follow checkbox, status buttons.
My Rides | Collapsible accordion. Badges, location, follow (hide if self), description, enrollment count for organized rides.
Rides Near Me | PUBLIC open enrollment only. No invite-required rides. No private rides. No enrolled/invited/declined rides. Filter by zip + radius.
Ride status badges | OPEN=green, CLOSED=orange, CANCELLED=red, PENDING=grey, COMPLETED=grey.
Tap any card | Navigates to Ride Detail — all status changes happen in Ride Detail, not inline on dashboard.
Exception | Pending Invites keeps inline ACCEPT/MAYBE/DECLINE buttons as quick-action shortcuts.
Rule | Detail
Who sees it | Organizer only — hidden for non-organizers
Content | Ride name, PUBLIC/PRIVATE badge, ride status badge, location (City ST · Trailhead), arrive time, depart time, description, REPLIES accordion
REPLIES | Collapsed by default. Tap to expand. Shows ACCEPTED/MAYBE/DECLINED/INVITED twisties. Only shows statuses with count > 0.
Rider detail | Name, callsign, vehicle type per rider in each status list
Tap card | Navigates to Ride Detail
Past rides | Not shown — future dates only
Cancelled rides | Not shown — removed immediately
From | To | Actions
INVITED | ACCEPTED | PATCH enrollment, queue map download, add calendar entry, notify organizer, send confirmation email
INVITED | MAYBE | PATCH enrollment, notify organizer
INVITED | DECLINED | PATCH enrollment, notify organizer, remove from Pending Invites
ACCEPTED | MAYBE | PATCH enrollment, remove calendar entry, notify organizer
ACCEPTED | DECLINED | PATCH enrollment, remove calendar entry, notify organizer
MAYBE | ACCEPTED | PATCH enrollment, trigger map download if needed, add calendar entry, notify organizer, send email
MAYBE | DECLINED | PATCH enrollment, notify organizer
DECLINED | ACCEPTED | PATCH enrollment, trigger map download, add calendar entry, notify organizer, send email
DECLINED | MAYBE | PATCH enrollment, notify organizer
Action | Triggers
FOLLOW | POST /follows, notify organizer: X started following you, send welcome email to user, add to broadcast list
UNFOLLOW | DELETE /follows, remove unread notifications from organizer, remove pending invite notifications, remove from broadcast list
Follow checkbox | Appears everywhere organizer name appears — hidden if organizer == current user
Global state | followedOrganizers in ViewModel — single source of truth, persisted to SharedPreferences
Interface | Direction | Detail

=== GroupTrack_SessionHandoff_Apr15_v1.docx ===
GroupTrack
Session Handoff — April 15, 2026
Branch: feature/convoy-event-ride  |  Commit: 17fb7635e

1. Completed This Session

All changes are in ConvoyViewModel.kt. Committed as a single atomic change.

1.1  Stale Rebroadcast Position Rejection
Meshtastic mesh nodes relay position packets from other nodes. These relayed packets carry the original GPS fix timestamp (pos.time, Unix seconds) from when the node was at a prior location. The system was accepting any non-zero lat/lon without checking the fix age, causing the lead track to draw lines back to stale locations mid-ride.

Fix: rideStartTimeMs is recorded when startGroupTrack() is called. In readLiveNodes(), any position whose pos.time predates rideStartTimeMs is rejected and falls back to lastKnownPosition (last valid fix from this ride). All rejections are logged as STALE POS REJECTED with node name, fix age in seconds, and coordinates.

1.2  Home Line Fix
lastKnownPosition, lastNodePositions, lastLeadLat, and lastLeadLon accumulated during pre-ride ticks from the Meshtastic node database, which stores the last known position from any prior session. On the first tick after startGroupTrack(), a segment drew from home coordinates to the current field position.

Fix: all four position maps/variables are cleared in startGroupTrack() and stopGroupTrack(). Pre-ride home coordinates can no longer seed the track.

1.3  Segment Jump Guard
When a node dropped GPS for one tick and returned with a stale or bad fix, the segment drawing code had no distance check and drew a line regardless of distance. Both the lead-only and multi-track drawing paths now reject any segment exceeding 0.25 miles (the distance covered at 30 mph in one second). Rejections logged as LEAD/MULTI SEGMENT JUMP REJECTED with distance and coordinates.

1.4  Altitude Meters to Feet
pos.altitude from the Meshtastic proto is in meters. Display was showing raw meter values. Fix: multiplied by 3.28084f and cast to Int. Verified at St. George, UT elevation (~5,500 ft displayed as 4,924 ft — consistent with actual radio-reported altitude).

1.5  Computed Speed (60-Second Window)
The radio's ground_speed field was noisy at 5-second broadcast intervals, producing readings of 98-120 mph at actual speeds of 30-35 mph. Replaced with a 60-second fixed window haversine calculation:

On first position seen for a node, open a window (store startMs, startLat, startLon)
Every tick, if 60 seconds have elapsed: speed_mph = haversineMiles(start, current) * 60
Reset window to current position after each calculation
Hold last computed speed between calculations
Falls back to 0f until first 60-second window completes

At 30 mph convoy speed, maximum error is approximately 10% (one position broadcast interval). Appropriate for convoy situational awareness.

1.6  Diagnostic Logging
All rejection paths log via convoyLog() for field diagnosis. Filter logcat on tag ConvoyLog. Watch for:


2. Build Information


3. Friday Field Test — April 18

This build is the primary test target for Friday. Key things to verify:

Lead track draws clean — no lines back to home or ride origin
No jump lines mid-route
Altitude reads correctly in feet
Speed reads accurately after first 60-second window warms up
Check logcat for STALE POS REJECTED hits — confirms rebroadcast theory

Radio hardware issue (Seeed devices) is unrelated to convoy software — awaiting Seeed technical support response. Do not spend time on radio hardware before Friday.

4. V3 Pickup Point — Next Session

All v2.4 track fixes are also present in the V3 build (same ViewModel). V3 open items from April 9 handoff:


5. Boarded for Next Build

5.1  Lead Cart 5-Point GPS Batch Packet
Design: lead cart only broadcasts a packet containing the last 5 GPS positions (1 per second) in a single mesh packet. Other carts unpack and draw 5 segments per update instead of 1. Smooths the lead track polyline and reduces the visual impact of any single bad fix. Not yet implemented — ready for next V2.4 sprint.

Session Handoff — April 22, 2026 (End of Day)
Branch: feature/convoy-event-ride
1. All Commits — April 21-22 Sessions
2. Current Build State
3. Features Completed — Both Sessions
3.1 Map System
3.2 Track System
4. CRITICAL — GPX/KML Import Authority Issue
4.1 The Problem
Android 14 scoped storage creates two distinct access control problems that block reliable track file import:
Problem A — File Listing: File.listFiles() on /sdcard/Documents/my_tracks/ only returns files created by the CURRENT app install. Files placed there by adb push, other apps (onX, Gaia), previous installs, or factory-reset recovery are invisible to listFiles(). This means the track picker panel only shows tracks the current install recorded — not imported files from external sources.
Problem B — File Reading via Intent: When a user taps a GPX/KML file in a file manager, Android sends an ACTION_VIEW intent to GroupTrack with a content:// URI. However, reading that URI requires the sending app to include FLAG_GRANT_READ_URI_PERMISSION. Some file managers do this; some don't. When permission is not granted, ContentResolver.openInputStream() throws SecurityException: 'requires that you obtain access using ACTION_OPEN_DOCUMENT or related APIs'.
4.2 What Was Tested
4.3 Why Play Store Deployment Resolves This
The MANAGE_EXTERNAL_STORAGE permission is the definitive fix. This permission grants full read/write access to all files on the device, making File.listFiles() see everything regardless of which app created the file. Here is why it is currently blocked and why Play Store deployment unblocks it:
1. SIDELOADED APPS (current state): Android 14 disables the MANAGE_EXTERNAL_STORAGE toggle in Settings for apps installed via adb or APK file. The permission appears in the list but the switch is greyed out and cannot be changed. This is a Google security policy to prevent sideloaded apps from accessing all device files.
2. PLAY STORE APPS (target state): Apps installed from Google Play Store can request MANAGE_EXTERNAL_STORAGE. The toggle in Settings becomes active. The user can grant it. Once granted, File.listFiles() returns ALL files in any directory, and ContentResolver can read any file URI without per-file permission grants.
3. PLAY STORE REVIEW: Google reviews apps requesting MANAGE_EXTERNAL_STORAGE. GroupTrack has a legitimate use case: offline map tile management and GPS track file management for outdoor navigation. This is an accepted category — apps like Gaia GPS and onX Offroad have this same permission. The review should approve it.
4. IMPLEMENTATION: The permission is already declared in AndroidManifest.xml. The isExternalStorageManager() check is already in the code (currently removed because it was non-functional for sideloaded apps). Once installed from Play Store: restore the permission check on first launch, prompt user to grant in Settings, done forever.
4.4 Interim Workarounds (Before Play Store)
Until Play Store deployment, these workarounds are available:
Workaround 1 — Import via Intent: User taps a GPX/KML file in a file manager that properly grants read permission. The import handler reads via ContentResolver, copies to my_tracks, and the file appears in the track list. This works with some file managers (tested: Files by Google first attempt succeeded with 2-track import). May not work with all file managers.
Workaround 2 — Record in App: Any track recorded by GroupTrack is immediately visible in the track list. Recording works correctly in both KML and GPX format. GPX is the default.
Workaround 3 — Direct File Fallback: A patch has been prepared (not yet built) that adds a fallback to the import handler. When ContentResolver is denied, it extracts the filename from the URI and attempts a direct File.readText() from the known my_tracks directory. This may work for files the app has filesystem access to.
Workaround 4 — Factory Reset Limitation: After factory reset, all file ownership records are cleared. Files pushed via adb after reset are owned by shell, not the app. The import intent handler is the only way to bring these files into the app's visibility.
4.5 Recommendation
Do not invest further time in scoped storage workarounds. The current implementation is sufficient for development and testing. The real fix is Play Store deployment with MANAGE_EXTERNAL_STORAGE properly granted. Focus remaining V2.4 development effort on: moving download controls to Map Viewer, export redesign (which does NOT have the permission issue since it reads files the app owns), and building V2.4/V3.0 releases for tester deployment.

4. RECORD Button Crash — P1 DEBUG TOMORROW

The RECORD button crashes the app. This appeared after the April 8 fixes. The WebView gate (nodes.isNotEmpty()) was reverted but the crash persists.

4.1 What We Changed That Could Cause This

4.2 Debug Approach

4.3 Most Likely Fix
ConvoyGpsService declares foregroundServiceType=health but calls requestLocationUpdates() in startLocationUpdates(). Android 14+ requires the foreground service type to match the actual capability used. Changing type to health while still using location will throw a SecurityException or crash on startForeground().
Likely fix: change foregroundServiceType back to location AND keep FOREGROUND_SERVICE_LOCATION in manifest. The early prompt issue was from initDeviceLocation() in the HTML map — that is already fixed separately.

5. Maintenance Structure — Design (Next Session)

5.1 Problem 1 — Apply List Maintenance Screen

5.2 Problem 2 — Capture New Master

5.3 Problem 3 — Asset Change Tracking

6. Open Items — Priority Order

7. Architecture Decisions — Do Not Revisit

=== GroupTrack_SessionSummary_Apr18_v1.docx ===
GroupTrack
Session Summary — April 18, 2026
Today's Work + Sunday Plan + Monday Carryforward
v1  |  CONFIDENTIAL
1. Today's Session — What We Accomplished
Saturday's session advanced GroupTrack V3.0 from mostly-built-but-unverified to validated-infrastructure-with-one-clearly-scoped-blocker. The work split across three streams: planning alignment, documentation production, and V3 infrastructure validation.
1.1 Planning Alignment Crystallized
Multiple back-and-forth clarifications converged on a three-release model that treats V2.4, V2.5, and V3.0 as distinct products with clean handoff criteria between them:


Architectural principle established: Convoy Map and Planning Map are independent surfaces sharing only filesystem (tiles, routes, waypoints, tracks) — no shared UI code. This isolates V2.5 mapping development from the hard-won Convoy Map stability.
1.2 Documents Produced
1.3 Code Inspection — Reality vs Assumptions
Five files inspected in the repo. Findings substantially revised the v2 spec assumptions. V3 registration is closer to built-and-needs-validation than needs-building.


Takeaway: the V3 registration flow was built and wired but never end-to-end tested against live backend. Today's work validated the backend and fixed the one bug blocking actual registration.
1.4 Backend Validation
PHP API at grouptrack.org confirmed live via curl. Real user_id UUIDs returned from registration calls. The URL path pattern was identified — the API uses path-info routing:

Working URL pattern:
POST https://grouptrack.org/convoy_api.php/users/register

Response:
{"user_id":"7a2b4116-352d-4f4d-b342-2d439c5ad3fd","status":"created"}

1.5 Code Fix Applied — API_BASE_URL
Root cause of why registration would have 404'd even with OAuth working:

Applied via patch_v3_apibaseurl_v1.py. Single-line change to ConvoyConfig.kt. Every API endpoint in ConvoyApiClient.kt automatically inherits the correct URL structure.

=== GroupTrack_Session_Apr13_WorkflowRules.docx ===

1. Navigation Architecture


=== GroupTrack_StandaloneMode_Spec_v2.docx ===
GroupTrack V2.4
Standalone GPS Mode — Single Path Design
April 20, 2026  |  v2  |  Simplified Approach
1. Design Principle
One method. Every cart goes through the same process. There is no standalone mode, no Gaia mode, no special path. Whether you have 10 radios on a mesh or one phone with no radio, the flow is identical:

1. Device appears as a node.
2. User selects lead cart from the node list.
3. Lead cart GPS drives the track on the map.

The only variable is WHERE the GPS data comes from. If a radio is connected, GPS comes from the radio. If no radio is connected or the radio has no GPS fix, GPS comes from the phone. That decision is a single fallback in one place — the tick loop.

2. Problem Statement
Currently, when no radio is connected, readLiveNodes() returns an empty list. No nodes exist in convoyState. The lead selection dialog never appears because there are no nodes to select. The RECORD handler auto-assigns lead to nothing. The tick loop has no nodes to compute. No track draws.

The problem is not that we need a special standalone mode. The problem is that the device does not appear as a node unless a radio is connected. Fix that one thing and the entire existing flow works.

3. Resolution — Two Changes
3.1 Change 1: Device Is Always a Node
The Android device should always appear as a node in convoyState, whether or not a radio is connected. The device has a name (Build.MODEL or user-set name), it has GPS, it has battery. It is a cart.


In all three cases, the node appears in the list. The user selects lead. The track draws. One path.

3.2 Change 2: Phone GPS Fallback in Tick
In the tick loop, after readLiveNodes() runs, if any node has lat=0.0 and lon=0.0 (no radio GPS), substitute the phone GPS position from LocationManager.getLastKnownLocation(). This is one if-statement:

if (node.latitude == 0.0 && node.longitude == 0.0) -> use phone GPS

This handles three scenarios with zero additional code paths: no radio, radio with no GPS module, radio with temporary GPS dropout.

4. What Stays the Same


5. Files to Modify


Two changes in one file. No new files. No new screens. No new navigation. No new mode.

6. GPS Acquisition
The phone GPS is already active when ConvoyGpsService starts (triggered by RECORD). It calls LocationManager.requestLocationUpdates(GPS_PROVIDER, 2000, 1f). So by the time the tick loop needs a position, the LocationManager has a fresh fix available via getLastKnownLocation().

No new GPS initialization is needed. We just read what ConvoyGpsService already started.

7. Node Identity Without Radio


8. Test Plan


9. Priority
This enables Google Play users to evaluate GroupTrack without purchasing radio hardware. Download the app, record a track, view trails, assess value. Then decide to buy radios for group rides. This is the user acquisition funnel — free solo use drives radio purchases for convoy use.

GroupTrack Standalone GPS Mode | V2.4 | April 20, 2026 | Single Path Design
Condition | Node Source | GPS Source
Radio connected, GPS fix | Radio node from nodeRepository | Radio GPS (lat/lon from position packet)
Radio connected, no GPS fix | Radio node from nodeRepository | Phone GPS fallback (LocationManager)
No radio connected | Device node created from Build.MODEL | Phone GPS (LocationManager)
Component | Change? | Why
Lead selection dialog | None | Shows node list. 1 node = auto-assign. 2+ nodes = pick. Same logic.
RECORD handler | None | Checks meshNodes.size. Works with 1+ nodes. Already handles auto-assign.
ConvoyEngine.compute() | None | Receives node list. Tags lead/tail. Generates track segments. Works with 1 node.
Track drawing | None | drawTrack() renders whatever segments compute() generates.
GPS recording | None | ConvoyGpsService already records phone GPS to GPX independently.
Lead lock | None | setLeadCart() locks the nodeId. Works for any node.
File | Change
ConvoyViewModel.kt | In readLiveNodes(): if nodeRepository returns empty, create one ConvoyNode from Build.MODEL + phone GPS. Return it as the sole node.
ConvoyViewModel.kt | In tick(): after readLiveNodes(), for any node with lat=0.0/lon=0.0, substitute phone GPS from LocationManager.getLastKnownLocation().
Field | Value
nodeId | !phone (fixed string, unique to this device)
callsign | android.os.Build.MODEL (e.g. SM-G998B, Pixel 7)
battery_pct | Device battery level from BatteryManager
status | ACTIVE
lastHeardMs | System.currentTimeMillis()
latitude / longitude | Phone GPS via LocationManager
altitude_m | Phone GPS altitude
speed_mph | Phone GPS speed
heading_deg | Phone GPS bearing
Test | Steps | Expected
1 — No radio solo | Launch with no radio. Tap RECORD. Device node appears in list. Select it. Drive. | Lead locks. Track draws on map from phone GPS. GPX saved on END.
2 — Radio with GPS | Launch with radio. 2+ nodes. Tap RECORD. Select lead. | Normal convoy operation. Track from radio GPS. No change from current behavior.
3 — Radio no GPS fix | Connect radio with GPS disabled. Tap RECORD. | Node appears but lat/lon = 0. Phone GPS substitutes. Track draws.
4 — Radio disconnect mid-ride | Start ride with radio. Unplug radio. | readLiveNodes() returns empty. Device node takes over. Track continues from phone GPS.
5 — No GPS permission | Deny location permission. Tap RECORD. | Location permission dialog appears. Does not start recording.


=== GroupTrack_Strategic_Context.docx ===
GroupTrack — Strategic Context for Handoffs
The 20,000 foot view. Read this BEFORE the tactical handoff docs.
Purpose of This Document
Tactical handoffs answer "what do I do." This document answers "why are we doing it, and what is the world we are building toward." Without this view, a fresh Claude can execute instructions but cannot anticipate, refactor, or push back when a tactical request conflicts with strategic direction. This document gives that 20,000 foot view so each session's work fits into the trajectory rather than just executing the day's checklist.
What GroupTrack Actually Is
GroupTrack is an Android-first off-road convoy tracking application built on a forked Meshtastic codebase. The original purpose: real-time GPS position sharing across a group of riders using Meshtastic mesh radios, with no cell service required. The product was built around real off-road riding — Fred (the developer) rides with off-road groups and uses GroupTrack on actual trail rides like the St. George to Bar 10 Grand Canyon route. Every architectural decision can be traced back to a real convoy-riding use case.
As of V2.4 the product is repositioning from "convoy-tracking app for radio owners" to a dual-mode product: free standalone GPS tracker (no radio required) for solo riders, plus convoy capabilities when a Meshtastic radio is added.
The V2.4 Audience-Expansion Strategy
Before V2.4, GroupTrack required a Meshtastic radio purchase to be useful. That capped the audience at riders who already owned (or were willing to buy) Meshtastic hardware. Small audience, slow growth.
V2.4 introduces standalone mode: open the app with no radio, get full GPS tracking, offline maps, trail overlays, and track recording. Functionally comparable to Gaia GPS and onX, free at the entry point. Strategic intent:
Lower the activation threshold — anyone with an Android phone can use GroupTrack solo, no purchase, no commitment
Compete on the standalone GPS tracker dimension where Gaia/onX are dominant but charge $30–80/year
Convert solo users to convoy users naturally — once on the platform, riding with a group becomes a desirable next step, and the radio purchase becomes the upsell
Establish GroupTrack as the rider's daily-driver GPS app, not a niche tool used only on convoy days
This means tactical decisions about V2.4 polish — UI quality, first-impression risk, ANR-free operation, professional packaging — are NOT cosmetic. They are the conversion funnel. A solo rider's first GPS app session has to feel as good as Gaia's. Anything less and they uninstall and never see the convoy features.
Why the V2.4 Import Rewrite Matters Strategically
The system file picker ANR is not just a bug. It hits the exact moment that determines whether a solo rider becomes a returning user: their first import of existing tracks from Gaia or onX. If that flow ANRs, the rider concludes "this app is broken" and uninstalls. The picker rewrite is a conversion-funnel fix, not a polish item.
It also previews the V2.5 architecture. V2.5 makes the Map Viewer the central hub for ALL track management — display, import, export, email, file ops. Building a custom in-app file browser for V2.4 import puts the foundation in place for V2.5. Tomorrow's tactical work serves both today's release and the next quarter's architecture. That's why the right answer is custom in-app browser, not picker-with-better-error-handling.

=== GroupTrack_TrailArchitecture_v2.docx ===
GroupTrack
National Trail Data Architecture
Unified Spatial Database: Trails + Waypoints + Routes
May 2, 2026  |  V2.5 Architecture v2  |  Confidential
1. Design Principles
One model, one method, expand by adding data.
The national data model is built once. Utah is the first dataset loaded. Arizona, Nevada, Colorado, and every other state use the same schema, same API, same client code. Expanding coverage means adding data to the server — never changing the app. The app loads trails by map viewport boundaries. It does not know or care what state or source the data came from.

Trails are map data. They move with tiles.
When a user downloads map tiles for a region, trail data for that same region downloads too. On the trail with no cell service, everything is local. Delete a region’s tiles, the trails for that region become purgeable.

Regions, not states.
Riders don’t ride by state boundary. A southern Utah rider is more likely to ride northern Arizona or eastern Nevada than northern Utah. The download unit is a user-defined region — a rectangle drawn on the map, or a predefined riding area. States are how we organize data on the server. Regions are how riders think about their territory.

One local spatial database for everything.
The GeoPackage holds trails, waypoints, and planned routes in a single file. One query pattern (bounding box), one backup path (cloud sync in V3.0), one file to manage. Waypoints and routes are first-class spatial data alongside trails.

Two-way data flow: server down, user up.
Federal and state trail sources feed the server. Users import locally-sourced trail data (GPX/KML from riding groups, local clubs, personal recordings). User-contributed data replicates up to the master database for other riders. The model grows from both directions.

2. Two-Tier Download Strategy
2.1 Tier 1 — Region Overview (z14)
The user defines a region by drawing a rectangle on the map or selecting a predefined riding area. The app downloads z14 tiles for that rectangle plus all trail data within those boundaries. z14 gives enough detail to see trail networks, identify riding areas, and plan rides. Tile storage is minimal at z14 — roughly 60–80 tiles per 10x10 mile area per source.
This is the discovery layer. The user sees the trail network, pans around, finds where they want to ride. Trail data is fully detailed at every zoom — only the tile resolution is low. You can zoom in on trails at z14 and see every turn; the satellite/topo underneath just gets blurry.
2.2 Tier 2 — Ride Detail (z15–18)
The user draws a smaller rectangle around a specific ride area and downloads z15–18 tiles. Trail data is already local from the Tier 1 download — no additional trail download needed. This is the riding layer: detailed satellite or topo imagery with trails overlaid.
Planning happens online where the user can zoom freely. The Tier 2 download captures the detail they need for offline riding. The ride area might be 10x10 miles around a specific trail system.
2.3 Download Math

3. Unified Local Database — GeoPackage
3.1 Why GeoPackage
GeoPackage (.gpkg) is an OGC international standard. It is a SQLite database with a defined schema for geospatial features. It is what Gaia GPS, onX, QGIS, ArcGIS, and government agencies use. Android has native SQLite support. The NGA (National Geospatial-Intelligence Agency) maintains an open-source Android library. One file, self-contained, spatial indexes built in.
Location: /sdcard/Documents/GroupTrack/maps/grouptrack.gpkg
Note: single database named grouptrack.gpkg, not trails.gpkg. This file holds all spatial data: trails, waypoints, and routes.
3.2 Database Schema
trails
Every trail segment from every source. Lazy-loaded by viewport bounds.
id              INTEGER PRIMARY KEY AUTOINCREMENT
source_id       TEXT NOT NULL        -- 'USFS', 'BLM', 'UGRC', 'UT_STATE', 'USER'
source_trail_id TEXT                 -- original ID from upstream (NULL for user imports)
name            TEXT
trail_type      TEXT                 -- ohv, atv, motorcycle, hiking, mixed
difficulty      TEXT                 -- easy, moderate, difficult, extreme
surface         TEXT                 -- dirt, rock, sand, gravel, paved
vehicle_class   TEXT                 -- atv, utv, motorcycle, full-size, mixed
distance_miles  REAL
geometry        BLOB NOT NULL        -- WKB with envelope (GeoPackage standard)
min_lat         REAL NOT NULL
max_lat         REAL NOT NULL
min_lon         REAL NOT NULL
max_lon         REAL NOT NULL
contributed_by  TEXT                 -- NULL=server, user_id for crowd-sourced
replicated      INTEGER DEFAULT 0    -- 0=local only, 1=synced to server
imported_at     TEXT

waypoints
User-created points of interest. Persist across sessions. Display on both maps.
id              INTEGER PRIMARY KEY AUTOINCREMENT
name            TEXT NOT NULL
description     TEXT
category        TEXT NOT NULL        -- trailhead, staging, hazard, poi, camp, fuel, water
latitude        REAL NOT NULL
longitude       REAL NOT NULL
altitude_m      REAL
icon            TEXT                 -- icon name for map display
created_at      TEXT
modified_at     TEXT
contributed_by  TEXT                 -- NULL=local, user_id for shared
replicated      INTEGER DEFAULT 0

routes
Planned routes. Sequence of waypoints connected by trail segments or free-drawn lines. Local to the device. Transferable to other riders in V3.0 ride definitions.
id              INTEGER PRIMARY KEY AUTOINCREMENT
name            TEXT NOT NULL
description     TEXT
route_type      TEXT                 -- loop, out_and_back, point_to_point
total_miles     REAL
geometry        BLOB                 -- WKB LineString of the full route path
min_lat         REAL
max_lat         REAL
min_lon         REAL
max_lon         REAL
waypoint_ids    TEXT                 -- comma-separated waypoint IDs in order
created_at      TEXT
modified_at     TEXT
shared          INTEGER DEFAULT 0    -- 0=local, 1=available for ride definitions

route_waypoints
Junction table: ordered waypoints within a route.
route_id        INTEGER NOT NULL
waypoint_id     INTEGER NOT NULL
sequence        INTEGER NOT NULL     -- order within route
PRIMARY KEY (route_id, sequence)

download_regions
Tracks what areas have been downloaded. Links trail lifecycle to tile lifecycle.
id              INTEGER PRIMARY KEY AUTOINCREMENT
name            TEXT                 -- 'Sand Hollow Area', 'SW Utah Riding'
tier            INTEGER NOT NULL     -- 1=overview z14, 2=ride detail z15-18
north           REAL NOT NULL
south           REAL NOT NULL
east            REAL NOT NULL
west            REAL NOT NULL
trail_count     INTEGER
tile_sources    TEXT                 -- 'SAT,TOPO' or 'SAT,HYB'
zoom_levels     TEXT                 -- '14' or '15,16,17,18'
downloaded_at   TEXT
status          TEXT DEFAULT 'PROTECTED'  -- PROTECTED or PURGEABLE

sources_meta
Metadata about what data sources are on the device.
source_id       TEXT PRIMARY KEY
name            TEXT
agency          TEXT
trail_count     INTEGER
coverage_bbox   TEXT                 -- north,south,east,west
last_synced     TEXT

4. Server Architecture
4.1 National Trail Database (RDS MySQL)
The server aggregates all federal and state sources into a single national trail database. The client queries by bounding box and receives a GeoJSON response. The server does not know about states when serving data — it only knows coordinates. States are an organizational concept for ingestion, not for queries.

CREATE TABLE trail_sources (
  id VARCHAR(50) PRIMARY KEY,
  name VARCHAR(255) NOT NULL,
  agency VARCHAR(100),
  api_url TEXT,
  api_type VARCHAR(20),           -- arcgis_rest, geojson_file, shapefile
  coverage_state VARCHAR(2),      -- UT, AZ, CO (for ingestion organization)
  coverage_north DECIMAL(9,6),
  coverage_south DECIMAL(9,6),
  coverage_east DECIMAL(9,6),
  coverage_west DECIMAL(9,6),
  field_mapping JSON,             -- upstream field names to GroupTrack schema
  trail_types VARCHAR(255),
  record_count INT DEFAULT 0,
  last_fetched DATETIME,
  refresh_interval_days INT DEFAULT 30,
  status VARCHAR(20) DEFAULT 'active'
);

CREATE TABLE trail_segments (
  id INT AUTO_INCREMENT PRIMARY KEY,
  source_id VARCHAR(50) NOT NULL,
  source_trail_id VARCHAR(100),
  name VARCHAR(255),
  trail_type VARCHAR(50),
  difficulty VARCHAR(50),
  surface VARCHAR(50),
  vehicle_class VARCHAR(100),
  distance_miles DECIMAL(8,2),
  geometry_json LONGTEXT NOT NULL,
  min_lat DECIMAL(9,6) NOT NULL,
  max_lat DECIMAL(9,6) NOT NULL,
  min_lon DECIMAL(9,6) NOT NULL,
  max_lon DECIMAL(9,6) NOT NULL,
  contributed_by INT,              -- NULL=agency, user_id for crowd-sourced
  approved INTEGER DEFAULT 1,      -- 0=pending review, 1=approved
  source_updated_at DATETIME,
  imported_at DATETIME DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP,
  INDEX idx_bbox (min_lat, max_lat, min_lon, max_lon),
  INDEX idx_source (source_id),
  INDEX idx_type (trail_type),
  FOREIGN KEY (source_id) REFERENCES trail_sources(id)
);

CREATE TABLE user_contributions (
  id INT AUTO_INCREMENT PRIMARY KEY,
  user_id INT NOT NULL,
  contribution_type VARCHAR(20),   -- trail, waypoint
  trail_segment_id INT,
  geometry_json LONGTEXT,
  metadata JSON,                   -- name, type, difficulty, etc.
  status VARCHAR(20) DEFAULT 'pending',  -- pending, approved, rejected
  submitted_at DATETIME DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP,
  reviewed_at DATETIME,
  FOREIGN KEY (user_id) REFERENCES users(id)
);
4.2 Trail API
GET https://grouptrack.org/trails_api.php
Response: GeoJSON FeatureCollection. All trail segments whose bounding box intersects the requested rectangle. Properties include name, trail_type, difficulty, surface, vehicle_class, distance_miles, source_id.

POST https://grouptrack.org/trails_api.php?action=contribute
Accepts user-contributed trail data (GeoJSON). Requires authentication. Goes into user_contributions with status=pending. After approval, inserted into trail_segments with contributed_by set.

5. Data Sources — National Model
5.1 Federal Sources (National Coverage)
USFS is the primary source with 195,000+ miles of trails and Motor Vehicle Use Map (MVUM) designations indicating which vehicle types are permitted. BLM is critical for western states where BLM manages vast acreage with OHV travel management areas.
5.2 State Sources (Ingested per State)
5.3 User-Contributed Data (Crowd-Sourced)
Users import GPX/KML files from their own rides, local riding clubs, or third-party trail apps. The import flow:
This creates a virtuous cycle: the more people ride, the more accurate and complete the trail database becomes. Locally-sourced data from riding clubs fills gaps that federal sources miss — unofficial trails, connector roads, seasonal closures, condition reports.

6. Client Architecture
6.1 Replacing Current Trail Loading
Current state: A 22MB GeoJSON file (Utah UGRC) is read from assets on TRAILS button tap. All 5,732 trails load into the Leaflet WebView at once via evaluateJavascript(). This caused ANR on startup (fixed by deferring to button tap) and still loads everything regardless of viewport.
New state: The 5,732 UGRC trails are converted to the GeoPackage schema and stored in grouptrack.gpkg. On TRAILS tap, Kotlin queries the GeoPackage for only the trails visible in the current viewport. The WebView receives a small GeoJSON payload (typically 20–500 features instead of 5,732). As the user pans or zooms, new queries fire and the overlay updates.
The conversion is a one-time migration: parse the existing GeoJSON from assets, extract each Feature, calculate bounding box, insert into the trails table with source_id=UGRC. After migration, the 22MB asset file is removed from the APK.
6.2 Lazy Loading Flow
Same flow for both Convoy Map and Map Viewer. Same ConvoyTrailManager serves both. Same overlay rendering code in the HTML maps.
6.3 Waypoint Display
Waypoints query from the same GeoPackage using a simple bounding box check on latitude/longitude. Rendered as Leaflet markers with category-specific icons. Tap for info popup with name, description, and actions (edit, delete, navigate-to, share).
6.4 Route Display
Routes render as a Leaflet polyline from the stored WKB geometry. Waypoints along the route render as sequenced markers. Tap route for info popup with distance, waypoint list, and actions (start navigation, export GPX, share).
6.5 Download Integration
The existing tile download UI gains a toggle: Download trails with tiles (default ON). When the user draws a download rectangle:
No new UI required for trail download — it piggybacks on the existing tile download flow. The only addition is the toggle and a line in the progress display.

7. Storage Management
7.1 Trails Follow Tiles
Download regions track both tile and trail areas. When a user deletes a tile region, the system checks whether trails in that area are still covered by another region. If not, trail records become PURGEABLE and can be cleaned up in a background pass.
In practice, trail data is tiny relative to tiles. A full state of trails is 15–30MB. A ride area of z15–18 tiles is 200MB–1GB. The storage cost is always tiles, never trails. Users will rarely need to purge trail data.
7.2 Storage Estimates
8. Trail Filtering & Display
Trail type filter is a WHERE clause on the GeoPackage query. Instant, no re-download. Filter controls in the TRAILS panel alongside the visibility toggle.
Tap any trail for info popup: name, type, difficulty, surface, distance, source. Actions: export GPX, share, add to route, report issue.

11. Implementation Phases
Phases 1–4 are the immediate priority: replace the current 22MB load with the GeoPackage lazy loader. This can ship as a V2.5.0 release. Phases 5–10 build out the full feature set. Phases 11–13 enable crowd-sourcing and national expansion.

=== GroupTrack_User_Guide_V2.4 (1).docx ===
GroupTrack
User Guide — Version 2.4
March 2026  |  Android  |  For Authorized Testers
GroupTrack is a custom Android app for coordinating multi-vehicle group rides without cellular or internet connectivity. It uses Meshtastic mesh radio hardware to share GPS positions between all vehicles in real time. No phone signal required. No server. No manual radio configuration.

1. USER ROLES

2. SPLASH SCREEN
When GroupTrack launches for the first time or after restart, a branded splash screen displays while the app initializes and waits for mesh nodes. The splash shows the GroupTrack logo, UTV desert background, developer credit, and website.
The splash automatically dismisses when the first mesh node is detected or after a brief initialization period.

3. MAIN MAP SCREEN
The main screen shows a live map with all convoy vehicles plotted as labeled markers. This is the primary operational screen during a ride.


MAP TYPES: SAT = Esri satellite imagery. HYB = Google satellite + roads hybrid. TOPO = Esri topographic with terrain. RD = Carto road map with points of interest.

4. BUTTON BAR

5. GROUP HUD PANEL
The GROUP HUD displays convoy-wide statistics as a transparent overlay on the left side of the map.


6. MY CART HUD PANEL

7. NODE HUD — INDIVIDUAL CART DETAIL
Tap any cart marker on the map to open the NODE detail panel for that vehicle.


8. MAP SETTINGS PANEL
Tap the map panel header (top right, showing MAP + source name) to expand the full settings panel. The header always shows the current tile source and ONLINE/LOCAL status.

8.1 Layer Selection
Four tile source buttons: SAT | HYB | TOPO | RD. Tap to switch. The active source is highlighted blue. Switching to an online source sets the indicator to ONLINE. Switching to offline mode serves tiles from local cache.

8.2 Download Zoom
Slider controlling the maximum zoom level for tile downloads. Range z16-z18. Higher zoom = more detail but more tiles and larger storage.

8.3 Fly Radius
Slider controlling how close the map zooms when flying to a search result. Range 1-10 miles. 10 miles gives a regional view for selecting a download zone. 1 mile gives a tight trail-level view.

8.4 Online / Offline Switch
Toggle between ONLINE (streaming tiles from internet) and LOCAL (serving tiles from device storage). Set to LOCAL before heading into the field. Set to ONLINE when back in connectivity.
The ONLINE/LOCAL indicator in the map header always reflects the current switch state: green = ONLINE, blue = LOCAL.

8.5 Location Search
Search for a named area before downloading map tiles. Type a location name (city, park, trail system, or region) and tap FIND AREA.


If the geocoder cannot find your location, try adding the state abbreviation (e.g. 'Sand Hollow UT'). For precise locations, raw coordinates are supported: 37.44, -113.25.

8.6 Download Region
Tap DOWNLOAD REGION to activate the rectangle draw tool on the map. Draw a box over the area you want to cache for offline use. When you release, a size estimation dialog appears.


8.7 Download Progress
During download, a progress chip appears above the button bar on the map screen showing tile count (X of Y tiles) and estimated time remaining. The gear panel is closed — you can continue using the map while tiles download.
A toast notification warns to keep the app open while downloading. When complete, a completion toast confirms the tile count downloaded.
Keep app open during download. Background download is not yet supported — closing the app will stop the download. Resume is automatic — tiles already on disk are skipped if you restart a download over the same area.

9. GROUPTRACK MENU
Tap the GroupTrack header at the top of the map to open the submenu. This is the main navigation hub for ride management and radio configuration.

EVENT / RIDE Menu Items

10. CREATE A RIDE (ORGANIZER)

8.4 Online / Offline Switch
Toggle between ONLINE (streaming tiles from internet) and LOCAL (serving tiles from device storage). Set to LOCAL before heading into the field. Set to ONLINE when back in connectivity.
The ONLINE/LOCAL indicator in the map header always reflects the current switch state: green = ONLINE, blue = LOCAL.

=== GroupTrack_User_Guide_V2_4_updated (1).docx ===
GroupTrack
User Guide — Version 2.4
March 2026  |  Android  |  For Authorized Testers
GroupTrack is a custom Android app for coordinating multi-vehicle group rides without cellular or internet connectivity. It uses Meshtastic mesh radio hardware to share GPS positions between all vehicles in real time. No phone signal required. No server. No manual radio configuration.
1. USER ROLES
2. SPLASH SCREEN
When GroupTrack launches for the first time or after restart, a branded splash screen displays while the app initializes and waits for mesh nodes. The splash shows the GroupTrack logo, UTV desert background, developer credit, and website. It automatically dismisses when the first mesh node is detected or after a brief initialization period.
3. MAIN MAP SCREEN
The main screen shows a live map with all convoy vehicles plotted as labeled markers. This is the primary operational screen during a ride.
NOTE: MAP TYPES: SAT = Esri satellite imagery. HYB = Google satellite + roads hybrid. TOPO = Esri topographic with terrain. RD = Carto road map with points of interest.
4. BUTTON BAR
5. GROUP HUD PANEL
The GROUP HUD displays convoy-wide statistics as a transparent overlay on the left side of the map.
6. MY CART HUD PANEL
7. NODE HUD — INDIVIDUAL CART DETAIL
Tap any cart marker on the map to open the NODE detail panel for that vehicle.
8. MAP SETTINGS PANEL
Tap the map panel header (top right, showing MAP + source name) to expand the full settings panel. The header always shows the current tile source and ONLINE/LOCAL status.
8.1 Layer Selection
Four tile source buttons: SAT | HYB | TOPO | RD. Tap to switch. The active source is highlighted blue. Switching to an online source sets the indicator to ONLINE. Switching to offline mode serves tiles from local cache.
8.2 Download Zoom
Slider controlling the maximum zoom level for tile downloads. Range z16-z18. Higher zoom = more detail but more tiles and larger storage.
8.3 Fly Radius
Slider controlling how close the map zooms when flying to a search result. Range 1-10 miles. 10 miles gives a regional view for selecting a download zone. 1 mile gives a tight trail-level view.
8.4 Online / Offline Switch
Toggle between ONLINE (streaming tiles from internet) and LOCAL (serving tiles from device storage). Set to LOCAL before heading into the field. Set to ONLINE when back in connectivity.
NOTE: The ONLINE/LOCAL indicator in the map header always reflects the current switch state: green = ONLINE, blue = LOCAL.
8.5 Location Search
Search for a named area before downloading map tiles. Type a location name (city, park, trail system, or region) and tap FIND AREA. The map flies to the search result and stays there — it will not snap back to your cart position while the search result is displayed.
NOTE: If the geocoder cannot find your location, try adding the state abbreviation (e.g. 'Sand Hollow UT'). For precise locations, raw coordinates are supported: 37.44, -113.25.
8.6 Download Region
Tap DOWNLOAD REGION to activate the rectangle draw tool on the map. Draw a box over the area you want to cache for offline use. When you release, a size estimation dialog appears.
8.7 Download Progress
During download, a progress chip appears above the button bar showing tile count (X of Y tiles) and estimated time remaining. The gear panel closes — you can continue using the map while tiles download.
NOTE: Keep app open during download. Background download is not yet supported — closing the app will stop the download. Resume is automatic — tiles already on disk are skipped if you restart a download over the same area.
8.8 Show / Hide Downloaded Areas
The SHOW DOWNLOADED button in the map settings panel displays a blue overlay on the map showing all areas where offline tiles have been downloaded. Use this to identify coverage gaps before heading into the field.
NOTE: The overlay is computed at zoom level 14 for performance. Each blue rectangle represents approximately a 2.5 square mile area. For precise field-level coverage, zoom in past zoom 16 with offline mode active to confirm tile rendering directly.
9. CONVOY SETTINGS
Convoy settings control how GroupTrack tracks the convoy, records the route, and identifies the lead vehicle. These settings are designed around two primary use cases: motorcycle group rides and SXS / UTV convoy rides. Choosing the right combination of settings significantly affects map track clarity and situational awareness.
9.1 Lead Lock
GroupTrack automatically identifies the lead cart by analyzing each vehicle's position in the convoy order. On straight sections this works perfectly. On switchbacks and tight turns at slow speed, position relationships temporarily shift and the algorithm can oscillate between carts, producing spaghetti track lines.
Lead Lock solves this by freezing the lead assignment after the convoy has physically sorted itself into a stable order. The lock fires automatically after 1/4 mile of movement from the RECORD button press — by that distance the convoy is strung out and the lead is clearly established.
9.2 Track Recording Mode — Choosing the Right Setting
GroupTrack records GPS track lines during a ride. The Track Recording Mode controls which vehicles draw track lines and what colors are used. Choosing the right mode for your ride type dramatically affects the usefulness of the recorded track.
TRACK ALL (Recommended for Motorcycle Group Rides)
Every cart in the convoy draws its own track line on the map in a distinct color. Each rider's path is individually visible. This is ideal for motorcycle group rides where individual lines, spread, and lateral positioning are meaningful data.
LEAD ONLY with Black Track (Recommended for SXS / UTV Convoy Rides)
Only the lead cart draws a track line, in solid black. All other carts follow the lead and do not draw independent lines. This produces a single clean authoritative track — the route the convoy actually traveled. This is the recommended mode for SXS, UTV, and truck convoys where all vehicles follow the same trail and individual lines add visual clutter without informational value.
NOTE: For the cleanest SXS convoy track: use LEAD ONLY mode with black track, enable Lead Lock, and tap I AM LEAD in MY CART before departure to lock the lead immediately without waiting for 1/4 mile accumulation.
9.3 Convoy Settings Summary — Quick Reference
10. GROUPTRACK MENU
Tap the GroupTrack header at the top of the map to open the submenu. This is the main navigation hub for ride management and radio configuration.
11. APPLY RIDE INFO TO RADIO

Tapping SHOW DOWNLOADED scans all downloaded tile areas and draws them as semi-transparent blue rectangles on the map. The overlay moves and scales with the map as you pan and zoom. Tapping HIDE DOWNLOADED removes the overlay. The button toggles between these two states.
The overlay remains visible while you tap DOWNLOAD REGION and draw a new download rectangle. This lets you draw adjacent areas without gaps. Use the SHOW DOWNLOADED overlay as your guide, then draw the next rectangle to fill uncovered terrain.
NOTE: The overlay is computed at zoom level 14 for performance. Each blue rectangle represents approximately a 2.5 square mile area. For precise field-level coverage, zoom in past zoom 16 with offline mode active to confirm tile rendering directly.

GroupTrack is an off-grid group awareness application for motorcycles, SxS/UTV, mountain bikes, hikers, and overlanders. It provides real-time convoy tracking over Meshtastic LoRa mesh radio — no cell service or internet required during operation.

Function Index
Use this index to find the page for any GroupTrack function. Testers use this index to locate functions in the certification checklist.

GroupTrack requires a Meshtastic-compatible LoRa radio connected via Bluetooth to function. The convoy map is always active — online services (V3.0) require internet connectivity.




=== GroupTrack_UtahTrailsData_Reference.docx ===
Utah Trails and Pathways — Data Reference
Source: Utah Geospatial Resource Center (UGRC) / SGID
Archived: April 13, 2026  |  For GroupTrack map overlay development

1. Dataset Overview
The Utah Trails and Pathways map layer represents off-street features and important road connections that augment Utah's recreational trail and transportation network. This map layer features many of Utah's trails and pathways but is not yet complete.


2. Access URLs

Feature Service (ArcGIS REST)
https://services1.arcgis.com/99lidPhWCzftIe9K/ArcGIS/rest/services/TrailsAndPathways/FeatureServer/0

GeoJSON Query — Bounding Box (St. George Area)
https://services1.arcgis.com/99lidPhWCzftIe9K/ArcGIS/rest/services/TrailsAndPathways/FeatureServer/0/query?where=1%3D1&geometry=-114.0,36.9,-113.4,37.4&geometryType=esriGeometryEnvelope&outSR=4326&f=geojson

GeoJSON Query — Custom Bounding Box Template
https://services1.arcgis.com/99lidPhWCzftIe9K/ArcGIS/rest/services/TrailsAndPathways/FeatureServer/0/query?where=1%3D1&geometry={WEST},{SOUTH},{EAST},{NORTH}&geometryType=esriGeometryEnvelope&outSR=4326&f=geojson
Replace {WEST},{SOUTH},{EAST},{NORTH} with decimal degree bounding box coordinates.

Open SGID PostgreSQL Query
select * from recreation.trails_and_pathways limit 10;

Dataset Landing Page
https://opendata.gis.utah.gov/datasets/utah-trails-and-pathways/about
https://gis.utah.gov/products/sgid/recreation/trails-pathways/

3. CartoCode Field — Facility Type
Use CartoCode for specific facility type queries. Critical for filtering OHV-relevant trails.


4. Class Field — General Facility Type


5. Data History
The dataset originated from map data purchased from Orbital View Inc., with full rights purchased by the GOED Office of Outdoor Recreation in July 2014. Subsequent updates:
•  2014 — Revisions from National Park Service
•  2015 — Additions from Emery County
•  June 2016 — Additions from Cache County, US Forest Service Geodata Hub (all forests except Sawtooth), Mountainland Association of Governments (Utah and Wasatch counties)
•  2018/2019 — Additions as part of multimodal project
•  June 2020 — Significant schema change
•  Ongoing — Updates by WFRC transportation planners
•  February 4, 2026 — Most recent update

6. GroupTrack Implementation Notes

Recommended Use
•  Query GeoJSON by bounding box as rider pans the map — dynamic loading
•  Display as Leaflet GeoJSON overlay on SAT or TOPO+ tile base
•  Filter by CartoCode=4 (Road-concurrent) and Class=Trail for OHV-relevant routes
•  Color-code by CartoCode — different colors for trail types
•  Tap trail to show name and allowed use attributes

Leaflet Integration
fetch(`${FEATURE_SERVICE_URL}/query?where=1=1&geometry=${bounds}&geometryType=esriGeometryEnvelope&outSR=4326&f=geojson`)
  .then(r => r.json())
  .then(data => L.geoJSON(data, { style: trailStyle }).addTo(map));

Bounding Box for St. George / Washington County
West: -114.1   South: 36.85   East: -113.3   North: 37.5

Additional Resources
Utah Trails Forum
Trails and Pathways Data Model (Google Sheets)
UGRC Atlas Interactive Map
Utah DNR ArcGIS Hub — OHV layers
Utah OHV Maps — Recreation.utah.gov
Field | Value
Category | Recreation
Data Type | Line GIS data
Steward | UGRC — Utah Geospatial Resource Center
Last Updated | February 4, 2026
Coordinate System | WGS84 (EPSG:4326)
License | Open / Public — State of Utah
Code | Type | Description
1 | Hiking Only | Hiking and walking only — not OHV relevant
2 | Hiking and Biking | Singletrack natural surface — hiking and biking allowed
3 | Paved Shared Use | Paved shared use path, recreational path, urban pathway
4 | Road-concurrent | On or adjacent to road — natural surface double track, forest service road, canal road. OHV RELEVANT.
5 | Biking Only | Biking primary or only use — high speeds, technical features
6 | Equestrian Primary | Equestrian primary use
7 | Steps | Steps or staircase — not accessible to wheeled devices
8 | Bridge/Tunnel | Grade change above or below ground — bridge or tunnel
9 | Link | Network route connection
Class | Description
Path | Paved off-street travelway for non-motorized users — wider than sidewalk
Trail | Natural surface travelway — dirt, gravel, slickrock, snow. OHV RELEVANT.
Link | Network route connection through parking lot, alleyway etc.
Steps | Concrete steps or stairs along a paved route
Grade Separated Crossing | Paved crossing above or below roadway for walking/bicycling
