=== GroupTrack_V2.4_Development_Plan.docx ===
GroupTrack
Release 2.4 — Development Plan
March 25, 2026  |  Branch: feature/convoy-event-ride  |  Author: Fred Kix


OBJECTIVES
Rider can draw a map area on the live map and download tiles for offline use
Tiles served from local device storage when internet is unavailable
All 4 tile types (SAT, HYB, TOPO, RD) work offline
Download size estimated and blocked if over 500MB ceiling
Downloaded regions listed and manageable
Field tested — WiFi off, map displays correctly on trail
Zero open items — clean enough to hand to external iOS developer

SEAL CRITERIA


FUNCTION DEVELOPMENT PLAN

F1 — Leaflet Draw Tool
Activate a rectangle draw mode in the Leaflet map. User draws bounding box. Coordinates passed to Android via JavaScript bridge.


F2 — Tile Calculator
Pure Kotlin utility that calculates the complete list of tile coordinates for a bounding box across zoom levels z10-z18. No side effects. Unit testable.


F3 — Download Size Dialog
After area drawn, show user the tile count and estimated MB before starting download. Block if over ceiling.


F4 — Tile Downloader
Kotlin coroutine-based downloader. Downloads tiles from active tile source URL to local device storage. Cancellable. Reports progress.


F5 — Leaflet Local Tile Serving
Configure Leaflet and Android WebView to serve tiles from local device storage. Detect local vs online tile source by URL prefix.


F6 — Download Progress UI
Show download progress in the gear settings panel. User can see tile count, MB remaining, and cancel the download.


F7 — Region List and Management
Track downloaded map regions. Show in gear panel. Allow deletion of individual regions.


F8 — Offline Mode Indicator
Small visual indicator showing whether current map is serving online or local cached tiles.


BUILD SEQUENCE


TECHNICAL REFERENCES
Tile URL Templates — Active Sources


Key Files

Local Storage Structure

Leaflet.draw CDN

V2.3 BACKLOG — STATUS REVIEW AT V2.4 SEAL

GroupTrack  |  Release 2.4 Development Plan  |  March 25, 2026  |  Fred Kix  |  visit www.grouptrack.org
V2.4 is the last standalone Android release before AWS backend integration. It proves the offline map download process end-to-end on a single device. All logic built here ports directly to V4.0 AWS implementation without rewrite. Upon seal, V2.4 becomes the source for the iPad iOS personal edition port.
V2.4 is sealed when ALL of the following are verified on device with WiFi off:
CRITERION | VERIFICATION METHOD
Draw bounding box on live map | Leaflet rectangle tool activates from gear panel. Box drawn successfully.
Tile count and MB displayed before download | Dialog shows X tiles, Y MB. Accurate estimate shown.
Download blocked if over 500MB | Draw large area. Blocked with clear message.
Tiles download to device storage | Check filesDir/tiles/ after download. Files present.
Leaflet serves tiles offline | Turn WiFi off. Open app. Map displays from local cache.
SAT tiles work offline | Switch to SAT. Tiles display. No network requests.
HYB tiles work offline | Switch to HYB. Tiles display.
TOPO tiles work offline | Switch to TOPO. Tiles display.
RD tiles work offline | Switch to RD. Tiles display.
Downloaded region appears in list | Gear panel shows region entry with size and date.
DELETE removes region | Tap DELETE. Files removed from storage. Entry gone from list.
Progress shown during download | ProgressBar visible. Count updates. CANCEL works.
Zero open items | All backlog items from V2.3 reviewed. Outstanding items documented.
ID | FUNCTION | TOOLS / FILES | DETAIL
F1-01 | Add Leaflet.draw plugin | convoy_map.html — CDN | Add to <head>: leaflet-draw.css and leaflet-draw.js from cdnjs.cloudflare.com/ajax/libs/leaflet.draw/. Initialize L.Control.Draw with rectangle only. Add drawControl to map.
F1-02 | Draw mode activation | convoy_map.html — JavaScript | function activateDrawMode() { drawControl.enable(); }. Called from Android via evaluateJavascript('activateDrawMode()').
F1-03 | Area selection callback | convoy_map.html — JavaScript | map.on(L.Draw.Event.CREATED, function(e) { var b = e.layer.getBounds(); Android.onAreaSelected(b.getNorth(), b.getSouth(), b.getEast(), b.getWest()); });
F1-04 | Android bridge receiver | ConvoyScreen.kt — WebViewClient | Add @JavascriptInterface fun onAreaSelected(n: Double, s: Double, e: Double, w: Double). Triggers size estimation dialog.
F1-05 | DOWNLOAD REGION button | ConvoyScreen.kt — gear panel | Button already exists as placeholder. Wire clickable to call evaluateJavascript('activateDrawMode()'). Button label: DRAW MAP AREA.
ID | FUNCTION | TOOLS / FILES | DETAIL
F2-01 | TileKey data class | ConvoyTileCalculator.kt — new file | data class TileKey(val z: Int, val x: Int, val y: Int). toString() = '{z}/{x}/{y}'. Companion fun fromString(s: String): TileKey.
F2-02 | lon2tile function | ConvoyTileCalculator.kt | fun lon2tile(lon: Double, z: Int): Int = floor((lon + 180.0) / 360.0 * (1 shl z)).toInt()
F2-03 | lat2tile function | ConvoyTileCalculator.kt | fun lat2tile(lat: Double, z: Int): Int { val r = Math.toRadians(lat); return floor((1.0 - ln(tan(r) + 1.0/cos(r)) / PI) / 2.0 * (1 shl z)).toInt() }
F2-04 | calculateTiles function | ConvoyTileCalculator.kt | fun calculateTiles(n,s,e,w: Double, zMin=10, zMax=18): List<TileKey>. For each z from zMin to zMax: get xMin=lon2tile(w,z), xMax=lon2tile(e,z), yMin=lat2tile(n,z), yMax=lat2tile(s,z). Iterate all x,y combinations. Return flat list.
F2-05 | estimateSizeMB function | ConvoyTileCalculator.kt | fun estimateSizeMB(tiles: List<TileKey>): Float = tiles.size * 15f / 1024f. Constant AVG_TILE_KB = 15f. Constant CEILING_MB = 500f.
F2-06 | isWithinCeiling function | ConvoyTileCalculator.kt | fun isWithinCeiling(tiles: List<TileKey>): Boolean = estimateSizeMB(tiles) <= CEILING_MB
ID | FUNCTION | TOOLS / FILES | DETAIL
F3-01 | Size estimation on area selected | ConvoyScreen.kt | On onAreaSelected() callback: launch coroutine, call calculateTiles() and estimateSizeMB(). Update UI state with results.
F3-02 | Size confirmation dialog | ConvoyScreen.kt — AlertDialog | Show: 'Download Map Area? X tiles, Y MB estimated. This may take several minutes.' Buttons: DOWNLOAD / CANCEL.
F3-03 | Ceiling exceeded dialog | ConvoyScreen.kt — AlertDialog | If over 500MB: 'Area too large — estimated Y MB exceeds 500MB limit. Reduce the selected area and try again.' Button: OK only.
F3-04 | Tile source display | ConvoyScreen.kt | Dialog shows which tile source will be downloaded: 'Source: Esri Satellite'. Matches currently active map type.
ID | FUNCTION | TOOLS / FILES | DETAIL
F4-01 | ConvoyTileDownloader object | ConvoyTileDownloader.kt — new file | object ConvoyTileDownloader. fun buildTileUrl(tile: TileKey, sourceUrl: String): String. Replace {z}/{x}/{y} and {z}/{y}/{x} in URL template.
F4-02 | Storage path builder | ConvoyTileDownloader.kt | fun tilePath(context: Context, source: String, tile: TileKey): File. Returns: context.filesDir/tiles/{source}/{z}/{x}/{y}.png. Creates parent directories.
F4-03 | Single tile download | ConvoyTileDownloader.kt | suspend fun downloadTile(url: String, dest: File): Boolean. OkHttp GET request. Write bytes to dest file. Return success/fail. Retry once on failure.
F4-04 | Batch download function | ConvoyTileDownloader.kt | suspend fun downloadTiles(context, tiles, sourceUrl, sourceName, onProgress): Result<DownloadSummary>. Iterate tiles. Call downloadTile per tile. Call onProgress(downloaded, total, failCount). DownloadSummary(downloaded, failed, totalMB).
F4-05 | Cancellation support | ConvoyTileDownloader.kt | Accept CoroutineScope parameter. Check isActive before each tile download. On cancellation: return partial DownloadSummary with downloaded count.
F4-06 | HTTP client | ConvoyTileDownloader.kt | Use OkHttp — already in Meshtastic dependencies. Connection timeout 10s. Read timeout 15s. No caching — write directly to file.
ID | FUNCTION | TOOLS / FILES | DETAIL
F5-01 | WebView file access | ConvoyScreen.kt — WebView setup | webView.settings.allowFileAccessFromFileURLs = true. webView.settings.allowUniversalAccessFromFileURLs = true. Required for Leaflet to load file:// tile URLs.
F5-02 | Local path constant | ConvoyConfig.kt | const val LOCAL_TILE_BASE = 'file:///data/user/0/com.geeksville.mesh.google.debug/files/tiles/'. Used to build local tile URL template.
F5-03 | setTileUrl local detection | convoy_map.html — JavaScript | Update setTileUrl(url): if url starts with 'file://' use as-is. Otherwise use as remote URL. L.tileLayer(url, {maxZoom:19, tileSize:256}). No crossOrigin needed for file:// tiles.
F5-04 | Switch to local tiles | ConvoyScreen.kt | After download completes: build local URL = LOCAL_TILE_BASE + sourceName + '/{z}/{x}/{y}.png'. Call evaluateJavascript('setTileUrl("' + localUrl + '")'). Store active source preference.
F5-05 | Restore online tiles | ConvoyScreen.kt | When user selects a tile type button: if local cache exists for that type, use local URL. Otherwise use online URL. Check filesDir/tiles/{sourceName}/ exists and has files.

=== GroupTrack_V2.4_Handoff_Apr23.docx ===
GroupTrack V2.4
End of Day Handoff — April 23, 2026
Session summary, completed work, open issues, and Google Play sprint plan
1. Session Context
This session began with a broken working tree caused by a git stash pop that overwrote two committed files (Main.kt and ConvoySubMenu.kt) with older stashed versions. The stash had been created the prior evening to preserve uncommitted changes overnight. Between the stash creation and the pop, commits had been made that added new parameters, renamed UI elements, and added the Services navigation icon. The stash pop merged the old versions on top of the new code, causing parameter mismatches and reverting the GroupTrack branding back to 'Convoy'.

Resolution: Main.kt and ConvoySubMenu.kt were restored to committed versions via git checkout HEAD. The two legitimately modified files (ConvoyScreen.kt with track picker changes, ConvoyMapViewerScreen.kt with async scan fix) were kept from the stash. Lesson learned: do not use git stash. Leave the working tree dirty overnight, or commit WIP to a throwaway branch.

2. Completed Today


Commit: 2cb99c404 — feat: V2.4 track picker mutual exclusion, async ALL, Map Viewer download FAB, standalone GPS, Esri tile swap
Prior known-good commit: 9d66c9781 — feat: V2.4 track viewer both maps, 3 map sources, intent filter import


3. Field Test Readiness
Three-day field test beginning April 24. Two devices deployed:


Field test focus areas:
Esri satellite imagery quality vs. prior Google hybrid at all zoom levels
Trail overlay visibility and accuracy on southern Utah trails
Track recording reliability over multi-hour rides
Standalone GPS mode (no radio) — pin positioning, auto-centering, track recording
Map Viewer download workflow — find area, set zoom, draw region, download tiles
Track panel — load individual tracks, ALL button performance, search filtering
V3.0 services UI — dashboard, organizer/rider toggle, rides search tabs


4. Open Issues — Detailed Background

4.1 GPX/KML Import Handler Not Executing
Severity: HIGH — blocks user adoption

Background: GroupTrack registers intent filters in AndroidManifest.xml for GPX and KML MIME types (application/gpx+xml, application/vnd.google-earth.kml+xml, application/octet-stream, text/xml, application/xml). When a user taps a GPX/KML file in a file manager, Android should route the intent to GroupTrack.

The import was confirmed working on April 22 at 15:08 — a file 'St George to Bar10.gpx' was successfully imported and saved to /Documents/my_tracks/. This was on commit 9d66c9781.

Current behavior: Tapping a GPX file in the file manager launches GroupTrack (grey screen appears, then black screen), but the import handler code does not execute. Logcat shows no GroupTrack log entries related to import — the intent reaches the activity but is not processed.

Root cause analysis:
The intent filters are correctly defined in AndroidManifest.xml and attached to the main activity.
The activity launches (visual confirmation of grey/black screen transition).
No SecurityException or Permission Denial in logcat — this is not a permissions issue.
The import handler code in ConvoyScreen.kt is unchanged between 9d66c9781 and current commit.
The failure is in the intent routing within the activity — the intent arrives but the handler that reads the URI and processes the file is not being triggered.
Suspect: Main.kt intent handling may have changed during stash resolution. Although Main.kt was restored to HEAD, the committed version may not have the import intent processing that existed at 9d66c9781. Need to diff Main.kt between 9d66c9781 and HEAD.

Next step: Run 'git diff 9d66c9781 HEAD -- app/src/main/java/com/geeksville/mesh/ui/Main.kt' and inspect any changes to intent handling, onCreate, or onNewIntent. The working version processed the intent URI and copied the file to my_tracks/. Something in that path is missing or broken.

Strategic importance: Fred has stated that import is the adoption gate. Users will not switch from Gaia/onX if they lose their track history. A user's Gaia/onX export is typically 1-2 large GPX files containing all their ride history. GroupTrack's multi-track parser splits these into individual tracks. The import must be seamless — tap file, GroupTrack handles it, tracks appear. No file pickers, no extra steps.

4.2 Track Export Redesign
Severity: MEDIUM — needed for complete user workflow

Current state: Track export uses a system file picker which is unfriendly for non-technical users and provides no control over presentation or navigation. A partially-built ConvoyTrackExportSheet.kt exists from a prior session with scan, search, multi-select, and MediaStore copy logic.

Required design:
In-app track list screen showing all tracks from /Documents/my_tracks/
Search by filename, sort by date
Multi-select with SELECT ALL toggle
Two export actions: (1) Export to Downloads — copies via MediaStore, (2) Email as attachment — opens Android share sheet with files attached
Result toast showing number of files exported

This replaces the current file picker approach entirely. No system UI, full GroupTrack branding and navigation control.

4.3 Intent Filter Cleanup
Severity: MEDIUM — required for Play Store submission

Current manifest includes overly broad intent filters: application/octet-stream, text/xml, application/xml. These cause GroupTrack to appear as a file handler for ANY unknown file type or ANY XML file on the device. Google Play review may flag this as inappropriate behavior.

Fix: Remove application/octet-stream, text/xml, and application/xml. Keep only application/gpx+xml, application/vnd.google-earth.kml+xml, and file extension filters for .gpx and .kml.

Risk: Some file managers may send GPX/KML files with generic MIME types (octet-stream or text/xml) instead of the correct GPX/KML types. Removing the broad filters may prevent GroupTrack from catching these. Mitigation: test with multiple file managers after the change. If a specific file manager sends the wrong MIME type, consider adding it back as a narrower filter.

4.4 Package Rename
Severity: HIGH — required for Play Store, one-time breaking change

Change applicationId from com.geeksville.mesh to com.grouptrack.android. This is the single largest change in the Google Play sprint because it touches multiple systems:

build.gradle.kts — applicationId change
grouptrack-release-key.jks — wire as signing key in keystore.properties
Firebase Console — new project for com.grouptrack.android, download new google-services.json
AndroidManifest.xml — update package references, authorities, FileProvider
Crashlytics — link to new Firebase project

What survives the rename:
Tile storage (/sdcard/Documents/GroupTrack/maps/tiles/) — package-independent path, no migration needed
Track storage (/sdcard/Documents/my_tracks/) — package-independent path, no migration needed

What resets:
SharedPreferences — session clears, permission dialogs reappear, first-launch experience triggers
App-internal filesDir — convoy ride configs, master configs, archives all reset

All testers must uninstall the old app and install the new one. This must be communicated clearly. Maps and tracks are preserved.

4.5 About/Attribution Screen
Severity: MEDIUM — legal requirement for distribution

GroupTrack is built on Meshtastic-Android (GPL v3), uses Leaflet (BSD 2-Clause) for map rendering, and Esri tile services for satellite and topographic imagery. All three require attribution before public distribution.

Required attributions:
Meshtastic — 'Built on Meshtastic-Android' + link to github.com/meshtastic/Meshtastic-Android. GPL v3 requires making modified source available on request. Note: convoy/ package files are original work, not modifications to Meshtastic code.
Leaflet — 'Map rendering by Leaflet' + leafletjs.com link. BSD 2-Clause. Also verify the built-in Leaflet attribution control is not suppressed on the map.
Esri — 'Map tiles by Esri' with specific attributions per source: SAT: Esri, Earthstar Geographics. TOPO: Esri, HERE, Garmin, FAO, NOAA, USGS.

Implementation: New Composable screen accessible from ConvoySubMenu or Settings. Single screen, one link per component. Minimal effort — half day.

4.6 MANAGE_EXTERNAL_STORAGE Permission
Severity: HIGH — Play Store unlocks this; justification needed for review

Android 14 blocks the MANAGE_EXTERNAL_STORAGE toggle for sideloaded apps. Play Store deployment enables the toggle. This is the definitive fix for the scoped storage issues that have plagued V2.4 development — file import failures, directory listing restrictions, and cross-app file access limitations.

For Play Store submission, a MANAGE_EXTERNAL_STORAGE declaration is required in Play Console under App Content > Sensitive Permissions. The justification must explain why all-files access is needed. GroupTrack's justification: offline navigation app that reads and writes map tiles and GPS track files to shared storage for cross-app interoperability (import from Gaia/onX, export to Google Earth).

Similar apps (Gaia GPS, onX Maps, Avenza Maps) successfully use this permission on the Play Store. Precedent is established.

Code changes required:
Restore isExternalStorageManager() check on app startup
First-launch dialog explaining why permission is needed
Graceful degradation if permission denied (reduced import/export functionality)

4.7 BLE Scan Button Not Appearing
Severity: LOW — workaround exists, not blocking Play Store

On fresh device after granting all permissions, the BLE scan button does not appear in the Connections screen. This is a Meshtastic-Android inherited issue. Workaround: close and reopen the app. The button appears on second launch. Root cause investigation deferred.


5. Google Play Sprint — Plan of Attack
Goal: GroupTrack V2.4 available on Google Play internal testing track. Target: 7-10 days from start.

5.1 Recommended Sequence


5.2 Risk Assessment



6. Architecture Notes for Next Session

6.1 Git Workflow
Do not use git stash. Options for preserving work overnight:
Leave working tree dirty — safest, simplest. Changes survive sleep/shutdown.
Commit WIP to throwaway branch: git checkout -b wip/[feature], git add -A, git commit -m 'WIP', git checkout feature/convoy-event-ride
If stash is accidentally used and causes conflicts, always restore committed files with 'git checkout HEAD -- filename'. Never use --theirs on files that have been committed since the stash was created.

6.2 Build Discipline
V2.4 = assembleGoogleRelease on device 8624SBCEDF00001789 (com.geeksville.mesh.google). V3.0 = assembleGoogleDebug with V3_FEATURES_ENABLED=true on device 24039703201775 (com.geeksville.mesh.google.debug). Never cross these. Always flip V3_FEATURES_ENABLED back to false after V3 build.

6.3 Patch Methodology
All code changes via Python patch scripts with exact string replacement and error exit on match failure. Scripts downloaded to ~/Downloads, run from ~/Meshtastic-Android. Script names uniquely versioned (never reuse). Always add encoding='utf-8' to file open calls for Windows compatibility.

6.4 Key File Locations
Tile storage: /sdcard/Documents/GroupTrack/maps/tiles/
Track storage: /sdcard/Documents/my_tracks/
Convoy source: app/src/main/java/com/geeksville/mesh/convoy/
HTML maps: app/src/main/assets/convoy_map.html, grouptrack_map.html
Trail data: app/src/main/assets/utah_trails_stgeorge.geojson
PHP API: /var/www/html/convoy_api.php on EC2 34.224.89.217
Release keystore: grouptrack-release-key.jks (backed up to 4 locations)

=== GroupTrack_V2.4_ReleaseNotes_Apr23.docx ===
GroupTrack V2.4
Release Notes
April 23, 2026 | Build: feature/convoy-event-ride
Life is too short. Enjoy your time on the trail. Never worry about a wrong turn.
Product Rename
Convoy Tracker is now GroupTrack. The app icon, menu headers, and bottom navigation bar all reflect the new brand. The Meshtastic bottom navigation icon is labeled 'GroupTrack' and opens the GroupTrack menu.

Map Sources
Map tile sources have been updated for licensing compliance and trail visibility.


Google Maps tile source has been removed. All map sources are now properly licensed for offline caching and distribution.

Trail Overlays
Utah OHV and recreation trails are now displayed as colored overlays on the SAT and TOPO map sources. Trail data is sourced from UGRC (Utah Geospatial Resource Center) and bundled with the app.


Tap TRAILS in the header bar to toggle trail overlay visibility. Trail tap popups show trail name, type, and motorized status.

Track Viewing — TRACKS Panel
Both the Convoy Map and Map Viewer now include a TRACKS panel for loading and viewing GPX/KML track files on the map.

How to use:
Tap TRACKS in the floating settings bar (Convoy Map) or header bar (Map Viewer)
Search for tracks by name using the search field
Tap a track to load it on the map as a green polyline. Tap again to remove.
ALL loads all tracks at once. FIT zooms to fit all loaded tracks. CLEAR removes all.
Track files are read from /Documents/my_tracks/ on the device.

Map Viewer
The Map Viewer is an independent map screen for viewing tracks and planning without convoy operations. Access via GroupTrack Menu > MAP SETTINGS > Map Viewer.

Map Viewer features:
Full-screen map with SAT, TOPO, and TOPO+ tile sources
Trail overlays with TRAILS toggle
TRACKS panel for loading GPX/KML files
FIND — search for cities, parks, or trail areas by name
Floating download button (bottom-right) for offline tile downloads
Download controls: ZOOM slider, DOWNLOAD REGION (draw area), SHOW DOWNLOADED

Map Tile Downloads
Download map tiles for offline use in areas without cell service. Available on both the Convoy Map and Map Viewer.

How to download:
Use FIND to navigate to the area you want to download
Set the ZOOM level (16-19) — higher zoom = more detail, more storage
Tap DOWNLOAD REGION — draw a rectangle on the map to select the area
Tiles download in the background. Progress bar shows completion.
Tap SHOW DOWNLOADED to see blue overlay showing which areas are cached

Tile storage: /Documents/GroupTrack/maps/tiles/ — shared across all GroupTrack versions. Tiles survive app updates and reinstalls.

GPX/KML Track Import
Import track files from Gaia GPS, onX Maps, or any app that exports GPX or KML files.

How to import:
Locate your GPX or KML file in the Android file manager or Downloads folder
Tap the file — Android will offer to open it with GroupTrack
GroupTrack processes the file and splits multi-track GPX files into individual tracks
Imported tracks appear in the TRACKS panel on both maps

Supported formats: GPX (GPS Exchange Format) and KML (Google Earth). Multi-track GPX files from Gaia/onX are automatically split into individual tracks.

Standalone GPS Mode
GroupTrack now works without a Meshtastic radio for solo navigation. When no radio is connected, the app uses the phone's GPS to create a single cart node.

Phone appears as a map pin with device model name (e.g. P50)
Map centers on phone GPS position on launch
GPS track recording works without radio
Trail overlays and track viewing fully functional

This enables users to evaluate GroupTrack as a standalone navigation app before investing in radio hardware.

Track Recording — GPX Format
Track recording now outputs standard GPX format (previously KML). GPX is the industry standard for GPS track exchange and is compatible with Gaia GPS, onX Maps, Google Earth, and all major mapping applications.

Recorded tracks are saved to /Documents/my_tracks/ with timestamp-based filenames.

Tile Storage
Map tiles are stored in a permanent, package-independent location:
/sdcard/Documents/GroupTrack/maps/tiles/

This location survives app updates, reinstalls, and the upcoming package rename for Google Play Store deployment. Tiles from the previous app-specific storage were migrated automatically on first launch.

Bug Fixes
Track picker panel no longer causes ANR (Application Not Responding). All file operations are now asynchronous.
TRACKS and settings panels are mutually exclusive — opening one closes the other. No more layout conflicts.
Standalone GPS centering fixed — map centers on phone position immediately on launch.
Map type selection persists correctly across menu bar toggles.
SKIP button now deletes temporary track files.

Known Issues

Coming Next — V2.5
Independent Planning Map with route creation and waypoint management
Snap-to-trail route planning using UGRC trail data
Advanced file management — subfolder creation, batch import/export
Google Play Store deployment — resolves all permission issues

GroupTrack V2.4
End of Day Handoff — April 29, 2026
Play Store Setup + Storage Permission Fix + V2.4 Open Items
Confidential — Internal Development
PART 1: Completed Today
1.1 Google Play Console Setup
All app content checklist items completed and approved:
1.2 Internal Testing Release
Note: This AAB does NOT have the storage permission fix. A new AAB must be built and uploaded.
1.3 Storage Permission Fix (CRITICAL)
Root cause identified and fixed: MANAGE_EXTERNAL_STORAGE, WRITE_EXTERNAL_STORAGE, and READ_EXTERNAL_STORAGE were commented out in AndroidManifest.xml by upstream Meshtastic developers. The comment said 'Only for debug log writing, disable for production.' The permissions appeared in intermediate build files (merged manifest) but were stripped from the final APK.
Fix applied: Uncommented all three permissions. Added tools:node="replace" to WRITE and MANAGE to force them through any build pipeline stripping. Verified with aapt2 that permissions appear in the final APK.
Result: App installed on Android 1, All Files Access toggle is active and grantable, offline maps load, tracks display in ~1 second, app is fully functional.
Important: The Play Store AAB (version 29320449) does NOT include this fix. A new AAB must be built and uploaded before the Play Store version will work correctly.
1.4 Code Changes Committed
Commit: 744c1588c on feature/convoy-event-ride
Changes in this commit:
Uncommented storage permissions in AndroidManifest.xml with tools:node="replace"
Updated targetSdk from 34 to 35 (config.properties TARGET_SDK=35)
Updated google-services.json for com.grouptrack.android package
Runtime MANAGE_EXTERNAL_STORAGE permission request in MainActivity

2. Lead Cart Redesign — Last V2.4 Release

Full redesign. Remove all distance accumulator logic. Replace with PRIVATE_APP declaration packet.

2.1  RECORD Button Decision Tree
RECORD tapped
No radio connected -> auto-assign My Cart as lead, use device GPS, start recording (standalone mode)
Radio connected + lead already locked -> start recording immediately, no dialog
Radio connected + no lead locked -> show dialog: I AM LEAD / SELECT LEAD / NO LEAD YET
I AM LEAD -> broadcast PRIVATE_APP packet with my node ID, lock lead on all carts, start recording
SELECT LEAD -> show node list, tap to select, broadcast packet with selected node ID, lock, start recording
NO LEAD YET -> start recording, lead unassigned, assign later via RECALC LEAD in settings

3.1  Tile Source Strategy — Thunderforest Decision
OPEN ISSUE: Only SAT layer downloading. Root cause suspected: Thunderforest TRAIL free tier quota (150,000 tiles/month) likely exhausted after 6+ area downloads. Tiles return 403/429, downloader logs failure and continues with other sources.

RECOMMENDATION: Remove Thunderforest TRAIL source entirely. Replace with Utah UGRC GeoJSON trail overlay (dynamic bounding box query — no tile quota). This provides better trail data for St. George/southern Utah riding area with no quota limits.

OHV filter: CartoCode=4 (road-concurrent forest service roads, double track)
Tap behavior: Show trail name and allowed use on tap

No API key required — public SGID data

=== GroupTrack_V24_RadioMap_Revisions_v2.docx ===
GroupTrack V2.4 — Radio & Map Revision Items
Date: April 13, 2026  |  Branch: feature/convoy-event-ride
Purpose: All V2.4 radio and map improvement items with implementation sequence

Implementation Sequence — In Order
Build and test each step before proceeding to the next.


MAP-01 — GPS Recording Interval
File: ConvoyGpsService.kt  |  Line: 249


MAP-02 — Lead Cart Multi-Point Broadcast
Buffer 5 GPS points on lead cart over 5-second window. Broadcast as single Meshtastic PRIVATE_APP packet. Zero channel traffic change.

Packet Specification

Why Heading and Speed Matter
•  Heading enables correct interpolation between points on curves and switchbacks
•  Speed confirms cart is moving — filters GPS drift when stationary
•  Together produce a track that follows the actual route rather than straight-line approximation
•  Speed data enables real-time convoy spread calculation with better accuracy

Timestamp Requirement — Critical
Every point MUST include a Unix timestamp. This enables:
•  Correct ordering of received points even if radio packets arrive out of order
•  Accurate speed calculation between consecutive points
•  Post-ride track reconstruction with correct timing
•  Snap-to-trail alignment using timestamp for interpolation

Implementation

MAP-03 — KML/GPX Overlay on Convoy Map
Rider imports a KML or GPX route file and displays it as an overlay on the convoy map.


MAP-04 — Utah UGRC Trail Overlay
Dynamic GeoJSON trail overlay from Utah SGID — queried by bounding box as rider pans map.


MAP-05 — USFS National OHV Trail Overlay
National Forest OHV trails for all 50 states — same dynamic bounding box query approach.


MAP-06 — Post-Ride Track Snap-to-Trail
Phase C. After ride completes — snap the recorded GPX to nearest trail line for clean export.


RADIO-01 — SHORT_FAST Bandwidth Analysis
Research item. Evaluate whether switching from LONG_FAST to SHORT_FAST enables faster update intervals with acceptable range trade-off.

Current — LONG_FAST

SHORT_FAST Analysis

MEDIUM_FAST — Better Trade-off

Recommendation
Stay on LONG_FAST. The multi-point broadcast in MAP-02 solves the track quality problem without any modem change. Range is more important than update frequency for OHV convoy use where riders can separate by 1-2 miles on technical terrain.

•  LONG_FAST 5-second interval with 5-point payload = best of both worlds
•  1-second effective track resolution on all devices
•  Full 3-5 mile range maintained
•  25% channel utilization — safe headroom

Future consideration: if convoy moves to urban/close-proximity use where range is not a concern, SHORT_FAST at 1-2 second intervals becomes viable. Document as future option.

Key Files Reference
Step | Item | Effort | Impact

=== GroupTrack_V24_UserManual.docx ===
GroupTrack
User Manual
Version 2.4
Off-Road Convoy Tracking for Android
May 2026
1. What is GroupTrack?
GroupTrack is an Android application for off-road convoy tracking. It uses Meshtastic mesh radios to share real-time GPS positions between riders in a group, allowing everyone to see where the convoy is on an offline map -- even in areas with no cell service.
GroupTrack shows each rider's position on the map in real time, identifies the lead and tail vehicles, records GPS tracks of your ride, and works completely offline using pre-downloaded map tiles.
1.1 What You Need

CRITICAL: Your T1000-E radio MUST remain on firmware version 2.6.11. Do NOT accept firmware update prompts. Newer firmware destabilizes BLE connectivity and radio configuration.
CRITICAL: Keep your T1000-E in your cart at all times during rides. The radio must stay within 50 feet BLE range of your phone. Exceeding this range causes Bluetooth supervision timeout pauses that disrupt tracking.
WARNING: Budget Android tablets (Tab8NEU and similar) have known Bluetooth issues with T1000-E radios. The BLE connection drops after 8-10 seconds. Use a name-brand phone or tablet.
2. Getting Started
2.1 Install GroupTrack
If you received a Google Play internal test invite, follow the installation instructions provided by your ride organizer. If you received a direct APK file, see the side-loading instructions in the install guide.
2.2 Grant Permissions
GroupTrack requires several permissions to function. The app will prompt you for each one on first launch.
Location Permission
- When prompted, tap "Allow While Using the App" first.
- The app will then explain why it needs background location and ask you to grant "Allow All the Time."
- This opens your device Settings. Find GroupTrack and select "Allow all the time."
- Return to the app.
WARNING: If you only grant "While using the app," GPS track recording will stop when you turn off your screen or switch apps. Your ride track will be empty.
Storage Permission
- The app will request "All Files Access" permission.
- This opens your device Settings. Find GroupTrack and toggle on "Allow access to manage all files."
- Return to the app.
This permission is required for offline map tiles and track file storage.
Notification Permission
- Grant notification permission when prompted.
- Required for background download progress notifications.
2.3 Connect to Your Radio
- Power on your Meshtastic radio (T1000-E).
- In GroupTrack, you will see a Bluetooth connection prompt or a radio list.
- Select your radio from the list. The app connects via BLE.
- Once connected, your position and other riders' positions will appear on the map.
TIP: Keep your radio powered on and within Bluetooth range of your phone (within 50 feet) throughout the ride. GroupTrack will automatically reconnect to your last radio on future launches.
3. The Convoy Map
The main screen is the convoy map. It shows your position and every connected rider in the group. The convoy map is for live ride operations.
3.1 Map Display
- Your cart ("My Cart") is shown on the map with your position updated in real time.
- Other riders in the convoy appear as separate markers with callsigns.
- The map uses satellite tiles by default with road and place name labels.
- Trail overlays display known OHV trails when toggled on in the Display Panel.
- Lost rider alerts appear when a rider drops off the mesh network.
3.2 Map Toolbar
The convoy map has a fixed toolbar at the top of the screen with the following controls:
- PLANNING MAP: tap to open the Planning Map for downloading tiles and planning rides.
- SAT / TOPO / TOPO+: switch between satellite imagery, topographic, and USGS topo maps.
- NET / LOCAL: switch between online tiles (requires internet) and offline cached tiles.
See Section 6 for full toolbar details.
3.3 Display Panel
A floating DISPLAY panel provides toggle controls for map layers. Tap the DISPLAY pill to expand. The panel is draggable -- hold and drag to reposition it anywhere on the map. See Section 7 for full details.
3.4 Lead and Tail
GroupTrack automatically identifies the lead vehicle (front of convoy) and the tail vehicle (rear of convoy). The lead cart is set when you start a group track. The convoy engine computes positions and heading relative to the lead.
3.5 Speed Display
Your current speed (mph) is displayed on the map HUD after a brief warmup period. During the first 60 seconds, the speed display may show 0 mph while the GPS acquires enough position samples to compute accurate speed.
4. The Planning Map
The Planning Map (formerly Map Viewer) is your tool for downloading offline map tiles, viewing trail data, and planning rides. Access it by tapping PLANNING MAP in the convoy map toolbar, or from the GroupTrack menu.
4.1 Search
The Planning Map has a search field always visible at the top of the screen. Type a city, park, trail name, or region and tap GO to fly to that location. Search results appear as a dropdown -- tap a result to navigate there.
4.2 Downloading Tiles
- Navigate to your ride area using the search field.
- Tap the DOWNLOAD button in the toolbar.
- Adjust the download zoom level slider (higher zoom = more detail, larger download).
- Tap "DOWNLOAD REGION" to activate draw mode.
- Draw a rectangle around the area you want to download.
- Confirm the download. It enters the queue and starts automatically.
- A notification appears in your system tray showing progress.
- You can close GroupTrack -- the download continues in the background.
TIP: Download maps on WiFi before your ride. A typical ride area (20-30 miles of trail) takes 5-15 minutes depending on zoom level.
4.3 Track Management
The Planning Map header includes a TRACKS button for quick access to track management. Tap TRACKS to open the track picker where you can select, rename, delete, and manage your recorded ride tracks directly from the planning view.
4.4 Download Panel Controls
- Download Region: draw a rectangle to download tiles for that area.
- Remove Area: draw a rectangle to delete tiles (coming in V2.5).
- Download Zoom slider: controls the maximum zoom level for downloads (16-19).
- Fly Over Radius slider: controls the zoom level when flying to search results.
4.5 Verifying Downloads
Toggle "Downloaded Areas" in the Display Panel. Blue shading shows where you have offline tiles. Toggle "Downloads in Progress" to see pink shading for areas currently being downloaded. Verify your ride area is fully covered before heading out.
4.6 Returning to Convoy Map
Tap CONVOY in the Planning Map toolbar to return to the convoy map.
5. Recording a Ride
5.1 Start Recording
- Tap the RECORD button on the convoy map.
- If prompted for location permission, grant "Allow all the time" as described in Section 2.2.
- The app begins recording your GPS track. A recording indicator appears on screen.
- The lead cart is set automatically.
5.2 During the Ride
- Your GPS position is recorded every 3 seconds and saved to a track file.
- The track line draws on the map as you move.
- Other riders' positions update via the mesh radio network.
- You can lock and unlock your phone -- recording continues in the background.
5.3 Stop Recording
- Tap the STOP button.
- A dialog appears asking you to name the track.
- Enter a name (e.g., "Bar 10 Trail April 2026") and confirm.
- The track is saved as a GPX file in your device storage.
5.4 Viewing Saved Tracks
Toggle Tracks ON in the Display Panel to see your recorded tracks overlaid on the map. Tracks are stored in the Documents/my_tracks/ folder and can be exported via the system file manager.
6. Map Toolbar
Both the convoy map and Planning Map share a consistent toolbar at the top of the screen.
6.1 Navigation Button
The leftmost button provides one-tap navigation between maps:
- On the convoy map: "PLANNING MAP" -- tap to open the Planning Map.
- On the Planning Map: "CONVOY" -- tap to return to the convoy map.
6.2 Tile Source Buttons
Three buttons select the active tile source:
- SAT: Esri World Imagery -- satellite/aerial photography with road and place name labels.
- TOPO: Esri World Topo Map -- topographic map with terrain shading and labels.
- TOPO+: Esri USA Topo Maps -- USGS-style topographic maps with contour lines.
The active source is highlighted. Tap any source to switch. Both online display and offline tiles switch together.
6.3 NET / LOCAL Toggle
- NET (green): tiles loaded from the internet in real-time. Requires data connection.
- LOCAL (blue): tiles loaded from downloaded cache on device. Works offline.
Switch to LOCAL before entering areas without cell service.
7. Display Panel
The Display Panel is a floating, draggable toggle panel that controls map layer visibility. It appears on both maps with the same options.
7.1 Opening and Closing
- Tap the "DISPLAY" pill to expand the panel and show all toggles.
- Tap "DISPLAY" again to collapse it back to a small pill.
7.2 Dragging
Touch and hold the Display Panel, then drag it to any position on the map.
7.3 Layer Toggles
8. Download Queue
Map tile downloads run in the background. You can queue multiple areas, close GroupTrack, and downloads continue. A notification in your system tray shows progress.
8.1 How It Works
- When you confirm a download in the Planning Map, it enters the download queue.
- The queue processes up to 2 downloads simultaneously.
- Each download fetches tiles for all active tile sources and their overlay layers.
- Downloads survive app backgrounding, screen rotation, and device sleep.
- If the app is killed or the device restarts, pending downloads resume automatically.
8.2 Notification
- A persistent notification appears in your system tray during downloads.
- Shows the download label, tile count, and progress percentage.
- Includes a CANCEL button to stop the download.
- Completion notification appears when a download finishes.
8.3 Queue Panel
The queue panel appears at the bottom of the Planning Map when downloads are active. Tap the summary pill to expand and see active downloads with progress bars, queued items, and completed downloads. CANCEL ALL and CLEAR DONE buttons provide quick queue management.
8.4 Download Visibility on the Map
Toggle "Downloads in Progress" in the Display Panel to see your download status directly on the map:
- Blue shading: tiles already downloaded and on disk.
- Pink shading: areas currently being downloaded or queued for download.
- Red shading: areas queued for tile removal (V2.5).
No more guessing what's covered -- it's visible at a glance on both maps.
9. Radio Configuration
GroupTrack includes tools to configure your Meshtastic radio for convoy use. The recommended settings are LONG_FAST mode, which provides 3-5 mile range with good data throughput for position updates.
- Apply Radio: applies the convoy master configuration to your connected radio.
- Archive/Restore: backs up your current radio settings before applying convoy config, and restores them after the ride.
WARNING: All riders in a convoy must be on the same frequency, channel, and modem preset. Use the Apply Radio feature to ensure consistent configuration.
10. Known Limitations (V2.4)
11. Troubleshooting
Track file is empty (0 track points)
Location permission is set to "While using the app" instead of "Allow all the time." Go to Settings > Apps > GroupTrack > Permissions > Location > Allow all the time.
Map is blank / no tiles visible
Offline tiles have not been downloaded for this area. Open the Planning Map, search for your area, and use DOWNLOAD to cache tiles. Also confirm that All Files Access permission is granted.
Radio won't connect
Ensure the radio is powered on and within 50 feet. Try toggling Bluetooth off and on. If using a budget tablet, try a different Android device.
Radio connects then disconnects
Usually caused by firmware version mismatch. Verify your T1000-E is on firmware 2.6.11. If updated past 2.6.11, contact GroupTrack team for downgrade instructions.
Speed stuck at 0 mph
Wait 60 seconds after starting a ride. If speed remains at 0 after 2 minutes, check that location permission is set to "Allow all the time."
App killed in background
Go to Settings > Battery > find GroupTrack > set to "Unrestricted." This prevents Android from killing the app when the screen is off.
Download is slow or stalling
Use a strong WiFi connection. Reduce the zoom level for faster downloads. Downloads process 2 at a time -- check the queue panel for status.
Downloads not visible on map
Toggle "Downloaded Areas" ON in the Display Panel to see blue shading where tiles are cached. Toggle "Downloads in Progress" for pink shading of active downloads.

GroupTrack V2.4 User Manual  |  May 2026  |  grouptrack.org
Item | Details
Android device | Android 10 or later. Tested on Samsung and Pixel devices.
Meshtastic radio | T1000-E. Must be on firmware 2.6.11. Must be on the same frequency and channel as other riders.
Bluetooth | The app connects to the radio via Bluetooth Low Energy (BLE).
Map tiles | Download offline map tiles over WiFi before your ride using the Planning Map.

Tracks | Show/hide your recorded GPS tracks on the map.
Trails | Show/hide trail data (currently Utah St. George area OHV trails).
Waypoints | Coming in V2.5. Will toggle user-created waypoints.
Routes | Coming in V2.5. Will toggle planned routes.

Budget device BLE | Low-cost tablets (Tab8NEU, etc.) cannot hold BLE connection to T1000-E. Use a name-brand Android phone.

=== GroupTrack_V24_WorkPlan_Apr29.docx ===




GroupTrack V2.4
Wednesday April 29, 2026
Work Plan — Problem Definitions & Repair Strategies

Goal: Complete all fixes, rebuild AAB, publish to Google Play Store

Prepared: April 28, 2026 — Confidential


Task Schedule Overview
All tasks are sequenced by priority. Track display reliability is the gating issue — all other work depends on a working track pipeline. Google Play submission closes the day.


Total estimated time: approximately 4.5 hours

Task 1: Remove 0.25mi Segment Jump Rejection
Problem Definition
ConvoyViewModel.kt contains a distance filter that rejects any track segment longer than 0.25 miles. When a radio drops and reconnects, the lead cart has moved beyond this threshold. The reconnection segment is silently discarded with a log message "LEAD SEGMENT JUMP REJECTED". This creates visible gaps in the track display.
The filter exists on two code paths:
Lead-only track: line 593 — if (segDist > 0.25f) { ... rejected }
Multi-cart track: line 617 — identical filter for all carts
Field test evidence: 5 radios covering 14 carts with long distance gaps between radios. Every radio dropout exceeding 0.25 miles of travel created a permanent gap in the displayed track.

Repair Strategy
Delete the distance check and rejection block at line 593 (lead track path)
Delete the distance check and rejection block at line 617 (multi-cart path)
Always create the segment regardless of distance
Keep the convoyLog for debugging but remove the skip logic

Design principle: Every segment draws, always. No exceptions. Angular lines through curves will be smoothed in V2.5. First priority is a complete, continuous track.

Files Modified
ConvoyViewModel.kt — lines 590-596 (lead path), lines 614-618 (multi path)

Task 2: Cart Position Substitution for Silent Carts
Problem Definition
When a cart drops from the node list (radio disconnect, out of range, interference), the tick() function in ConvoyViewModel.kt stops accumulating trail segments for that cart. The track display freezes at the last known position and no new segments are drawn until the cart reappears.
Current code at line 577-578:
val leadNode = if (_leadLockedFlag) state.lead else null
if (leadNode != null) { ... }  // null on disconnect = nothing draws
When state.lead is null, the entire trail accumulation block is skipped. The track goes dead.

Field test evidence: Lead cart track stopped recording/displaying whenever the radio dropped. With 5 radios spread across 14 carts and long distances, this happened frequently. Tracks were always broken despite continuous GPS availability on the device.

Repair Strategy
Universal rule for ALL carts, not just the lead:
If a cart is not reporting, set its position to whichever is further along: its own last known position OR the furthest active cart behind it.

Implementation steps:
When state.lead is null but lockedLeadNodeId is set, do not skip the trail block
Use lastLeadLat / lastLeadLon as the current anchor (already tracked)
Check other active carts in the node list — find any cart whose position is further along than the lead's last known position
"Further along" = greater haversine distance from the first trackpoint, or simply further in the direction of travel
If an active cart is beyond the lead's last position, substitute that cart's position as the new trackpoint for the trail line
When lead cart reappears in the node list, resume using its actual position — draw connecting segment regardless of distance (Task 1 already ensures this)
Apply the same logic to the multi-cart path (line 607+) for all carts in the convoy

Key constraints:
Lead assignment NEVER changes — lead stays locked to cart 1 always
Only the position source temporarily falls back to nearest active cart
Track lines never go backwards — substituted position must be further along than last known
The tick engine already tracks all cart positions — data is available, just not being used for trail building

Files Modified
ConvoyViewModel.kt — tick() function, lines 575-635

Task 3: Test Track Display with Radio Disconnect
Test Plan
Build debug APK with Tasks 1-2 applied
Install on Android 1: adb -s 8624SBCEDF00001789 install -r -d [apk]
Start track recording with radio connected
Verify track draws continuously while driving
Power off the T1000-E radio mid-ride
Continue driving — verify track continues drawing using GPS/substituted position
Power radio back on — verify track connects through with no gap
Stop recording — verify GPX file has continuous trackpoints throughout
Check convoy log for any JUMP REJECTED messages (should be zero)

Success criteria: Continuous track line from start to finish with zero gaps, regardless of radio state changes.

Task 4: Auto-Pause GPS Recording
Problem Definition
The GPS LocationListener has a 5-meter minimum distance filter. When stationary (lunch stop, staging area), no positions fire and no trackpoints are written. This creates time gaps in the GPX file. The April 27 field test showed a 1 hour 23 minute gap at a lunch stop — the recording was running but no points were being written.
Users also restart recording to reassign the lead cart or recover from a perceived issue, creating multiple short GPX files that must be manually stitched together to reconstruct a full day's route.

Repair Strategy
Track the timestamp of the last GPS position received in ConvoyGpsService
On each LocationListener callback, check elapsed time since last position
If no position change for 60 seconds (1 minute), log auto-pause but keep the GPX file open
When the next position arrives after the pause, auto-resume — write the new trackpoint directly
The GPX file draws a straight line from the pause point to the resume point — this is correct behavior (lunch stop to departure)
No new temp file is created — single continuous GPX per session

The auto-pause is transparent to the user. Recording indicator stays active. No stop/start cycle needed.

Files Modified
ConvoyGpsService.kt — LocationListener callback (~line 244), add lastPositionTimestamp tracking

Task 5: GPX Combine — Select Multiple Tracks, Merge as One
Problem Definition
Field testing required multiple stop/start recording cycles during a single day. The April 26 route produced 2 separate GPX files (abc and bar_10) that together represent the complete route. Currently there is no way to select multiple tracks and combine them into a single GPX file for sharing or analysis.
April 27 had similar fragmentation — the full day's route was split across multiple recordings due to app restarts and lead cart reassignment.

Repair Strategy
In the track picker list, allow multi-select mode (checkboxes instead of single-tap load)
Add a COMBINE button next to ALL / FIT / CLEAR
When COMBINE is tapped, read all selected GPX files
Sort trackpoints by timestamp across all files
Write a new combined GPX file with all trackpoints in chronological order as a single <trkseg>
Prompt user for a file name for the combined track
Save to my_tracks directory and refresh the list

Date grouping enhancement: Add a date filter next to the existing controls. Tap a date to show only tracks from that day. Makes it easy to select all recordings from a field test day and combine them.

Files Modified
ConvoyScreen.kt — track picker UI (~line 1040+), add multi-select and COMBINE button
ConvoyScreen.kt — new function convoyMergeTracks() for GPX combining

Task 6: Debug Track List Slow Opening
Problem Definition
Tapping the TRACKS button shows "0 of 0" for 30-60 seconds before displaying the file list. The directory contains 31 files (10 displayed after filtering). The listing function (convoyListTracks) runs on Dispatchers.IO and only reads file names — it does not parse file contents. Despite this, the display is severely delayed.
Observations:
GeoJSON trails are already loaded and displayed before TRACKS is tapped — IO dispatcher is not competing with GeoJSON loading
One trashed file in the directory is 31MB — filesystem stat on this file may contribute
450,000 tile files at z18 may cause filesystem inode pressure affecting all IO operations
Individual track loading after selection is fast — only the initial name listing is slow

Repair Strategy
Add timing logs to convoyListTracks: log timestamp before and after listFiles(), filter, sort
Add timing log around the MainScope launch and withContext callback
Run app and tap TRACKS — check logcat for exact timing of each operation
If listFiles() is fast but UI update is delayed — issue is Compose recomposition or main thread blocking
If listFiles() is slow — issue is filesystem pressure from tile directories; consider caching the file list
Clean up trashed/temp files to reduce directory enumeration load

Diagnostic Commands
adb -s 8624SBCEDF00001789 logcat -v time | grep -i "convoyListTracks\|track.*list\|track.*time"

Task 7: Debug ANR "Cancel or Wait" on App Open
Problem Definition
The app displays an Application Not Responding dialog ("Cancel or Wait") on startup. This occurs before any radio is connected. It happens even after a device reboot. Tapping Cancel force-kills the app, which can interrupt any active GPS recording session (foreground service dies with the app process).
The ANR dialog appears when the main (UI) thread is blocked for more than 5 seconds. Something during app initialization is performing heavy work on the main thread.

Likely candidates:
GeoJSON trail overlay loading — 5,724 features across 8 Utah counties parsed from bundled assets
SharedPreferences initialization or migration logic
WebView initialization (Chromium cold start)
Tile directory enumeration on first map render
MeshService binding and initialization

Repair Strategy
Clear logcat, launch app fresh, capture ANR trace:
adb -s 8624SBCEDF00001789 logcat -c
# Launch app, wait for ANR dialog
adb -s 8624SBCEDF00001789 logcat -d | grep -iE "ANR in|Input dispatching|Slow|BlockMonitor" | head -20

Pull the ANR traces file for detailed stack trace:
adb -s 8624SBCEDF00001789 shell cat /data/anr/traces.txt | head -100

The stack trace will show exactly which method is blocking the main thread
Move the blocking operation to a coroutine on Dispatchers.IO or a background Thread
If it is GeoJSON loading: move to background thread, show map without trails, add trails once loaded
If it is WebView init: already unavoidable on first launch, consider splash screen to mask it

Files Modified
TBD — depends on diagnostic results. Likely ConvoyScreen.kt or MainActivity.kt

Task 8: Show Downloads Performance Fixes
Problem Definition
The Show Downloaded Areas button triggers a filesystem scan to determine the bounding rectangle of downloaded tile regions. The scan uses listFiles() recursively through tile directories.
ConvoyScreen.kt line 512 scans zoom level 18, which contains approximately 450,000 tile entries. This takes 4+ minutes. Lines 1243 (ConvoyScreen) and 472 (MapViewerScreen) already use z14 (76 directories, 1-2 seconds).
Additional issues: no loading feedback, button can be tapped multiple times stacking scan threads, no way to cancel a running scan.

Repair Strategy
Four sub-fixes:
Change z18 to z14 at line 512 and update tile path to "SAT/14" — reduces scan from 450K entries to 76 directories
Add a loading spinner (CircularProgressIndicator) visible while scan thread is running
Disable the button (or set a flag to ignore taps) while scan is in progress
Store the scan Thread reference; before starting a new scan, interrupt the existing one and check an isScanning flag inside the forEach loop to bail out early

Files Modified
ConvoyScreen.kt — line 512 (z level + path), line ~510 (spinner state), line ~508 (button disable)

Tasks 9-11: Build, Publish, and Verify
Task 9: Rebuild AAB
After all fixes are applied and tested:
Commit all changes: git add -A && git commit -m "V2.4 field test fixes"
Build release AAB:
./gradlew bundleGoogleRelease -x uploadCrashlyticsMappingFileGoogleRelease
Copy to Downloads:
cp app/build/outputs/bundle/googleRelease/app-google-release.aab "/c/Users/kixaz/Downloads/GroupTrack_v2.4_final.aab"

Task 10: Play Store Submission
Open https://play.google.com/console
Click GroupTrack app

=== GroupTrack_V25_Backlog.docx ===
GroupTrack V2.5 — Backlog and Task Planning
Captured 2026-05-07. Carry forward into V2.5 development planning.
Purpose
V2.5 follows the V2.4 Play Store release. The strategic goal is twofold: (1) build out the standalone product (no-radio, solo-rider) to full feature parity with Gaia GPS and onX, expanding GroupTrack's audience to riders who don't yet own a Meshtastic radio; (2) lay the architectural foundation for national trail coverage and cloud-synced tile/track distribution. V2.5 is the bridge from "convoy-tracking app for radio owners" to "free GPS tracker for any rider, with convoy capability when a radio is added."
Items below are organized by theme. Priorities for the V2.5 release scope are flagged. Architecture-level work is the longest-running.
Architecture — National Trail Model
Replaces the current 22 MB Utah GeoJSON with a unified GeoPackage architecture and three engines that handle ingestion, distribution, and local display.
Components
Unified GeoPackage file: grouptrack.gpkg, replaces the 22 MB Utah GeoJSON entirely.
Ingestion engine: pulls from authoritative sources (UGRC, BLM, USFS, etc.) on a per-region basis. Outputs into the GeoPackage.
Distribution engine: serves regions to clients. Region-based downloads paired with the same region's tile set.
Local display engine: efficient query and render of GeoPackage data on Android. Replaces current GeoJSON loader.
Rollout plan
Utah pilot first — replaces existing 5,732-trail Utah GeoJSON with GeoPackage equivalent. Validates the pipeline end-to-end.
Expand to neighboring states (AZ, NV, ID, CO) once Utah is stable.
National coverage as Phase 3 of V2.5.
This is the longest single piece of V2.5 work. Multiple weeks, not days.
Track Display Selector — Filter Tabs and In-Progress Files
Captured tonight: extend the track display selector on ConvoyScreen.kt and ConvoyMapViewerScreen.kt (the on/off toggle for track overlays on the map) to include in-progress files alongside saved tracks.
Filter tabs: All / Saved / In-Progress (mirroring the Work With Tracks pattern).
Reuses ConvoyTrackOps.isInProgress() predicate that already exists. No new file ops logic.
In-progress files become selectable AND renameable from the selector — solves the "the only way to rename unsaved files is to display them" problem.
UI work only. Backend already complete.
Track Import — Move to Map Viewer
V2.4 will ship with track import in the convoy map's MAP SETTINGS submenu (alongside Export). V2.5 moves it to the Map Viewer settings menu, aligning with the broader V2.5 architecture: Map Viewer becomes the hub for ALL track management — display, import, export, email, file management.
Remove "Import Tracks from Downloads" from ConvoySubMenu.kt MAP SETTINGS
Add to Map Viewer settings menu
Same composable, different menu placement. Small refactor.
Standalone Product Polish — Gaia/onX Feature Parity
V2.4 introduces standalone mode functionally. V2.5 polishes it to feel like a finished product for the solo rider.
Higher-resolution GPS recording — 1-second interval option (currently fixed at 3s)
Mileage / trip odometer display — referenced in V2.4 known limitations as "planned for future"
Speed warmup tightening — currently shows 0 mph for ~60 seconds while GPS samples accumulate
Track stats display — distance, elevation gain, moving time, max speed (post-ride summary)
Waypoint marking on the map (POIs along a ride)
Solo rider onboarding flow — first-launch tutorial that doesn't assume a radio
Marketing copy on splash / about screen positioning the app as standalone-friendly
Map Manager Phase C
Substantial map system work captured in earlier sessions. Listed here for completeness; full design exists in earlier docs.

GroupTrack V2.5 Session Handoff
Ready to Build | May 10, 2026
Upload this document + codebase files to start next session. Build MapSourceManager.kt first.
1. Project Context
Project: GroupTrack - Android convoy tracking / off-road navigation app
Codebase: Meshtastic Android fork, branch feature/convoy-event-ride

2. Immediate Task: Build MapSourceManager.kt
Create MapSourceManager.kt that reads map_sources.json from assets and serves tile URLs. This replaces all hardcoded tile source logic. The patch script must modify these files:

File 1: ConvoyConfig.kt - Remove hardcoded sources
Current hardcoded constants to remove/replace:

    "SAT" to "https://services.arcgisonline.com/.../World_Imagery/.../tile/{z}/{y}/{x}",
    "TOPO" to "https://services.arcgisonline.com/.../World_Topo_Map/.../tile/{z}/{y}/{x}",
    "TOPO+" to "https://server.arcgisonline.com/.../USA_Topo_Maps/.../tile/{z}/{y}/{x}",

These become lookups into MapSourceManager. TILE_SOURCES, ACTIVE_TILE_SOURCE, ESRI_LABELS_URL, and ESRI_TRANSPORT_URL all replaced.

File 2: ConvoyMapViewerScreen.kt - Replace hardcoded tileSources
Line 109: Hardcoded tileSources list:

    Triple("SAT", "Satellite", "https://services.arcgisonline.com/.../World_Imagery/..."),
    Triple("TOPO", "Topo", "https://services.arcgisonline.com/.../World_Topo_Map/..."),
    Triple("TOPO+", "Topo+", "https://server.arcgisonline.com/.../USA_Topo_Maps/...")
)

if (url.contains("/Reference/World_Transportation/MapServer/tile/")) { ... SAT_LABELS_TRANSPORT ... }
if (url.contains("/Reference/World_Boundaries_and_Places/MapServer/tile/")) { ... SAT_LABELS_PLACES ... }
Replace with generic interceptor that reads cache_dir from the source layers array in JSON. Iterates all overlay layers for the active source, matches URL patterns dynamically.

File 3: ConvoyTileDownloader.kt - Replace hardcoded SAT logic
Line ~118: Hardcoded SAT label download:
if (sourceName == "SAT") {
    val labelUrls = listOf(
        Pair("SAT_LABELS_TRANSPORT", ConvoyConfig.ESRI_TRANSPORT_URL),

    )
    // downloads overlay tiles...

Replace with generic layer iteration: read all layers for the source from MapSourceManager, download each layer to its cache_dir. No source-specific if-statements.

4. V2.5 Build Sequence
Phase 1A: Data-Drive Tile Sources (current focus)
1. MapSourceManager.kt - reads map_sources.json, serves tile URLs, handles multi-layer sources
2. Clone MapViewerScreen into standalone PlanningMapScreen with own launcher
3. Source picker UI - long-press map bar heading to change slot assignment
4. Multi-layer download from JSON layers array (generic, no source-specific code)
5. Source validation - connection test, API key test, tile format test, render preview
6. Source maintenance screen (developer long-press)
7. JSON maintenance - add/edit/validate sources, export updated JSON

Phase 1B: Data-Drive Trails
1. Kotlin ingestion engine (ConvoyTrailOps.kt) porting Python script
2. Trail source catalog with user-selectable filters (OHV default)
3. Download full sources from agency URLs, ingest on-device
4. Viewport queries lazy load trails to map, replace GeoJSON asset loader
5. Dedup on ingestion with review screen

Phase 2: Waypoints
1. Reclaim long-press for waypoint creation, add RIDERS button
2. Waypoint type picker (10 types), alias model with proximity check
3. Map legend from waypoint_types table

Phase 3: Tracks and Routes
1. Track alias model matching waypoints. Delete through aliases.
2. Track rename cascading to route name via primary alias
3. Route creation (draw, snap-to-trail, convert from track, import)

Phase 4: Area Management (Later)
Viewport zoom defines download areas. Block-based queued downloads. Smart first pull guided by trail coverage.

5. Key Architecture Decisions (Settled)
5.1 Databases
grouptrack_trails.db: Public agency trail data. SQLite. Read-only. Downloaded on-demand. FREE.
grouptrack_user.spatialite: Personal data. SpatiaLite. Read-write. Waypoints, routes, tracks, tile sources, journal.

5.2 Entity Models
Waypoint identity: Coordinates (lon/lat). Names are aliases in waypoint_aliases table. Rename creates alias. Delete removes alias. Waypoint deleted when no aliases remain.
Track identity: GPX/KML file. Names in track_aliases. Same alias/delete model as waypoints. Primary alias rename cascades to route name.
Route: Never independently shared. Sharing controlled by configurable app_setting.
Trail: Never individually deleted. Source/region removal only. Display toggle, not deletion.
Area delete: Removes tiles only (biggest storage). Spatial data stays.

5.3 Ownership and Delete
Creator delete cascades to collective (V3.0). Non-creator delete removes local alias only. Local deletes do not impact shared data unless removing something I created.

5.4 Sync Model
Offline-first. All local changes queue outbound in strict chronological order. Local DB only updated during explicit user-initiated data pulls. One process syncs all downloaded areas.

5.5 Data Protection
Three layers: automatic backup (launch + pre-destructive, last 5), change journal (every INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE with before/after JSON + geometry WKT, 30-day retention), rollback (single-op undo, transaction, point-in-time).

5.6 Map Source Model
map_sources.json defines all sources with layers, validation rules, cache dirs. Code is generic - reads JSON, executes rules. No source-specific logic. User can add custom sources after validation passes.

5.7 GroupTrack as Awareness Conduit
Not a data provider. Catalog of public URLs at grouptrack.org. Nightly cron checks for source updates (HEAD requests). Users download from agencies directly. Each device unique to rider needs.

6. Schema Reference
grouptrack_user_schema_v2.sql is the hardened schema with 20 tables/views and 24 lifecycle trigger placeholders. Every trigger has exact business rules documented in SQL comments with source quotes. Schema committed to docs/.

Key tables: tile_sources, map_slots, source_layers, waypoints, waypoint_aliases, waypoint_types, routes, tracks, track_aliases, ride_areas, source_ingestions, transfer_queue, change_journal, backup_log, data_log, dedup_log, app_settings.

Key rules in triggers: waypoint proximity check (25m), no waypoint moves, rename creates alias, delete through aliases with orphan cleanup, track name cascade to route, area delete = tiles only, share validation against ride.private, route sharing controlled by app_settings, outbound sync queuing.

7. Standing Rules (20)
1. Never auto-delete user data. 2. Never embed API keys. 3. Share is opt-in. 4. Offline-first. 5. Canvas independence. 6. Cumulative collection. 7. Routes never independently public. 8. Private rides block sharing. 9. Published content recoverable. 10. Trails/routes never individually removed. 11. First source wins dedup. 12. Ops/Dialogs pattern. 13. Every change journaled. 14. Backup before destructive ops. 15. Journal stores before+after+WKT. 16. Schema before code. 17. Triggers before features. 18. Patch scripts uniquely versioned. 19. Never commit flags=true. 20. Preserve geometry precision.
8. Hardware Restrictions
1. T1000-E firmware: LOCK to 2.6.11 max. Newer destabilizes BLE + radio config.
2. Radio stays in cart during rides. Keep within 50ft BLE range. Exceeding causes supervision timeout pauses.

2. Download Queue Architecture
2.1 Components
2.2 Key Design Decisions
WorkManager for background execution -- downloads survive app backgrounding, screen rotation, device sleep.
Max 2 concurrent downloads. Queue processes in priority order.
Progress updates: StateFlow only, no disk I/O. Status changes (complete/failed/cancelled) save to JSON.
Notification update interval: every 250 tiles (was 50). Reduces system overhead.
Queue panel at BottomStart, 280dp wide, clear of download panel at BottomEnd.
Esri tiles confirmed -- zero Google URLs in codebase. Download URLs verified on device.
3. Download Queue Priority -- Design Rule

Source refresh trigger: when user changes a tile source for a map slot, app detects existing downloaded regions. Prompts: 'Refresh to new source?' If yes, queues priority-7 refresh entries. New downloads (priority 3) always service before refreshes (priority 7). Refresh may take days. This is user-triggered, not automatic. Respects Standing Rule 1.

GroupTrack V2.4 / V2.5
Session Handoff Document
May 12, 2026 | Handoff for May 16+ Session

1. Current Build State
The V2.4 release build compiles successfully but has one critical blocker: the Tracks display toggle crashes the app when toggled ON because convoyLoadTrack() reads 68 GPX/KML files synchronously on the main thread, causing an ANR kill.

Last committed build: 0c9d67953 (before tracks load-all)
This build is stable and deployable. All features work except the tracks display toggle (which was not wired to load tracks at that point).

Latest build (uncommitted): tracks load-all + grey-out fix
After the crash was discovered, the Tracks toggle was greyed out as V2.5. A build is running with this fix. If successful, this is the release candidate.

1.1 What Works (Confirmed by Testing)
1.2 What Does NOT Work (Blockers)
1.3 UI Polish Issues (Non-blocking)
2. Tracks Display Toggle - Detailed Fix Plan
2.1 Root Cause
The app has TWO separate track systems in JavaScript:
- trackLines[] - live GPS recording track, drawn by drawTrack(), toggled by toggleTracks()
- importedTracks{} - saved track files loaded by loadTrackFile(), toggled by toggleTrackFiles() (Planning Map) or showTracks()/hideTracks() (Convoy Map, after our JS fix)

The crash occurs because convoyLoadTrack() in ConvoyScreen.kt reads files synchronously:
fun convoyLoadTrack(context, fileName, color, webView) {
    val text = file.readText()  // BLOCKS MAIN THREAD
    val coords = convoyParseGpx(text)  // BLOCKS MAIN THREAD
    webView?.evaluateJavascript("loadTrackFile(...)", null)
}

With 68 files, this blocks the main thread for 60+ seconds, triggering Android's ANR watchdog which kills the app.
2.2 Fix: Async Track Loading
The fix is to wrap all file I/O in a coroutine on Dispatchers.IO, then post the JavaScript call back to Main:

ConvoyScreen.kt tracks toggle callback:
onTracksToggle = {
    tracksVisible = !tracksVisible
    if (tracksVisible) {
        if (tracksLoaded) {
            webViewRef.value?.evaluateJavascript("showTracks()", null)
        } else {
            tracksLoaded = true
            kotlinx.coroutines.MainScope().launch {
                val dir = java.io.File(Environment.getExternalStoragePublicDirectory(
                    Environment.DIRECTORY_DOCUMENTS), "my_tracks")
                val files = dir.listFiles()?.map { it.name }?.sorted() ?: emptyList()
                files.forEachIndexed { idx, name ->
                    val color = colors[idx % colors.size]
                    withContext(Dispatchers.IO) {
                        val text = File(dir, name).readText()
                        val coords = if (name.endsWith(".gpx")) convoyParseGpx(text)
                            else convoyParseKml(text)
                        val json = coords.joinToString(",", "[", "]") { ... }
                        withContext(Dispatchers.Main) {
                            webViewRef.value?.evaluateJavascript(
                                "loadTrackFile('$name', '$json', '$color')", null)
                        }
                    }
                }
            }
        }
    } else {
        webViewRef.value?.evaluateJavascript("hideTracks()", null)
    }
}

The same pattern applies to ConvoyMapViewerScreen.kt using loadTrackOnMap() which already uses MainScope().launch with Dispatchers.IO (see line 693). The Planning Map version should work correctly once wired because loadTrackOnMap is already async.

Key difference: loadTrackOnMap() in MapViewerScreen is already async (uses MainScope().launch + Dispatchers.IO). convoyLoadTrack() in ConvoyScreen is synchronous. Either make convoyLoadTrack async, or use loadTrackOnMap's pattern.
