=== GroupTrack_V25_SpatialArchitecture_v1.docx ===
GroupTrack V2.5
Spatial Architecture Planning
Decisions captured May 9, 2026 | Do not re-litigate
1. Architecture overview
GroupTrack separates map canvas (tile imagery) from spatial data (trails, waypoints, routes, tracks). The canvas is user-configurable — riders pick their own tile sources and manage their own API keys. GroupTrack never embeds, distributes, or licenses tile source keys. The spatial data layer is GroupTrack's product.

1.1 Two databases
grouptrack_trails.spatialite — shipped asset, free, public. Official agency trail data (UGRC, BLM, USFS). Read-only on device. Lazy loads to map viewport via spatial index. Always available, no download step, no area boundaries. Updated when GroupTrack ships a new release. This is what gets riders in the door.
grouptrack_user.spatialite — rider's data, read-write. Waypoints, routes, tracks collected through rider activity. Downloaded via ride enrollment in V3.0. Shared through the ride engine when subscription activates. Survives app updates and uninstalls (stored in /sdcard/Documents/GroupTrack/data/).

1.2 Canvas independence
GroupTrack ships 12 default tile sources: 3 satellite, 3 topo, 3 road, 3 trail. All free, no API key required (except Thunderforest Outdoors which has a free key). Users add their own sources — CalTopo, Gaia, onX subscribers enter their own URL template and API key. If a tile source goes offline or changes pricing, the user deletes or updates the entry. GroupTrack is completely out of the tile sourcing business.
The Esri migration question (V25 Backlog Seq 19) drops in priority permanently. Canvas selection is a user preference, not a GroupTrack dependency.

1.3 Design principles
Offline-first: all spatial data downloads to the device before the rider leaves connectivity. No lazy loading in the field except trails (which are always local).
Cumulative collection: rider's local database grows over time. Primary area download plus ride enrollments add data. Nothing removed without explicit user action.
No auto-delete: NEVER programmatically delete user files. All deletes require explicit user action with confirmation.
Share is opt-in: the 'share' flag on tracks, waypoints, and routes is always a deliberate user action, never automatic.

2. Map overlay model
2.1 Visual patterns
Blue fill: tiles downloaded for this area. Existing behavior, unchanged from V2.4.
Black diagonal stripes: waypoints, routes, and tracks present for this area. New in V2.5.
Blue + black stripes: complete offline coverage — both tiles and spatial data.
Stripes only (no blue): spatial data present but no offline tiles — rider has trail-related data but map imagery loads from network only.
One area outline serves both tile and spatial boundaries. The same drawn polygon defines both coverages.

2.2 Show controls
Both Planning Map and convoy map display the coverage overlays. Two toggle buttons: 'Tiles' and 'Spatial' (labeled as waypoints, routes, tracks). Rider can show one or both. Convoy map shows only — no create, no delete. Planning Map is the management hub.

2.3 Area management — Planning Map only
Five actions, all on Planning Map (renamed from Map Viewer in V2.5): Draw area (polygon on map), Download (tiles and/or spatial for drawn area), Refresh (re-download updated data for existing area), Remove (delete tiles and/or spatial within drawn area), Queue (view transfer manager).
All download and area management functionality is removed from convoy map in V2.5. Convoy map is purely operational — it shows coverage overlays, renders trails, displays riders, and records tracks. No downloads, no area editing, no queue access. Clean HUD for ride time.



=== GroupTrack_V3_Architecture_Plan.docx ===
GroupTrack
V3.0 Architecture & Migration Plan
Monday Planning Document  |  March 28, 2026  |  AWS Backend Integration
Executive Summary
V3.0 migrates GroupTrack from a fully offline standalone app to a cloud-assisted platform where the AWS backend handles ride creation, user enrollment, and invite distribution while the core mesh radio operation remains entirely offline and unchanged.

The migration strategy is built around one principle: the radio never needs a cloud connection. Everything before the ride (enrollment, ride creation, invite, map download) can use the cloud. Everything during the ride uses the mesh. The app must work transparently in both states.



1. Current JSON Entity Inventory
These are the data entities that exist today as local JSON files. Each must be mapped to the V3.0 hosted model.

1.1 convoy_ride — the .convoy file
Location: app/convoy_events/{UUID}.json


MIGRATION: Rides created locally in V2.4 are recreated in V3.0 by posting to POST /rides. The eventId UUID is preserved as rides.ride_id so existing .convoy files remain valid. No data loss.

1.2 master_config.json
Location: app/src/main/assets/master_config.json — baked into APK. 47 radio config fields across 6 groups. Read-only.

V3.0 STATUS: Unchanged. Stays in assets. Rides only override channel_name and channel_psk. Everything else is master config.

1.3 convoy_apply_list.json
Location: app/src/main/assets/ — defines which fields get written during Apply Radio.
V3.0 STATUS: Unchanged. Stays in assets. Not user-configurable, not ride-specific.

1.4 master.cfg (binary)
Location: app/src/main/assets/ — Meshtastic DeviceProfile binary. 38 fields. Built from master_config.json.
V3.0 STATUS: Unchanged. Not hosted in RDS.

1.5 WorkingConfig (in-memory)
Not persisted. Built at runtime by merging master_config.json + ride JSON + radio reads.


1.6 Organizer Profile (SharedPreferences)


2. V3.0 RDS Schema — Stage 1 Tables
Four tables required for V3.0 launch. Create Monday before any Android work.

2.1 users

2.2 rides

2.3 invites

2.4 enrollments

2.5 SQL — Create Schema
Run from EC2 SSH session:
mysql -h convoy-tracker-db.cudtjxrtdbql.us-east-1.rds.amazonaws.com -u convoy_admin -p convoy_tracker

CREATE TABLE users (
  user_id CHAR(36) PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT (UUID()),
  google_id VARCHAR(255) UNIQUE NOT NULL,
  email VARCHAR(255) UNIQUE NOT NULL,
  first_name VARCHAR(100) NOT NULL,
  last_name VARCHAR(100) NOT NULL,
  cell VARCHAR(20), zip_code VARCHAR(10),
  is_active BOOLEAN DEFAULT TRUE,
  expires_at DATETIME, created_at DATETIME DEFAULT NOW()
);

CREATE TABLE rides (
  ride_id CHAR(36) PRIMARY KEY,
  organizer_id CHAR(36) NOT NULL,
  ride_name VARCHAR(255) NOT NULL,
  channel_name VARCHAR(11) NOT NULL,
  channel_psk VARCHAR(512) NOT NULL,
  ride_date DATE NOT NULL, description TEXT,
  zip_code VARCHAR(10), is_public BOOLEAN DEFAULT FALSE,
  map_bounds_north DECIMAL(10,7), map_bounds_south DECIMAL(10,7),
  map_bounds_east DECIMAL(10,7), map_bounds_west DECIMAL(10,7),
  map_tile_source VARCHAR(10) DEFAULT "SAT", map_zoom_max INT DEFAULT 18,
  created_at DATETIME DEFAULT NOW(), expires_at DATETIME NOT NULL,
  FOREIGN KEY (organizer_id) REFERENCES users(user_id)
);

CREATE TABLE invites (
  invite_id CHAR(36) PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT (UUID()),
  ride_id CHAR(36) NOT NULL, token VARCHAR(64) UNIQUE NOT NULL,
  created_by CHAR(36) NOT NULL, claimed_by CHAR(36),
  claimed_at DATETIME, expires_at DATETIME NOT NULL,
  created_at DATETIME DEFAULT NOW(),
  FOREIGN KEY (ride_id) REFERENCES rides(ride_id),
  FOREIGN KEY (created_by) REFERENCES users(user_id)
);

CREATE TABLE enrollments (
  enrollment_id CHAR(36) PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT (UUID()),
  ride_id CHAR(36) NOT NULL, user_id CHAR(36) NOT NULL,
  callsign VARCHAR(50), vehicle_type VARCHAR(100),
  enrolled_at DATETIME DEFAULT NOW(),
  status ENUM("active","cancelled") DEFAULT "active",
  FOREIGN KEY (ride_id) REFERENCES rides(ride_id),
  FOREIGN KEY (user_id) REFERENCES users(user_id)
);


3. Authentication — Google Sign-In Strategy

3.1 Online vs Offline States

KEY PRINCIPLE: Never block radio operation on authentication state. If Google Sign-In fails or is unavailable, the mesh map, HUD, GPS tracking, and radio config all continue working using locally cached data.

IMPORTANT: The organizer developer settings gate (long press header) remains independent of Google Sign-In. Uses existing hardcoded password. Do not entangle these two auth systems.

3.2 Google Sign-In Flow

3.3 Off-Network Behavior


4. Radio Write Process — V3.0 Impact Assessment
The radio write engine is the most complex and proven component in GroupTrack. V3.0 must not change it. The only change is where the ride JSON originates.

4.1 Current Radio Write Flow — Proven in V2.4

V3.0 CHANGE: Stage 1 only. The ride JSON source changes from local file to downloaded JSON from RDS. WorkingConfig build, binary build, and all radio write steps are UNCHANGED.

4.2 Ride JSON Source Change

The local JSON cache is the bridge. Downloaded rides save to convoy_events/ exactly like .convoy imports. The radio write engine never knows the difference between a local and downloaded ride.


5. Invite Link Flow — End to End
5.1 Organizer Creates and Sends Invite

5.2 Rider Downloads and Uses Ride

6. Map Area — Ride Creation Dependency
The ride record in RDS stores the map bounding box set by the organizer during ride creation. When any rider downloads the ride via invite, the app automatically starts downloading tiles for that area. No manual selection required.


7. Future Entity Stubs
These do not exist yet but must be architected so they can be added in V3.x and V4.0 without rewriting V3.0 code.


V3.0 API design principle: every GET /ride/{token} response includes an empty extensions object. Future versions populate extensions with waypoints, org info, etc. V3.0 Android clients ignore unknown fields — forward compatible by design.

8. V3.0 Monday Build Order
PREREQUISITE: Resolve offline maps bug first — see GroupTrack_OfflineMaps_Debug.docx. The offline map pipeline must be proven working before automated map download is built.

User has signed in before (cached credentials) | App loads normally using cached user_id. Full offline operation.
User has never signed in | Shows offline banner: Sign in required to activate account. Organizer features locked. Existing local rides still work.
Google servers unreachable — user previously signed in | Same as cached — use local cache. No re-authentication required.

Major discovery: Existing database contains sophisticated features that current PHP API doesn't expose. This gap explains why V3.0 implementation appeared complex - advanced functionality already exists in database.

Location Services: user zip_code, latitude, longitude, default_radius_miles

Users: Only 6/23 fields used (basic profile only)
Rides: Only 6/23 fields used (basic ride creation only)
Enrollments: Only 3/7 fields used (missing callsign, vehicle, status workflow)
Overall: ~20% of database capability exposed via current API

Expand existing PHP endpoints rather than rebuild

3. Major Implementation Gap Analysis
IMPLEMENTATION GAP: PHP API vs Database Capability Mismatch
Current PHP API uses ~20% of available database fields. Sophisticated features like map bounds, trailhead selection, vehicle tracking, terms management, and location services are in database but not accessible via API.
3.1 Missing PHP API Functionality
Users API Gaps
Location services: zip_code, lat/lon, radius_miles not in /users/register
Terms management: 6 terms tracking fields not implemented
Subscription: expires_at field not managed
Account status: is_organizer, email_opt_in not settable
Push notifications: fcm_token not managed
Rides API Gaps
Map integration: 6 map_bounds fields not in /rides endpoint
Trailhead selection: trailhead_waypoint_id not implemented
Route planning: route_id not connected
Organization: org_id not implemented
Status management: ride_status enum not managed
Map configuration: map_tile_source, map_zoom_max not settable
Enrollments API Gaps
Vehicle tracking: callsign, vehicle_type not collected
Status workflow: enum supports 5 states, API uses binary logic
No enrollment management endpoints (accept/decline/cancel)
Invites API Gaps
Expiration: expires_at field exists but not managed in creation
Claiming: claimed_by, claimed_at tracking not implemented
Token management: field mismatch (invite_token vs token)

=== GroupTrack_V3_Implementation_Plan.docx ===
GroupTrack
V3.0 Implementation Plan — Separated Build Strategy
March 29, 2026  |  Branch: feature/convoy-event-ride  |  Gate: Wednesday Field Test
1. Executive Summary
GroupTrack V3.0 migrates from a fully offline standalone app to a cloud-assisted platform. The migration must be built in two completely isolated phases with a hard gate between them — V2.4 Wednesday field test confirmation — to ensure the proven offline radio engine is never destabilized by backend integration work.
The core principle: build everything that has no device dependency first. Schema, service layer, sign-in screen, and API wiring are all safe to build now. The single connection point — replacing the existing ride creation flow with an API-backed version — is the last thing wired, and only after Wednesday confirms V2.4 is solid.

2. Architecture — What Changes and What Doesn't
2.1 Unchanged in V3.0

2.2 What V3.0 Adds

2.3 The Single Connection Point
There is exactly one place where V3.0 wires into existing V2.4 code: the ride creation flow. In V2.4, Create a Ride saves a local JSON file. In V3.0, it POSTs to /rides and saves the response to local cache. Everything downstream — WorkingConfig, radio write, RIDE tab display — is identical because both versions produce the same local JSON.


=== GroupTrack_V3_MapEnhancements_v1.docx ===
GroupTrack
V3.0 Map Revisions & Enhancements
Comprehensive Map Feature Specification
April 19, 2026  |  v1  |  CONFIDENTIAL
1. Executive Summary
GroupTrack V3.0 introduces comprehensive map management enhancements that transform the platform from basic convoy coordination to advanced off-grid navigation and planning. These enhancements integrate V2.5 Map Manager capabilities with V3.0's cloud-enabled services, creating a sophisticated mapping platform for outdoor recreation.
1.1 Key Enhancements
V2.5 Map Manager integration with V3.0 online services
Dual map architecture: Planning Map and Convoy Map independence
Advanced ride creation with map bounds and trailhead selection
Route planning tools with community sharing capabilities
Enhanced offline mapping with tile management
GPX/KML import/export with metadata preservation
Map configuration management and tile source selection
1.2 Strategic Impact
These map enhancements position GroupTrack as a comprehensive off-grid navigation platform, distinguishing it from basic coordination tools. The integration creates network effects through route sharing while maintaining core offline capabilities for field operations.
Architecture Decision: Two independent map systems enable simultaneous planning and convoy operations without conflict. Users can plan routes while participating in active convoys.
2. V2.5 Map Manager Integration
The V2.5 Map Manager provides advanced mapping capabilities that integrate seamlessly with V3.0's cloud services while maintaining full offline functionality.
2.1 Architecture Overview
The V2.5 Map Manager operates as an independent mapping system accessible through V3.0's services menu. This separation ensures convoy operations remain unaffected while enabling sophisticated planning capabilities.
2.2 Database Integration
V2.5 Map Manager integrates with the existing database schema through specific field mappings that enable route planning to influence ride creation.
Route Storage Integration
-- V2.5 routes connect to V3.0 rides via route_id
UPDATE rides SET route_id = ? WHERE ride_id = ?;

-- Auto-calculate map bounds from route data  
UPDATE rides SET 
    map_bounds_north = ?, map_bounds_south = ?,
    map_bounds_east = ?, map_bounds_west = ?
WHERE ride_id = ?;
Waypoint Integration
-- Trailhead selection from V2.5 waypoint library
UPDATE rides SET trailhead_waypoint_id = ? WHERE ride_id = ?;

-- Waypoint types: trailhead, staging, hazard, POI
SELECT waypoint_id, name, type, latitude, longitude 
FROM waypoints WHERE type = 'trailhead' AND deleted_at IS NULL;
3. Dual Map Architecture
GroupTrack V3.0 implements two independent map systems to serve different use cases without operational conflicts.
3.1 Planning Map
The Planning Map provides comprehensive route planning, area exploration, and preparation tools. It operates independently of convoy activities and focuses on pre-trip planning.
Planning Map Capabilities
Full-screen map interface with advanced navigation tools
Route creation via waypoint placement and path drawing
Area selection for ride boundary definition
GPX/KML import with route preview and validation
Waypoint library management with categorization
Tile download management with progress tracking
Route sharing to community track library
Planning Map Entry Points
3.2 Convoy Map
The Convoy Map handles real-time convoy coordination during active rides. It focuses on operational awareness and participant tracking.
Convoy Map Capabilities
Real-time participant position tracking
Lead lock and convoy formation management
HUD overlay with convoy status information
GPS track recording for ride completion
Radio integration for mesh network communication
Offline operation for areas without cellular coverage
State Isolation: Planning and Convoy maps maintain completely separate state to prevent conflicts. Users can plan future routes while participating in current convoys.
4. Enhanced Ride Creation with Map Integration
V3.0 ride creation incorporates sophisticated map features that leverage the full database schema capabilities previously unused by the basic PHP API.
4.1 Map Bounds Integration
Ride creators can now define precise geographic boundaries for their events using interactive map tools.
Database Schema Utilization
-- Map bounds fields in rides table (previously unused)
map_bounds_north    DECIMAL(10,7),

map_bounds_east     DECIMAL(10,7),
map_bounds_west     DECIMAL(10,7),

-- Map configuration options
map_tile_source     VARCHAR(10) DEFAULT 'SAT',  -- SAT, TOPO, HYB
map_zoom_max        INT DEFAULT 18
Map Bounds Selection Methods
4.2 Trailhead Selection Integration
The ride creation process connects to the V2.5 waypoint library for consistent trailhead management across planning and operational phases.
Waypoint Integration Workflow
Access V2.5 waypoint library during ride creation
Filter waypoints by type (trailhead, staging, POI)
Preview waypoint details with coordinates and descriptions
Create new waypoints directly from ride creation interface
Auto-populate ride metadata from selected trailhead information
Database Integration
-- Trailhead selection updates rides table
UPDATE rides SET trailhead_waypoint_id = ? WHERE ride_id = ?;

-- Waypoint details accessible for ride display
SELECT w.name, w.latitude, w.longitude, w.description 
FROM rides r
JOIN waypoints w ON w.waypoint_id = r.trailhead_waypoint_id
WHERE r.ride_id = ?;

<gpx creator="GroupTrack V3.0" version="1.1">
  <metadata>
    <name>Desert Loop Adventure</name>
    <desc>Organized ride: moderate difficulty, 45 miles</desc>
    <author>
      <name>Trail Boss Mike</name>
      <email>mike@example.com</email>
    </author>
    <keywords>desert,loop,moderate,group-ride</keywords>
    <extensions>
      <grouptrack:ride_id>ride_uuid</grouptrack:ride_id>
      <grouptrack:difficulty>moderate</grouptrack:difficulty>
      <grouptrack:vehicle_type>4x4 recommended</grouptrack:vehicle_type>
    </extensions>
  </metadata>
</gpx>

11. Technical Specifications
11.1 Database Schema Updates
Map enhancements utilize existing database fields that require API integration for full functionality.
Rides Table Map Fields
-- Existing fields to be utilized by enhanced APIs
map_bounds_north      DECIMAL(10,7),    -- North boundary latitude
map_bounds_south      DECIMAL(10,7),    -- South boundary latitude  
map_bounds_east       DECIMAL(10,7),    -- East boundary longitude
map_bounds_west       DECIMAL(10,7),    -- West boundary longitude
map_tile_source       VARCHAR(10),      -- SAT, TOPO, HYB, STREET
map_zoom_max          INT,              -- Maximum zoom level
trailhead_waypoint_id CHAR(36),         -- FK to waypoints table
route_id              CHAR(36),         -- FK to routes table
New Tables Required
-- Routes table for V2.5 integration
CREATE TABLE routes (
    route_id CHAR(36) PRIMARY KEY,
    name VARCHAR(255) NOT NULL,
    description TEXT,
    distance_miles DECIMAL(8,2),
    elevation_gain INT,
    difficulty ENUM('easy','moderate','difficult','expert'),
    route_type ENUM('loop','out-and-back','point-to-point','figure-8'),
    gpx_data LONGTEXT,
    created_by CHAR(36),
    created_at DATETIME DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP,
    is_public BOOLEAN DEFAULT TRUE
);

-- Waypoints table for trailhead management
CREATE TABLE waypoints (
    waypoint_id CHAR(36) PRIMARY KEY,
    name VARCHAR(255) NOT NULL,
    latitude DECIMAL(10,7) NOT NULL,
    longitude DECIMAL(10,7) NOT NULL,
    type ENUM('trailhead','staging','hazard','poi') DEFAULT 'poi',
    description TEXT,
    created_by CHAR(36),
    created_at DATETIME DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP,
    deleted_at DATETIME NULL
);
11.2 API Enhancements
Map functionality requires expanded API endpoints to support the complete feature set.
Enhanced Ride Creation
// POST /rides - Enhanced with map fields
{
    "ride_name": "Desert Loop Adventure",
    "description": "Moderate difficulty desert exploration",
    "ride_date": "2026-05-01",
    "start_time": "09:00",
    "trailhead_waypoint_id": "waypoint_uuid",
    "route_id": "route_uuid",
    "map_bounds_north": 34.0622,
    "map_bounds_south": 34.0422, 
    "map_bounds_east": -118.2337,
    "map_bounds_west": -118.2537,
    "map_tile_source": "SAT",
    "map_zoom_max": 18,
    "is_public": true
}
Route Management APIs
// Route management endpoints
POST   /routes              // Create route from planning
GET    /routes?user_id={}    // User's route library
PUT    /routes/{id}          // Update route metadata
DELETE /routes/{id}          // Remove route (soft delete)

// Waypoint management endpoints

GET    /waypoints?type={}    // Filter by type
PUT    /waypoints/{id}       // Update waypoint
DELETE /waypoints/{id}       // Remove waypoint

=== GroupTrack_V3_Online_Offline_Manual_v2.docx ===
GroupTrack
V3.0 Online / Offline Manual
Features, Functions, and Intent
v2  |  April 18, 2026  |  CONFIDENTIAL
Revisions from v1
First-time setup is three steps — Sign In, accept combined Terms & Policy, Dashboard — not four
Terms & Policy is a single existing document covering rider terms, organizer terms, and privacy policy
Subscription mechanics simplified: hardcoded subscribed for all signed-in users during initial V3.0
'Coming Soon' is reserved for mapping functions that are moving to V2.5 — all other incomplete features show placeholders describing intent
iPad implementation noted as pending Monday planning decision — V2.5 or V3.0
1. What GroupTrack Is
GroupTrack is an off-grid awareness platform for any group activity in areas without cell service. It is designed for off-road vehicles, hikers, mountain bikers, overlanders, search and rescue teams, and hunting parties — anyone who needs to coordinate with others in places where phones stop working.

The platform combines three capabilities into a single Android app:
Real-time group position awareness over LoRa mesh radio — no cell tower required
Organized ride management — create, invite, enroll, broadcast — all before you lose signal
Offline maps and trail overlays — downloaded in advance, available everywhere

1.1 The Core Idea — Online Before, Offline During
GroupTrack splits its functionality along one essential boundary: what happens online before a ride, and what happens offline during a ride.
Before the ride, while you still have connectivity, the cloud handles coordination work — creating rides, sending invites, downloading maps, applying radio configurations. Everything gets prepared, downloaded, and cached to the device.
During the ride, when you are out of cell range, everything runs locally. The mesh radio handles position updates between riders. The map tiles are already on the device. The ride configuration is already on the radio. No network calls happen while you are actually riding.

Key principle: the radio never needs the cloud. Authentication failures, network outages, or server problems never interrupt an active ride. Once you have prepared online, the ride works offline — always.
1.2 Platform Support
V3.0 ships for Android. iPad support is under active planning for a future release (V2.5 or V3.0 — to be confirmed). Web-based access and a native iOS port are on the roadmap for later releases.
2. Understanding Feature States in V3.0
As you explore V3.0 you will encounter screens in one of three states. Understanding these helps set expectations for what each screen can do right now.


Why the split? V2.5 is delivering a standalone trail product that addresses mapping functionality in depth — including map source stability, Utah OHV overlays, and a new Planning Map architecture. V3.0 focuses on cloud-based ride coordination. Each release has a clear purpose, and users get a stable foundation at each step.
3. Online vs. Offline Services
GroupTrack presents two service categories. Which one you interact with depends on whether you have connectivity and what you are trying to accomplish.


3.1 How the App Decides Which Mode You Are In
Every app launch, GroupTrack checks three things:
Do I have cached sign-in credentials? (Am I a returning user?)
Is there network connectivity right now?
Are my accepted Terms & Policy version current?
Based on the answers, the app routes you to the right starting screen — the Dashboard if everything is current and you are online, the Convoy Map if you are offline, or sign-in if you are new.
4. Getting Started
4.1 First Install
When you install GroupTrack for the first time, the app launches into a sign-in flow. You need to be connected to Wi-Fi or cell data the first time — account creation requires network access. After first-time setup, the app works offline.

First-time setup is three steps:
Sign In with Google — tap the Google sign-in button, choose your account
Accept the Terms & Policy — a single combined document covering rider terms, organizer terms, and privacy policy
Dashboard — you have landed on the main online services screen

Not ready to sign in? The Skip for now link on the sign-in screen takes you directly to the Convoy Map. You can use offline features (tile download, radio config, import GPX/KML) without an account. Sign in any time later from the menu.

4.2 Terms & Policy
GroupTrack uses a single combined Terms & Policy document that covers:
Rider Terms — how riders use the app and participate in rides
Organizer Terms — additional terms that apply when you become an organizer and create rides
Privacy Policy — what information is collected and how it is shared (notably: your name and email are shared with organizers of rides you follow or enroll in)
The document is shown in full during sign-up. If the document is updated, you are prompted to re-accept the new version on your next app launch.
4.3 What Sign-In Unlocks
Signing in enables all online features. After your first successful sign-in, you can use online features any time you have network access. Offline features are always available whether you sign in or not.


4.4 Subscription Status
GroupTrack operates on a freemium model. Most features are free forever. Organizer features and online ride management are part of the $3.00/month subscription tier.

For the initial V3.0 release, all signed-in users receive full subscription access at no cost. A go-live date will be announced when subscription billing activates. Users active during this period will be notified in advance and will retain access to their ride history and downloaded data regardless of subscription status.
5. Online Features
Online features require network connectivity and a signed-in account. They are organized around the Dashboard, which is the landing screen after sign-in.
5.1 Dashboard
Dashboard is the landing screen for all online services. It is a live data screen, not a button launcher. Three sections stack vertically from top to bottom:
Notifications — system announcements, ride updates, broadcast messages from organizers you follow
Enrollments — rides you are enrolled in, upcoming first, then active, then recently completed
Invites — pending invitations awaiting your accept or decline
Each entry is tappable for more detail. Invites have inline ACCEPT and DECLINE buttons. Enrollments have a VIEW DETAILS button that takes you to the full ride record.
The bottom navigation bar on the Dashboard provides access to the other main online sections: Rides, Explore, Tracks, Profile, Field Radio.
5.2 My Rides
The My Rides screen organizes your rides into tabs:
ALL — every ride you have any relationship to
ENROLLED — rides you have enrolled in as a rider
ORGANIZED — rides you have created as an organizer
COMPLETED — rides that have ended
Tap any ride to see its detail screen — trailhead, planned route, enrollment list, organizer contact. Organizers can also send invites and broadcast messages from this screen.
5.3 Create Ride (Organizer)
Any signed-in user can become an organizer and create rides. The organizer upgrade is available from the Profile screen and is covered by the same combined Terms & Policy document — no separate acceptance flow is needed beyond confirming the upgrade.

Creating a ride requires:
Ride name
Date
Trailhead — selected by tapping the map or picking from your waypoint library
Map area — bounding box for tile download, drawn on the map or defined by an existing route
Description (optional)
Public or private visibility
The SAVE RIDE button stays disabled until both trailhead and map area are set. These gates are highlighted in orange so you know what is missing.
When the ride is saved, GroupTrack automatically generates a unique radio channel name (CONVOY-XXXX) and a random channel PSK. These travel with the ride to every rider who enrolls, so everyone ends up with matching radio configuration.
5.4 Send Invite and Broadcast
From a ride's detail screen, organizers have two ways to invite riders.
Send Invite — Targeted
Send Invite generates a single-use invite link. Share it via:
QR code — display on screen, another person scans with their phone
Copy Link — copies the invite URL to the clipboard
Share Sheet — uses the Android system share sheet for text, email, or messaging apps
Invite link format: grouptrack.org/invite/{token}
Broadcast — Followers
Broadcast sends the ride notification to everyone who follows you as an organizer. Broadcasts are one-time per ride — once sent, the ride is broadcast and cannot be rebroadcast to the same follower list.
5.5 Accept Invite
Tapping an invite link (or scanning the QR code) opens the Invite Accept screen. You see the ride name, date, organizer, a download warning, and a summary of what accepting means.
Accepting does four things:
Downloads the ride JSON to local storage
Starts automatic map tile download for the ride's map area
Sets a flag marking the radio ready to accept the ride's channel configuration
Creates an enrollment record tying you to the ride
You are also offered the option to add the ride to your calendar.
5.6 Explore Rides
Explore shows public rides that organizers have chosen to list publicly. Search by zip + radius, map area, or organizer name. Each public ride shows a small map vignette with the staging area marked, the date, the organizer's contact info, and a FOLLOW option.
5.7 Profile
The Profile screen shows your account information and related entries:
Your name, email, and subscription status
Callsign, vehicle type, zip code (editable)
Email opt-in toggle for broadcasts
Become Organizer upgrade
Ride History link
Following link
Sign Out button
5.8 Ride History
Ride History shows every ride you have participated in, most recent first. For each ride you see:
Name, date, organizer
Organizer's email
Full rider list with email addresses
EXPORT GPX button to save the ride's track to Downloads
Entries can be searched by name, date, or riding area.
5.9 Following
Following is your list of organizers whose rides and broadcasts you want to see. For each organizer you can see their name, email, and optionally phone. Unfollow any time.
5.10 Track Library
Track Library is a public repository of ride tracks donated by participants of organized rides. Filter and search by difficulty, riding area, or recommendation percentage. Tap any track for its detail — map preview, difficulty, recommend %, donor name, and a DOWNLOAD TO DEVICE button. Downloading a track also auto-downloads the map tiles for its area.
6. Offline Features
Offline features work anywhere, with or without network connectivity, and regardless of sign-in status. These are the core operational capabilities — what the app does during a ride.
6.1 Convoy Map
The Convoy Map is the main operational screen. It runs as a background process whenever the app is open. The Convoy Map shows three behaviors based on what is happening, called RideStates:


Lead Lock
In CONVOY and ORGANIZED states, the Convoy Map automatically tracks the lead cart. A Lead Lock slider in settings configures the distance threshold (300 to 1500 feet, default 330 feet) at which a rider is considered the leader.
RECORD and STOP
RECORD starts GPS track recording. STOP ends the recording and prompts for a track name. In ORGANIZED state, STOP also offers a survey (difficulty, recommendation, track donation) before saving.
Map Settings
The Map Settings panel offers: Offline/Online tile toggle, Map type selector (Satellite, Hybrid, Topo), Track Lead Only, Show Downloaded Areas, Track format (GPX/KML), Tile Download Region entry, Zoom range (Z14 to Z18), Overlay Selector entry.
6.2 Map Manager — Coming Soon in V2.5
Map Manager is the comprehensive planning map toolset — tile download regions, waypoint creation, route planning, overlay management, Utah OHV trail data. In V3.0, the Map Manager entry point shows a Coming Soon notice because this functionality is being delivered in V2.5 via a dedicated Planning Map architecture.

Why is Map Manager delivered in V2.5 instead of V3.0? V2.5 is a standalone trail product release focused on map foundation work — multiple map sources, Utah OHV GeoJSON overlays, a new Planning Map separate from the operational Convoy Map. This structure avoids touching Convoy Map's hard-won stability while building out planning features in depth.

Map functions arriving in V2.5's Planning Map:
Find Area — viewport, geocoder, name search, waypoint radius search
Tile Download — draw a region, pick zoom levels, download tiles
Import GPX/KML — bring in tracks and routes from files
Overlay Selector — pick routes, tracks, waypoints to display
Waypoint Library — manage trailheads, staging areas, hazards, POIs
Route Planner — draw routes by tapping waypoints
Utah OHV overlay — motorized vehicle trail data (ships in V2.4 and V2.5)

V3.0 integration points for organized rides (Set Trailhead picker, Set Map Area picker) reference the V2.5 Planning Map once available.
6.3 Field Radio
The Field Radio screen is the interface to the connected LoRa mesh radio. Always accessible, online or offline, signed in or not. The screen shows:
BLE connection status
Active channel name and frequency
Spreading factor and transmit power
Current callsign
Main actions:
Apply Ride Config — push the current ride's channel and PSK to the radio
Apply Master Config — push the master GroupTrack radio configuration
Verify Config — read back the radio's settings and compare to expected
Change Long Name to LEAD — set callsign to LEAD for the lead rider
Bluetooth Toggle Reconnect — cycle BLE to recover from disconnects

Applying ride config is what gates participation in an organized ride. If your radio does not have the correct channel and PSK, you are invisible to the organized mesh. Apply ride config before leaving the trailhead.
6.4 Archive and Restore
The Archive and Restore screen manages saved radio configurations. Every time you apply a new configuration, the previous one is automatically archived. You can restore any archive to quickly revert. Archives are listed by date and time with a human-readable summary.
6.5 Import GPX/KML
Import GPX/KML lets you bring tracks and routes from outside sources. Flow:
Tap Import GPX/KML
Android file picker opens
Choose Route or Track
Give it a name
MD5 duplicate check runs
File saved to local library; appears in Overlay Selector
6.6 Your Tracks

=== GroupTrack_V3_Registration_Paywall_Spec_v2.docx ===
GroupTrack
V3.0 Registration & Stubbed Paywall Specification
v2  |  April 18, 2026  |  Walkaway Gate Document  |  CONFIDENTIAL
Revisions from v1
isSubscribed() is now hardcoded to return true with inline activation instructions — no PAYWALL_ENABLED flag
Rider Terms, Organizer Terms, and Privacy Policy are a single combined document that already exists — collapsed former s02 and s03 into one Terms & Policy screen
Placeholder screens for non-working features describe intent; 'Coming Soon' is reserved for mapping functions that are moving to V2.5
iPad implementation scope (V2.5 vs V3.0) flagged for Monday's planning discussion — not resolved in this document
1. Purpose and Scope
This document specifies the complete V3.0 registration flow and the stubbed paywall design. It is the gate document for V3.0 walkaway: until registration is built, stubbed-function definitions cannot be meaningfully reviewed because most V3 stubs depend on an authenticated user_id.

Registration is the entry point for all V3.0 online functionality. Every downstream screen (Dashboard, My Rides, Create Ride, Invite, Explore, Profile, Track Library) depends on a valid user_id cached locally from the registration flow.

Scope:
Registration flow — s01 Sign In through s04 Dashboard, including the combined Terms & Policy screen
Session persistence — SharedPreferences cache fields, offline behavior, re-acceptance triggers
Paywall as stub — s01b built and navigable; isSubscribed hardcoded true at walkaway
Online/Offline auth boundary
Go-Live activation steps — the minimum set of code changes to flip subscription from stub to live
Build inventory — what exists, what needs completion, per file
2. Stub and Placeholder UX Pattern
V3.0 walkaway produces a fully navigable app. Every screen in the V3 user flow opens and does something sensible. Three states exist for any given screen:


No non-mapping screen in V3.0 shows a 'Coming Soon' banner. Either the screen works, or it clearly describes its intent as a placeholder. This ensures V3.0 is a complete-feeling product at walkaway even though Phase C wiring continues toward go-live.
3. Registration Flow Overview
Registration runs top-to-bottom exactly once per user on the installed device. After successful completion, subsequent app launches use cached credentials and route directly to the Dashboard (s04) or the Convoy Map depending on online state.

3.1 Top-Level Launch Routing
App launch
   |
   v
ConvoySessionManager.resolveLaunchRoute(context)
   |
   |-- No cached user_id          --> s01 Sign In
   |-- Cached, terms current      --> s04 Dashboard
   |-- Cached, terms outdated     --> s02 Terms & Policy (re-accept)
   |-- Cached, offline            --> Convoy Map (never blocks radio)

3.2 First-Time User Path
Four screens, executed in order:

3.3 Returning User Path
4. Screen-by-Screen Specification
4.1  s01 — Sign In
File: ConvoySignInScreen.kt (EXISTS, partial)
Purpose
First-time authentication entry point. Google One Tap acquires a Google ID token, exchanged for a GroupTrack user_id via POST /users/register.
UI Elements
GroupTrack logo and tagline
Sign In with Google button (Google-branded)
Skip for now link — offline-only use (radio-only riders)
Brief copy explaining what sign-in unlocks
Behavior
User taps Sign In with Google
Google One Tap displays account selector
User selects account, grants profile scope
SDK returns ID token, email, first/last name
App calls ConvoyApiClient.registerUser()
On success, user_id cached via ConvoySessionManager.saveUser()
Route directly to s02 Terms & Policy (paywall bypassed by hardcoded isSubscribed=true)
Error Handling
Google Sign-In failure: error banner, offer retry or Skip for now
Network failure during registration: cache Google creds, mark registration_pending=true, route to Convoy Map, retry on connectivity

4.3  s02 — Terms & Policy (Combined)
File: ConvoyTermsScreen.kt (NEEDS BUILD)

Purpose
Display and capture acceptance of the single combined Terms and Policy document covering rider terms, organizer terms, and privacy policy. Version tracking triggers re-acceptance when the document is updated.

Rider Terms, Organizer Terms, and Privacy Policy are a single document that already exists. There is no separate Privacy screen, no separate Organizer Terms screen at signup. s06 Become Organizer references this same document for organizer-specific acceptance at upgrade time.

UI Elements
Document title and version (e.g., 'GroupTrack Terms & Policy v1.0')
Scrollable document body (loaded from asset file or GET /terms)
Section anchors for quick navigation: Rider Terms, Organizer Terms, Privacy
I ACCEPT checkbox
CONTINUE button (disabled until checkbox checked)
Decline and Exit link
Behavior
Load current terms version from GET /terms (fall back to bundled asset if offline)
Display document with version header
User reviews, checks I ACCEPT, taps CONTINUE
Cache accepted version: SharedPreferences key terms_accepted = version string
POST /users/accept-terms with user_id, version, timestamp
Route to s04 Dashboard
Version Mismatch Re-Acceptance
On every launch, compare cached terms_accepted against the current published version. Mismatch triggers routing to s02 on next sign-in regardless of other state. Cached version updated only on fresh acceptance.

Walkaway Responsibility
Dashboard renders from live API when online, from last-cached data when offline. At walkaway, inline accept/decline and ride-detail expand are intent-described placeholders where the API call is stubbed — the UI responds and acknowledges, the server write is queued or logged until go-live completes the backend.
5. Session Management and SharedPreferences
File: ConvoySessionManager.kt (EXISTS — partial)
SharedPreferences name: grouptrack_session

5.1 Cache Fields

5.2 Hardcoded isSubscribed — Walkaway Implementation
The isSubscribed() function is the single point of control for subscription gating. At walkaway it is hardcoded to return true. Activation instructions are embedded as comments inside the function so there is no external config to find, no flag to remember, no ambiguity.

// ConvoySessionManager.kt

/**
 * Returns true if the user has an active subscription.
 *
 * WALKAWAY STATE: Hardcoded to return true for all signed-in users.
 * This allows full V3.0 feature access without subscription billing.
 *
 * TO ACTIVATE SUBSCRIPTION BILLING AT GO-LIVE:
 *   1. Comment out the hardcoded 'return true' below
 *   2. Uncomment the expires_at check block
 *   3. Ensure users.expires_at is being updated by the receipt
 *      verification endpoint (convoy_api.php POST /billing/verify)
 *   4. Ensure Google Play Billing SDK is wired (see ConvoyBillingClient.kt)
 *   5. Update /users/register to stop setting expires_at = 2099-12-31
 *
 * No other code changes are required anywhere else in the app.
 * Every subscription gate calls isSubscribed() — change this, change all.
 */
fun isSubscribed(ctx: Context): Boolean {
    // WALKAWAY: return true for all signed-in users
    if (!isSignedIn(ctx)) return false
    return true

    // GO-LIVE: uncomment the block below, remove 'return true' above
    //
    // val expires = ctx.getSharedPreferences(PREFS, 0)
    //     .getLong(KEY_EXPIRES_AT, 0L)
    // return expires > System.currentTimeMillis()
}

5.3 Key API Surface on ConvoySessionManager
fun isSignedIn(ctx: Context): Boolean
fun isSubscribed(ctx: Context): Boolean  // Hardcoded true at walkaway (see 5.2)
fun saveUser(ctx, userId, googleId, email, firstName, lastName)
fun getUserId(ctx: Context): String?
fun clearSession(ctx: Context)  // Sign out
fun resolveLaunchRoute(ctx, onlineAvailable: Boolean): LaunchRoute
fun acceptTerms(ctx: Context, version: String)
fun needsTermsReaccept(ctx: Context, currentVersion: String): Boolean

5.4 LaunchRoute Enum
enum class LaunchRoute {
    SIGN_IN,        // No cached user_id
    SUBSCRIPTION,   // Post go-live only; never reached at walkaway
    TERMS,          // Cached user_id, terms version mismatch
    DASHBOARD,      // All checks pass
    CONVOY_MAP      // Offline or Skip-for-now
}
6. API Endpoints — Registration Flow
File: ConvoyApiClient.kt (EXISTS)
Base URL: https://34.224.89.217/  (HTTPS via Let's Encrypt)


6.1 Request/Response Contracts
POST /users/register
Request:  { google_id, email, first_name, last_name }
Response: { user_id: UUID, expires_at: datetime, is_new: bool }
At walkaway: PHP sets expires_at = 2099-12-31 for all registrations. At go-live: expires_at is set by receipt verification flow.
7. Online / Offline Behavior
KEY PRINCIPLE: Authentication state must never block radio operation. Mesh map, HUD, GPS tracking, radio config always work using locally cached data.


7.1 Pending Registration Recovery
If Google succeeds but POST /users/register fails, user is NOT forced back through Google sign-in on next attempt:
Google creds (id, email, names) cached with registration_pending=true
User routed to Convoy Map with banner: 'Sign-in pending — online features available once connected'
On every launch and connectivity change, background task retries using cached Google creds
On success, user_id cached, registration_pending cleared, s02 Terms flow triggered on next app resume
8. Paywall Stub Design Summary
8.1 Stub Mechanism
s01b paywall screen is FULLY BUILT and navigable from dev menu
isSubscribed() hardcoded to return true (see section 5.2)
resolveLaunchRoute() never routes to SUBSCRIPTION at walkaway because isSubscribed is always true
All subscription-gated screens (Dashboard, Rides, Invite, Explore, etc.) behave as if user is subscribed
users.expires_at in RDS set to 2099-12-31 for walkaway-era registrations (backup belt-and-suspenders)
Dashboard profile shows 'SUBSCRIBED' status badge

8.2 What Makes This a Safe Stub
Single point of control — only isSubscribed() changes at go-live. No scattered flags to find.
Self-documenting — activation instructions live inside the function body, not in a separate config doc.
Screen stays testable — dev menu force-route keeps s01b exercised through walkaway testing.
Reversible — hardcoded true can be re-enabled instantly if go-live encounters issues.

GroupTrack
V3.0 Stubbed Processes Inventory
Final Version - Complete Implementation Roadmap
April 19, 2026  |  FINAL v2  |  CONFIDENTIAL
1. Document Purpose & Scope
This inventory catalogs all 29 atomic stubbed processes in GroupTrack V3.0, derived from comprehensive where_used analysis, real database schema inspection, and existing PHP API evaluation. Each process has explicit implementation requirements to eliminate rediscovery in future development cycles.
1.1 Process Identification Methodology
where_used analysis: traced function calls to identify stub patterns and integration points
Database schema inspection: documented all 66+ fields across 5 tables
PHP API gap analysis: identified 80% of database capabilities unused by current API
Android integration mapping: connected UI screens to backend requirements
1.2 Key Findings
Database capability mismatch: Sophisticated 66-field schema vs basic 20-field API usage
Advanced features already in database: map bounds, location services, terms tracking, status workflows
Implementation strategy: expand existing PHP patterns rather than rebuild from scratch
Implementation Reality: Database supports advanced features that current PHP API doesn't expose. V3.0 completion = API expansion, not database redesign.
2. Core API Backend Processes
Primary backend integration processes derived from ConvoyApiClient method signatures and real database schema requirements.
3. Dashboard Data Feed Processes
Real-time data endpoints for ConvoyDashboardScreen.kt. UI components expect these feeds but currently display placeholder data.
4. V2.5 Map Integration Processes
Advanced mapping processes for V2.5 Planning Map integration. Database already supports map_bounds, trailhead_waypoint_id, route_id fields.
5. User Management & Session Processes
User account and session management. Database has 23 user fields but PHP API only uses 6 basic fields.
6. Content & Community Processes
Community content sharing and discovery features. Requires new database tables for track library and rating system.
7. GPS & Location Services
Location tracking and GPS services integration. Connects V2.4 convoy tracking with V3.0 community features.
8. Communication & Notification Systems
User communication, notifications, and messaging infrastructure for platform engagement.
9. Implementation Strategy & Priorities
Ordered implementation sequence based on user impact, technical dependencies, and platform value delivery.
9.1 Critical Path (V3.0 Launch Blockers)
SP01-SP06: Core API backend processes - expand existing PHP to use full database schema
SP07-SP09: Dashboard data feeds - implement missing GET endpoints for live data
SP17: Subscription validation - implement expires_at timestamp checking
9.2 High Value (Major Feature Enablers)
SP10-SP14: V2.5 Map Manager integration - connect advanced mapping to V3.0 services
SP15-SP16: User profile completion - use all 23 database fields for rich profiles
SP26-SP28: Communication systems - notifications, email, real-time updates
9.3 Community Features (Network Effects)
SP19-SP22: Content sharing system - track library and community ratings
SP23-SP25: Location services - proximity discovery and local recommendations
SP29: In-app messaging - enhanced coordination tools
9.4 Technical Dependencies
Database expansion: SP19-SP22 require new tables (ride_tracks, track_ratings)
V2.5 integration: SP10-SP14 depend on Map Manager completion
Infrastructure services: SP26-SP28 require external service integration (Firebase, AWS SES)
10. Success Metrics & Validation
Measurable criteria for determining successful implementation and platform readiness.
10.1 Implementation Completion
All 29 processes have status WORKING (no PLACEHOLDER or MISSING)
PHP API uses >80% of available database fields (currently ~20%)
End-to-end user workflows completable without mock data
V2.5 Map Manager fully integrated with V3.0 services
10.2 Platform Capability
Location-based organizer discovery functional
Community content sharing operational
Real-time notifications and updates working
Premium feature gates functional but disabled
10.3 Deployment Readiness
Value-first service delivery complete (all features free)
Billing infrastructure ready but disabled
Database performance optimized with proper indexing
Monitoring and error tracking operational
Implementation Success = 29 WORKING processes + full database utilization + V2.5 integration + value-first service delivery

=== GroupTrack_V3_Walkaway_Activity_v1.docx ===
GroupTrack
V3.0 Walkaway Activity Document
Current State Summary + Completion Plan
April 19, 2026  |  v1  |  CONFIDENTIAL
1. Executive Summary
This document defines the V3.0 walkaway state achieved on April 19, 2026, and establishes the completion plan for delivering a comprehensive, value-driven user experience. V3.0 will launch as a complete platform with all services available, focusing on solving real user needs and creating demand before any monetization implementation.
1.1 Walkaway Definition
V3.0 walkaway delivers a complete user experience where every service is functional, every workflow solves real problems, and users can accomplish their off-grid coordination goals without limitation. All features work as designed, from basic convoy coordination to advanced ride management and organization services. Users experience the full platform value before any payment consideration.
1.2 Strategic Approach — Value Before Monetization
V3.0 follows a strategic value-first launch approach designed to establish user dependency and demonstrate clear value before implementing any payment requirements. This strategy prioritizes user acquisition, engagement, and satisfaction over immediate revenue, building a strong foundation for sustainable long-term monetization.

Complete service delivery creates user dependency on advanced functionality
Real-world problem solving establishes clear value proposition
User base growth and engagement data inform optimal monetization timing
60-day grace period ensures smooth transition when paywall activates
1.3 Achievement Summary
As of April 19, 2026, GroupTrack V3.0 has reached walkaway state with validated infrastructure supporting complete service delivery. All core functionality is working, advanced features from V2.5 mapping development are ready for integration, and the completion path to full-service launch is clearly defined.
Strategic Launch: V3.0 delivers complete value first, establishes user dependency, then monetizes when timing is optimal for sustainable growth.
2. Current State Mapping
Comprehensive assessment of V3.0 components categorized by implementation status. Based on validation performed April 19, 2026, including live device testing, backend verification, and infrastructure validation.
2.1 Infrastructure — VALIDATED
2.2 Registration Flow — WORKING
2.3 Core Screens — NAVIGABLE
2.4 Backend Integration — VALIDATED
3. April 19 Validation Results
Comprehensive validation performed across infrastructure, application functionality, and integration points. All validation conducted on clean Android 2 device with factory reset and fresh V3.0 debug installation.
3.1 Infrastructure Validation
Backend API confirmed live: curl tests to grouptrack.org returned valid JSON responses with real user_id UUIDs
Google Cloud OAuth infrastructure provisioned end-to-end: project, consent screen, 2 Android clients, test user registered
API_BASE_URL bug identified and fixed: registration calls now reach correct PHP endpoints without 404 errors
Android development environment validated: clean 50GB storage, developer mode configured, ADB functional
3.2 Application Validation
V3.0 dev scenarios launch successfully: clear session and seed user data functions working
Dashboard navigation confirmed functional: organizer/rider differentiation working, bottom nav responsive
All V3 screens accessible and navigable: Profile, My Rides, Create Ride, Settings screens load without crashes
Terms and Privacy content displays properly: 6-section content in each screen, acceptance flow functional
3.3 Integration Validation
ConvoyApiClient methods reach backend successfully: no 404 errors with fixed API_BASE_URL configuration
Session management working: isSignedIn(), isSubscribed() checks functional, dev data persistence confirmed
Navigation routing logic validated: resolveLaunchRoute() correctly determines first screen based on session state
Build system fixed: APK naming now correctly reflects build content (V3.0 debug vs V2.4)
Validation Outcome: V3.0 infrastructure is production-ready. Core functionality confirmed working. Clear path to go-live identified.
4. Completion Requirements for Full Service Delivery
Specific technical requirements to deliver complete V3.0 functionality with all services operational. This approach prioritizes complete user value delivery over immediate monetization, ensuring users experience the full platform capability.
4.1 Package Migration — REQUIRED FOR OAUTH
OAuth integration cannot function until package migration is complete. This enables real Google Sign-In and unlocks the complete V3.0 user experience.

4.2 Complete Backend API Implementation
All backend services must be fully functional to deliver the complete V3.0 experience. Every feature should work as designed without limitation.

4.3 Billing Infrastructure — Built But Not Enforced
Payment infrastructure should be complete and tested, but not blocking any functionality. This allows future monetization activation without service interruption.

Google Play Billing integration: fully functional but returns success for all users
Subscription validation API: implemented with override returning active subscription
Premium feature identification: tagged in code but gates disabled
Grace period notification system: built and ready for future activation
4.4 V2.5 Map Manager Integration
Advanced mapping functionality developed during V2.5 must be fully integrated and operational in V3.0, providing users with comprehensive off-grid navigation tools.

Offline tile management and download capabilities
Route planning and waypoint management tools
GPX/KML import and export functionality
Overlay management for tracks, routes, and points of interest
5. Complete Service Delivery Timeline
Ordered sequence of development phases to deliver complete V3.0 functionality with all services operational. Timeline prioritizes user value delivery and platform adoption over monetization.
5.1 Phase 1 — Foundation & OAuth (V2.4 Final)
Execute package migration: applicationId change, google-services.json replacement, keystore alignment
Test Google Sign-In end-to-end: verify OAuth client match, successful user registration, session creation
Validate core infrastructure: confirm all API endpoints reachable with proper authentication
Deploy with billing infrastructure: payment systems ready but non-blocking
5.2 Phase 2 — Complete Backend Services
Implement full ride management: create, edit, delete rides with complete metadata and geographic boundaries
Build organization ecosystem: organizer profiles, follower management, broadcast communications
Deploy dashboard services: real-time notifications, ride status updates, community activity feeds
Activate content sharing: public track library, route discovery, community contributions
5.3 Phase 3 — V2.5 Map Integration
Integrate Map Manager functionality: offline tile management, route planning, waypoint systems
Deploy advanced mapping tools: GPX/KML import/export, overlay management, area download
Enable Planning Map architecture: independent from convoy map with full feature set
Test complete mapping workflow: plan offline, execute convoy, share tracks
5.4 Phase 4 — Production Launch
Publish OAuth consent screen: promote from Testing to Production for unlimited users
Deploy complete production backend: all endpoints active with full feature support
Launch comprehensive platform: every feature operational, complete user experience
Monitor usage and engagement: collect data for optimal monetization timing decision
5.5 Future — Strategic Monetization
Monetization activation occurs when platform value is proven and user dependency established. Timeline driven by strategic considerations rather than technical constraints.

Data-driven timing: user engagement metrics and retention analysis inform decision
60-day advance notice: users receive clear communication about upcoming changes
Granular feature gating: premium features clearly identified and communicated
Smooth transition: existing workflows preserved, enhanced features require subscription

1. Infrastructure — How the Website Works

2. How to Deploy Website Updates
Every website change follows this 3-step process from local Git Bash:

Step 1 — Upload new index.html from Downloads
scp -i ~/.ssh/convoy-api-key-2.pem ~/Downloads/index.html ec2-user@34.224.89.217:~/

Step 2 — SSH to EC2 and move to web root
ssh -i ~/.ssh/convoy-api-key-2.pem ec2-user@34.224.89.217
sudo mv ~/index.html /var/www/html/index.html

Step 3 — Verify
curl -s http://localhost/ | head -5
Should return the first 5 lines of the HTML. Then test in browser: http://34.224.89.217

Deploy PDF Documents
# From local Git Bash — create docs folder first time only
ssh -i ~/.ssh/convoy-api-key-2.pem ec2-user@34.224.89.217 "sudo mkdir -p /var/www/html/docs && sudo chmod 755 /var/www/html/docs"
# Upload PDFs
scp -i ~/.ssh/convoy-api-key-2.pem ~/Downloads/"GroupTrack_Installation_Guide_V2.4.pdf" ec2-user@34.224.89.217:~/
scp -i ~/.ssh/convoy-api-key-2.pem ~/Downloads/"GroupTrack_User_Guide_V2.4.pdf" ec2-user@34.224.89.217:~/
# SSH and move to docs folder
sudo mv ~/GroupTrack_Installation_Guide_V2.4.pdf /var/www/html/docs/
sudo mv ~/GroupTrack_User_Guide_V2.4.pdf /var/www/html/docs/
The website downloads section links to docs/GroupTrack_Installation_Guide_V2.4.pdf and docs/GroupTrack_User_Guide_V2.4.pdf — filenames must match exactly.

=== GroupTrack_WhereUsed_Reference_v1.docx ===
GroupTrack
Complete Field-Level Where-Used Reference
68 Files  |  396 Functions  |  Lifecycle-Tagged Call Chains
May 2026  |  MANDATORY REFERENCE BEFORE ANY CODE CHANGE  |  Confidential
Lifecycle Phase Legend
RULE: Any function tagged INIT must NOT require permissions, network, or launch activities. Any function tagged TICK must have permission guard if it accesses GPS/Location.

1. Meshtastic Base Layer Modifications
These are changes made to files OUTSIDE the convoy/ package. Stock Meshtastic does not have these. Every change here runs during app startup and affects ALL users.
1.1 MainActivity.kt
1.2 ui/Main.kt
1.3 navigation/ConvoyNavigation.kt
1.4 app/build.gradle.kts
1.5 AndroidManifest.xml

2. ConvoyViewModel.kt — Critical Path Analysis
47 functions. This is the heart of the convoy system. Every tick cycle, every GPS call, every state update flows through here.
2.1 INIT Phase — Runs During App Startup
WARNING: Everything in init{} runs on the MAIN THREAD during Activity.onCreate(). No permissions are granted yet. No UI is visible. Any blocking call here causes ANR.
ISSUE: scanImportDirectory() runs in init via viewModelScope.launch. It queries MediaStore which may require storage permission. On a fresh install this may fail silently or block.
2.2 TICK Phase — Runs Every 3–5 Seconds
WARNING: tick() runs from init{} before map loads. Permission guard added in efa1b677e.
2.3 Tick Call Chain — Full Trace

=== GroupTrack_day23_plan (1).docx ===
GroupTrack.org
Day 23 Plan
March 23, 2026  |  Branch: feature/convoy-event-ride  |  kixazz25/Meshtastic-Android

Priority 1 — Radio Write Persistence
WorkingConfig is wiped every time the user navigates to the Meshtastic connections screen. Two separate radio reset functions are the cause. This is a field-blocking issue — the organizer cannot pair a radio mid-session without losing all config work. Must be fixed before field deployment.

Priority 2 — GPS Broadcast Interval Slider
Smart position broadcast interval is fixed in master config. Riders in fast-moving convoys need more frequent updates; slower rides can conserve battery with longer intervals. Slider reads live from radio, writes back only if changed — no reboot required for position config changes.

Day 23 Primary Tasks

UI Checklist — Attack as Soon as Available
These items can be worked in any order. None depend on Amazon EC2.

=== GroupTrack_product_roadmap_v8 (5).docx ===
GroupTrack.org
Product Roadmap v7
Last Updated: March 22, 2026  |  Author: Fred Kix  |  Repo: kixazz25/Meshtastic-Android
Platform Overview
GroupTrack.org is a Meshtastic-based off-grid group ride coordination system built as a fork of the Meshtastic Android client. It provides real-time mesh networking for vehicle convoys, event ride organization, and field coordination without cellular dependency. Domain: GroupTrack.org.


Product Tiers and Pricing

V1 — Manual Setup Group Ride Tracker


V2 — Self-Service Setup + Backend Ride Registration

V2 Architecture

V2 Radio Config Engine — COMPLETE March 19, 2026


V2 Backend Ride Registration — IN PROGRESS March 22, 2026


V2 Pending Features

Backend Architecture — V2
Database Schema — AWS RDS MySQL

Ride Registration Flow

Environment Reference

GroupTrack.org  |  Product Roadmap v8  |  March 23, 2026
VERSION | PLATFORM | STATUS | DESCRIPTION
V1 | Android | COMPLETE | Manual Setup Group Ride Tracker
V2 | Android + iOS | IN PROGRESS | Self-Service Setup + Backend Ride Registration
V3 | Android + iOS | PLANNED | ATAK-Modeled Map Integration
V4 | Android + iOS | PLANNED | KML Track History Overlay
TIER | PRICE | DESCRIPTION
Personal Edition | Free | Ships with the default master radio configuration. Supports all core tracking, HUD, recording, and map functions. All organizer, rider, and group definitions stored and managed on the GroupTrack.org online database. Full offline operation once configured.
Unique Ride Definition | $3.00/mo | Upgrade tier for groups requiring custom ride definitions. Organizer defines the ride parameters and map region. Riders pull ride configuration to automatically customize their radio settings and download map tiles for the ride area. Includes full ride definition, invite distribution, and map pre-loading. Designed for recurring group rides with unique channel and configuration requirements.
STATUS: COMPLETE  |  APK: ConvoyTracker-V1-debug.apk

Manual radio configuration | User configures radio manually before use | COMPLETE
STATUS: IN PROGRESS  |  Branch: feature/convoy-event-ride  |  Last commit: 012fdbaad

Channel auto-generation | CONVOY-XXXX, AES-256 PSK via SecureRandom | COMPLETE
Master config in assets | master_config.json + master.cfg + convoy_apply_list.json | COMPLETE
MILESTONE: Binary cfg engine proven end-to-end. Ride config applied to factory-reset radio. 40 fields verified. Zero manual radio knowledge required.
FEATURE | DESCRIPTION | STATUS

Two-stage ReconnectWait | Stage 1: binary reboot. Stage 2: channel write reboot. Both gated. | COMPLETE
Archive capture | Radio state archived before every config change | COMPLETE
Archive Restore Screen | List archives by node, select, restore. Wired to convoy submenu. | COMPLETE
Master config capture | Reads 40 fields from radio including positionPrecision and channelIsMuted | COMPLETE
ARCHITECTURE PIVOT: Ride distribution moves from .convoy email attachment to AWS-hosted REST API. Rider receives invite link, taps it, enrolls online, downloads ride JSON. Radio engine unchanged.

Invite link flow | Organizer sends link. Rider taps. App calls API. Downloads ride JSON. | OPEN
Local ride storage | Downloaded ride JSON saved to local Room/SQLite DB | OPEN
Online/offline gate | Create ride, invite, enroll = online only. Everything else = offline. | OPEN

TABLE | FIELD | TYPE | DESCRIPTION
users | user_id | UUID PK | Unique user identifier
users | google_id | VARCHAR | Google Sign-In identifier
users | first_name | VARCHAR | First name
users | last_name | VARCHAR | Last name
users | cell | VARCHAR | Cell phone number
users | email | VARCHAR | Email from Google account
rides | ride_id | UUID PK | Unique ride identifier
rides | ride_name | VARCHAR | Human readable ride name
rides | ride_date | DATE | Scheduled ride date
rides | channel_name | VARCHAR | Meshtastic channel name (max 11 chars)
rides | channel_psk | VARCHAR | AES-256 32-byte key base64 encoded
rides | organizer_id | UUID FK | References users.user_id
rides | created_at | DATETIME | Creation timestamp
enrollments | enrollment_id | UUID PK | Unique enrollment identifier
enrollments | ride_id | UUID FK | References rides.ride_id
enrollments | user_id | UUID FK | References users.user_id
enrollments | role | ENUM | ORGANIZER or RIDER
enrollments | enrolled_at | DATETIME | Enrollment timestamp
invites | invite_id | UUID PK | Unique invite identifier
invites | ride_id | UUID FK | References rides.ride_id
invites | invited_by | UUID FK | References users.user_id — organizer only
invites | invite_token | VARCHAR | Unique random token — this IS the invite link
invites | claimed_by | UUID FK | Null until claimed by rider
invites | created_at | DATETIME | Invite creation timestamp
invites | claimed_at | DATETIME | Null until claimed
# | ACTOR | ACTION
1 | Organizer | Creates ride in app (online) — stored in MySQL
2 | Organizer | Sends invite link via any method (SMS, email, WhatsApp)
3 | Rider | Taps invite link — GroupTrack.org opens via deep link
4 | App | Calls GET /ride/{invite_token} — downloads ride JSON
5 | App | Saves ride JSON to local DB — marks invite claimed
6 | Rider | Goes offline — applies ride config to radio via existing engine
ITEM | VALUE
Branch | feature/convoy-event-ride
Last Commit | 012fdbaad — convoy file import, MediaStore scanner, ConvoyFileReceiver
Build | ./gradlew assembleGoogleDebug
Install | adb -s 8624SBCEDF00001789 install -r app/build/outputs/apk/google/debug/app-google-debug.apk

=== GroupTrack_user_manual_v2 (1).docx ===
GroupTrack.org
User Manual  —  Version 2
March 2026  |  Android  |  Off-Grid Group Ride Coordination


This manual covers all screens, navigation paths, and features of GroupTrack.org V2. V2 adds self-service radio configuration, ride creation and transfer, and a fully automated binary radio config engine. No manual radio knowledge is required.


Contents
1. Installation — new device setup
2. First Launch and Radio Pairing
3. Main Map Screen — layout, map controls, RECORD button
4. The Button Bar — GROUP | MY CART | HIDE | GEAR
5. GROUP HUD Panel — convoy status overlay
6. MY CART HUD Panel — your vehicle stats
7. NODE HUD Panel — individual cart detail (tap any cart on map)
8. The GroupTrack Menu — all menu items explained
9. Create a Ride — organizer workflow
10. Transfer Event/Ride Details via Email — send ride to riders
11. Apply Ride Info to Radio — configure radio for a ride
12. ReconnectWait Screen — two-stage radio write process
13. Verify Config Screen — confirm all 40 fields
14. Restore Prior Config Post Ride — archive restore
15. Developer Settings Panel — master config, apply list
16. Rider Workflow — receiving and applying a ride
17. Field Reference — radio config values, status indicators
18. Troubleshooting

1. Installation
What You Need

Enable Installation from Unknown Sources
Android blocks apps from outside the Play Store by default. Enable this once before installing:

Android 8.0 and later:
